**2. Trigger point:** no correspondence. Orbital contents (fat,
extraocular muscles) are not part of the trigger point mapping
system. ✗

**3. Ashi correspondence:** no correspondence. Patients with
ophthalmic conditions do not identify tenderness at the inferior
orbital margin. Retrobulbar pain is deep orbital ache, not surface
palpation tenderness. ✗

**4. Marma point:** no direct correspondence. Apanga Marma (अपांग)
is at the lateral corner of the eye (closer to GB1). Avarta Marma
(आवर्त) is above the eyebrow. Neither addresses the inferolateral
orbital margin specifically. ✗

**5. Head zone:** V2 infraorbital territory. The therapeutic mechanism
is LOCAL (direct retrobulbar access) rather than segmental
convergence. ✗

**6. Anatomy Trains:** no correspondence. Orbital fat and extraocular
muscles are not part of any myofascial meridian chain. ✗

**7. Neurogenic spot:** no data (Kim 2017 = trunk only). ✗

**8. Cupping point:** not applicable (periorbital). ✗

**Convergence score: 1/8** (TCM ✓ only). Qiuhou is a pure
TCM-specific point whose value derives from ANATOMICAL ACCESS
(retrobulbar space) rather than cross-tradition convergence. The
cross-tradition mapping system does not evaluate points that work
through direct deep-structure access rather than surface stimulation
of convergence zones.

#### Chỉ định truyền thống (DATA — ghi nhận, không endorse as mechanism)

Nguồn cổ:
- Extra point collections: Qiuhou for "all eye diseases, brightening
  vision." The name reflects its anatomical approach: accessing
  structures BEHIND the eyeball.

Nguồn hiện đại (Deadman, WHO):
- Optic atrophy (视神经萎缩)
- Optic neuritis
- Retinitis pigmentosa
- Glaucoma (adjunctive IOP reduction)
- Myopia (limited evidence)
- Strabismus
- Central retinal vessel occlusion

Kinh nghiệm lâm sàng nổi bật:
- **BL1+EX-HN7 pair:** Jingming + Qiuhou is the standard periorbital
  pair for ophthalmic acupuncture — BL1 from superomedial, EX-HN7
  from inferolateral. Used together in 5/9 RCTs in the optic atrophy
  MA (Zhi et al. 2019).
- **Optic atrophy protocol:** Qiuhou + BL1 + GB20 + periorbital
  points (BL2, ST1, TE23) + distal points (LI4, LR3, ST36). 28–60
  sessions in clinical studies.
- **Glaucoma IOP protocol:** BL1+EX-HN7 pair specifically studied
  for acute IOP reduction (Chen et al. 2020).
→ Cross-ref: AP-07 BL1 (periorbital pairing)

#### Bằng chứng hiện đại

**① Optic atrophy — 🟡 Plausible**

Systematic review and MA (Zhi et al. 2019, Evidence-Based
Complementary and Alternative Medicine, 9 RCTs, 513 participants/608
eyes): Qiuhou featured in 5/9 studies. Visual acuity MD 0.18
(p<0.00001), P-VEP100 latency improved (MD −6.80, p<0.0001), total
effectiveness OR 4.29 (95% CI 2.56–7.19). Critical limitation:
only 2/9 described randomization adequately; NO blinding; all
Chinese-language; authors' own conclusion: "it cannot be concluded
that manual acupuncture is more effective than medication alone" due
to methodological flaws.

**② Glaucoma IOP reduction — 🟡 Plausible**

Single-blinded RCT (Chen et al. 2020, n=45): BL1+EX-HN7
acupuncture and electroacupuncture both significantly reduced IOP at
60 min vs sham (p<0.05). 24-week RCT for normal-tension glaucoma:
improved BCVA, increased RNFL thickness, reduced IOP, slower visual
field deterioration. Small samples; short-term effects predominate.

**③ Retinitis pigmentosa — 🔴 Unvalidated**

Protocol published (Huang et al. 2021, BMJ Open, n=64,
sham-controlled) — results pending. Pilot case series (Bittner et al.
2014, n=12, uncontrolled): 5/11 showed scotopic sensitivity
improvements, some maintained 10–12 months. No sham control.

**④ Myopia — 🔴 Unvalidated**

Cochrane review (Wei et al. 2022): only 2 small RCTs (131 children),
neither using Qiuhou, neither showing efficacy. No conclusions
possible.

#### Dynamic acupoint properties

No disease-state sensitization data for the inferior orbital margin.
The point's therapeutic value is based on ANATOMICAL ACCESS
(retrobulbar approach) rather than surface sensitization. The orbital
region does not show the dynamic threshold changes seen at somatic
points during visceral pathology.
→ Cross-ref: C04 S4 (Dynamic Acupoint Phenomenon)

#### Cơ chế giải thích (BSA framework attribution)

**Direct retrobulbar access — anatomical mechanism:**

Unlike most acupoints that work through neural afferent stimulation →
central processing → efferent modulation, Qiuhou works primarily
through DIRECT ACCESS to orbital structures:

1. **Retrobulbar fat stimulation:** needle in orbital fat may activate
   local nociceptors → reflexive changes in orbital blood flow via
   trigeminal-autonomic reflex (V2 → pterygopalatine ganglion →
   ophthalmic artery vasodilation).

2. **Inferior oblique muscle stimulation:** mechanical stimulation may
   affect proprioceptive feedback → oculomotor nuclear complex in
   brainstem.

3. **IOP mechanism:** needle insertion into retrobulbar space may
   mechanically decompress orbital pressure or reflexively modulate
   aqueous humor dynamics through autonomic pathways. Acute IOP
   reduction within 60 min suggests vascular/hydraulic rather than
   structural mechanism.

Gap: specific mechanism for visual function improvement is unknown.
No molecular effector identified.
→ Cross-ref: A01 S1.4, AP-07 BL1 (orbital access mechanism)

#### Kỹ thuật kích thích + dose-response

Châm (B01) — SPECIALIZED TECHNIQUE:
1. Patient closes eyes and looks UPWARD (displaces globe superiorly,
   away from needle path)
2. Practitioner gently pushes eyeball upward with a finger
3. Insert needle perpendicular to orbital margin, then follow contour
   of inferior orbital wall posteriorly
4. Depth: 0.5–1.0 cun (13–25 mm). **MAXIMUM: 26 mm** (cadaver data)
5. **CRITICAL: NO lifting, NO thrusting, NO twisting, NO rotating.**
   These manipulations are FORMALLY CONTRAINDICATED — they
   dramatically increase globe perforation risk.
6. Insert SLOWLY only
7. Post-withdrawal: press firmly with sterile cotton for ≥1 minute

De Qi: sensation of distension or fullness behind the eye. May be
difficult to distinguish from discomfort — err on the side of
caution.

EA: used in some glaucoma protocols (Chen 2020) at BL1+EX-HN7 pair.
LOW intensity only.

Bấm huyệt (B02): not applicable (digital pressure does not access
retrobulbar space).

Cứu (B08): not applicable (periorbital).

Dry needling (B11): not applicable.

**Practitioner restriction:** ONLY by practitioners with specific
ophthalmic acupuncture training. Not appropriate for general
acupuncture practice.
→ Cross-ref: B01 S3.1

#### An toàn

⚠️ **DANGEROUS POINT — HIGH RISK OF SERIOUS COMPLICATIONS**

**Documented adverse events (Lee et al. 2023, Korean Journal of
Ophthalmology, 14 cases/15 eyes, 1982–2020):**
- Globe perforation (penetrating injury): **80% of serious AE cases**
  (12/15 eyes)
- Associated: vitreous hemorrhage, retinal tears/detachment,
  endophthalmitis, proliferative vitreoretinopathy, subretinal
  hemorrhage, traumatic optic neuropathy
- Traumatic cataract: 2 cases
- Retrobulbar hemorrhage → optic atrophy: 1 case
- **Visual outcomes: 20/20 recovery to permanent blindness**
- **Injuries occurred with licensed practitioners** (6/14 cases)

**Critical depth thresholds (cadaver study):**

| Depth | Structure at risk |
|---|---|
| ~13 mm | End of relatively safe zone |
| 26.5 ± 1.7 mm | **Posterior ciliary artery** |
| ~40 mm | **Optic nerve + ophthalmic artery** |
| ~41 mm | **Superior ophthalmic vein** |

**Absolute contraindications:**
- Anticoagulant therapy or bleeding disorders
- Globe injury or recent ocular surgery
- Orbital infection or inflammation
- Uncooperative patient (movement = perforation risk)
- Practitioners without ophthalmic acupuncture training

**Emergency protocol if retrobulbar hemorrhage:**
- Immediate needle removal
- Apply firm direct pressure to orbit
- Monitor for orbital compartment syndrome (proptosis, ↓visual acuity,
  ↑IOP, restricted eye movements)
- Urgent ophthalmology referral — may require lateral
  canthotomy/cantholysis
→ Cross-ref: AP-07 BL1 (L295-316, retrobulbar hemorrhage management)

**Comparison with other dangerous acupoints:**

| Point | Risk | Access |
|---|---|---|
| CV22 | Tracheal puncture | Suprasternal → trachea |
| GV15/GV16 | Brainstem/spinal cord | Cisterna magna |
| BL1 | Retrobulbar hemorrhage | Superomedial orbit |
| **EX-HN7** | **Globe perforation (80%)** | **Inferolateral orbit** |

---

### EX-HN8 — Thượng Nghênh Hương (上迎香, Shàngyíngxiāng)

**Evidence tier: B (Standard Entry)**

"Upper Yingxiang" (上 = upper, 迎香 = Welcome Fragrance = LI20). Also
known as Bitong (鼻通, "Nose Through"). At the junction of the nasal
bone and lateral nasal cartilage, ~0.5 cun superior and medial to
LI20 (Yingxiang). The MOST evidence-supported nasal extra point:
featured in ~26% of RCTs in a 39-trial Bayesian MA for allergic
rhinitis (Yin et al. 2020, n=3,433). Sits at the V1/V2 dermatome
boundary — the transition zone between ophthalmic (external nasal
nerve from nasociliary) and maxillary (infraorbital nerve) trigeminal
territories — potentially stimulating both divisions simultaneously.

#### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): on the face, at the junction of
the nasal bone and the nasal cartilage, near the upper end of the
nasolabial groove.

Anatomical landmarks: palpate along the lateral nose upward from LI20
(at nasal ala midpoint). The point is at the transition from bony
nasal skeleton to cartilaginous framework — palpable as a slight
step where rigid nasal bone gives way to flexible lateral nasal
cartilage. ~0.5 cun superior and medial to LI20.

Spatial relationship with LI20:
- LI20 (Yingxiang): nasolabial groove, at nasal ala midpoint level.
  V2 infraorbital territory.
- EX-HN8 (Shangyingxiang): upper nasolabial groove, bone-cartilage
  junction. V1/V2 boundary.
- Through-needling: classical technique threads subcutaneously from
  LI20 upward to EX-HN8, intensifying the nasal effect.

Needle depth: 0.3–0.5 cun oblique, directed upward at ~45° toward
nasal cavity center. Subcutaneous only — deep needling contraindicated
(nasal cavity entry risk).

#### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply — V1/V2 boundary zone:
- **External nasal nerve (from anterior ethmoidal nerve, from
  nasociliary nerve, from V1 ophthalmic division):** innervates the
  nasal dorsum, tip, and upper lateral nose. Emerges between nasal
  bone and lateral nasal cartilage — precisely at EX-HN8's location.
- **Infraorbital nerve (V2 maxillary division):** nasal branches
  supply lateral nose and nasal ala. V2 territory extends upward from
  LI20 and overlaps with V1 at the bone-cartilage junction.
- **Anterior ethmoidal nerve (V1 nasociliary, internal branch):**
  before emerging externally, the anterior ethmoidal nerve traverses
  the nasal cavity, supplying nasal mucosa (anterior-superior septum,
  lateral wall). Stimulating the external branch at EX-HN8 activates
  afferents whose central connections overlap with internal nasal
  mucosal afferents.

The V1/V2 dermatome boundary runs approximately through the
bone-cartilage junction. Stimulation at EX-HN8 may activate both:
- V1 pathway: nasociliary → anterior ethmoidal → nasal mucosal
  afferents → parasympathetic reflex via pterygopalatine ganglion
- V2 pathway: infraorbital → maxillary division → overlapping nasal
  territory

Spinal segments: none (cranial nerve V1/V2).

Vascular: angular artery (terminal branch of facial artery, ascending
along the nose) — relatively large at this location.

Fascia/muscle: nasalis muscle (compressor and dilator naris parts),
levator labii superioris alaeque nasi. Periosteum of nasal bone /
perichondrium of lateral nasal cartilage.
→ Cross-ref: A01 S1.4

#### Phân loại huyệt (Point Category Membership)

Extra point (经外奇穴): EX-HN8 in WHO nomenclature. Not on any
meridian.

Five Shu: not applicable. Yuan-Source: not applicable.
Luo-Connecting: not applicable. Xi-Cleft: not applicable.
Back-Shu: not applicable. Front-Mu: not applicable.
Eight Confluent: not applicable. Hui-Meeting: not applicable.

Special designations:
- **LI20 satellite point:** named relative to LI20 (Yingxiang),
  positioned ~0.5 cun superior. Together they form a nasal point
  pair for rhinitis/sinusitis protocols.
- **Dual name:** WHO designation EX-HN8 Shangyingxiang; alternative
  Bitong (鼻通, "Nose Through") reflects primary functional indication
  (nasal patency).
- **Most evidence-supported nasal extra point:** featured in the
  largest acupuncture MA for allergic rhinitis (39 RCTs).
→ Cross-ref: B01 S1.3.3

#### Cross-tradition mapping

**1. TCM acupoint:** EX-HN8 (Shangyingxiang 上迎香 / Bitong 鼻通).
Functions: opens nasal passages (通鼻窍), dispels Wind-Heat
(疏风清热). Indications: rhinitis (allergic and chronic), sinusitis,
nasal obstruction, anosmia, nasal polyps. Essentially a
single-indication family: ALL relate to nasal patency and mucosal
function. ✓

**2. Trigger point:** no well-established trigger point. The nasalis
and levator labii superioris alaeque nasi are small facial muscles
not systematically mapped in Travell & Simons. ✗

**3. Ashi correspondence:** patients with nasal congestion and sinus
pressure commonly identify tenderness and pressure sensation along
the lateral nose. Pressing beside the nose during congestion is a
semi-intuitive behavior. 🟡

**4. Marma point:** Phana Marma (फण, "Serpent's Hood") — located at
the sides of the nostrils. Injury consequence: anosmia (loss of
smell). Phana is typically described at or near the nasal ala/nostril,
slightly inferior to EX-HN8's bone-cartilage junction. Partial
correspondence — within the same functional nasal zone but not
exactly co-located. 🟡

**5. Head zone:** V1/V2 boundary. V1 anterior ethmoidal branches
directly supply the nasal mucosa — the target tissue for rhinitis
treatment. Stimulation at EX-HN8 may modulate nasal mucosal vasomotor
tone through V1 afferent → trigeminal sensory nuclei →
parasympathetic reflex via pterygopalatine ganglion. A genuine
sensory nerve → target organ connection. 🟡

**6. Anatomy Trains:** small nasal muscles are facial expression
muscles not integrated into major myofascial meridian chains. ✗

**7. Neurogenic spot:** no data (Kim 2017 = trunk only). ✗

**8. Cupping point:** not applicable (nose). ✗

**Convergence score: 4/8** (TCM ✓, Ashi 🟡, Marma 🟡, Head zone 🟡).
Moderate convergence for a nasal extra point. The Phana Marma
correspondence (shared nasal location + anosmia indication) and the
V1 anterior ethmoidal → nasal mucosa afferent connection provide the
strongest cross-tradition signals.

#### Chỉ định truyền thống (DATA — ghi nhận, không endorse as mechanism)

Nguồn cổ:
- Extra point collections: Shangyingxiang/Bitong for "opening nasal
  passages, clearing nasal obstruction." The dual name reflects
  emphasis on function: 鼻通 literally = "nose through" = restoring
  nasal patency.

Nguồn hiện đại (Deadman, WHO):
- Rhinitis (allergic and chronic)
- Sinusitis (maxillary, frontal)
- Nasal obstruction
- Anosmia/hyposmia
- Nasal polyps

Kinh nghiệm lâm sàng nổi bật:
- **Standard allergic rhinitis protocol:** LI20 + EX-HN8 (bilateral)
  + Yintang + LI4 + ST36 + BL13 + GV14. EX-HN8 is a CORE local
  point.
- **Through-needling technique:** threading subcutaneously from LI20
  upward to EX-HN8 stimulates the full nasal lateral nerve territory
  in a single needle pass. Classical technique documented in Chinese
  acupuncture textbooks.
- **Chronic rhinitis protocol:** EX-HN8 + GV23 (Shangxing) + BL7
  (Tongtian) + LI4.
→ Cross-ref: AP-02 LI20 (bidirectional link established — LI20 now
cross-refs EX-HN8 including through-needling technique and Yin 2020 MA)

#### Bằng chứng hiện đại

**① Allergic rhinitis — 🟡 Plausible**

Bayesian network MA (Yin et al. 2020, Chinese Medicine, 39 RCTs,
3,433 participants): all acupuncture types superior to sham for TNSS
(total nasal symptom score) and RQLQ (rhinoconjunctivitis quality of
life). Shangyingxiang featured in ~10/39 RCTs (~26%). Most frequent
acupoints: LI20 (~25 studies), Yintang (~22), LI4 (~18), GV14 (~16),
BL13 (~14), ST36 (~11), Shangyingxiang (~10), GB20 (~9).

Additional MA (He et al. 2022, European Journal of Medical Research,
30 RCTs, 4,413 participants): acupuncture superior to no intervention
and sham for nasal symptoms and quality of life, comparable to
antihistamines.

Acupoint catgut embedding MA (2023, American Journal of
Otolaryngology, 17 RCTs, 1,231 patients): LI20 and Shangyingxiang
among key acupoints. OR 5.19 (95% CI 3.14–8.58) favoring catgut
embedding.

**② Chronic rhinitis / sinusitis — 🔴 Unvalidated**

Traditional indication with anatomical rationale (V1 anterior
ethmoidal → nasal mucosa). No condition-specific controlled evidence
separable from allergic rhinitis data.

**③ Anosmia — 🔴 Unvalidated**

Traditional indication. V1 olfactory connections provide theoretical
basis. No modern evidence.

#### Dynamic acupoint properties

The nasolabial region may show increased tenderness during acute
rhinitis/sinusitis — nasal mucosal inflammation can produce referred
tenderness to the lateral nose through V1/V2 trigeminal convergence.
Nasal mucosal engorgement during allergic rhinitis increases tissue
turgor in the lateral nasal region, potentially making the point more
palpable and tender. No controlled sensitization studies.
→ Cross-ref: C04 S4 (Dynamic Acupoint Phenomenon)

#### Cơ chế giải thích (BSA framework attribution)

**Primary pathway: V1/V2 trigeminal → nasal mucosal vasomotor
reflex.**

```
V1 (external nasal / anterior ethmoidal) + V2 (infraorbital)
→ trigeminal sensory nuclear complex (brainstem)
→ Descending projections to:
  → Superior salivatory nucleus → pterygopalatine ganglion
  → Parasympathetic fibers to:
    → Nasal mucosal blood vessels (vasodilation/vasoconstriction)
    → Nasal glands (secretion modulation)
    → Lacrimal gland
```

**V1 anterior ethmoidal significance:** the anterior ethmoidal nerve
has both EXTERNAL branches (skin at EX-HN8) and INTERNAL branches
(nasal mucosa from INSIDE). Stimulating the external branch activates
afferents whose central connections overlap with internal nasal
mucosal afferents — a "shortcut" to modulate nasal function from the
skin surface.

**Comparison with LI20:**
- LI20 (V2): infraorbital afferents → V2 to brainstem. Access to
  pterygopalatine ganglion through V2 maxillary connections.
- EX-HN8 (V1/V2 boundary): additional V1 nasociliary pathway →
  direct nasal mucosal afferent overlap. May access BOTH V1 and V2
  pathways.
- Through-needling (LI20→EX-HN8): activates V2 at insertion and
  V1/V2 boundary at terminus = maximum trigeminal nasal coverage.

**Pterygopalatine ganglion significance:** the pterygopalatine
(sphenopalatine) ganglion is the principal parasympathetic relay for
the nose. Modulating its activity is the proposed mechanism for
acupuncture's effect on nasal symptoms. Note: deep needling to the
sphenopalatine ganglion directly (Xinwu point) has its own evidence
base (MA, 11 RCTs). EX-HN8 is the SURFACE approach to the same
neural circuit that Xinwu targets DEEP.

Gap: V1/V2 dual-pathway contribution not experimentally verified.
→ Cross-ref: A01 S1.4, A02 S4.5

#### Kỹ thuật kích thích + dose-response

Châm (B01): 0.3–0.5 cun oblique, directed upward at ~45° toward
nasal cavity center. Subcutaneous only. De Qi: distension and soreness
at the nose, may radiate to nasal bridge. Thin needle (0.25 mm).
Retention: 20–30 minutes.

Through-needling from LI20: insert at LI20, direct needle
subcutaneously upward to reach EX-HN8 region. Single needle
stimulates full nasal lateral territory (V2→V1/V2 boundary).

Bấm huyệt (B02): fingertip pressure at the bone-cartilage junction.
Self-applicable. Pressing alongside the nose during congestion is a
common self-care behavior that approximates EX-HN8 stimulation.

Cứu (B08): not commonly used. Risk of burns to thin nasal skin.

Dry needling (B11): not standard for nasal points.

Acupoint catgut embedding: used in chronic rhinitis protocols —
catgut thread implanted subcutaneously for sustained stimulation.
Supported by MA data (OR 5.19).
→ Cross-ref: B01 S3.1, B02

#### An toàn

**Angular artery:** the angular artery (terminal facial artery branch)
ascends along the nose and is relatively large here. Bruising common.
Avoid deep or vigorous insertion.

**Nasal cavity penetration:** deep needling directed medially could
penetrate through the bone-cartilage junction into the nasal cavity.
Stay superficial (subcutaneous only), angle upward not inward.

**Eye proximity:** medial canthus and lacrimal apparatus nearby
superiorly. Needle direction should be along the nose, not toward
orbit.

**Pregnancy:** NOT contraindicated.

---

### EX-HN9 — Nội Nghênh Hương (内迎香, Nèiyíngxiāng)

**Evidence tier: C (Minimum Viable Entry)**

"Inner Yingxiang" (内 = inner, 迎香 = Welcome Fragrance = LI20). On
the nasal mucosa, at the junction of the inferior turbinate and the
nasal septum. The ONLY standard extra point needled intranasally — a
unique technique requiring insertion through the nostril onto the
nasal mucosa itself. Counterpart trio: LI20 (external, nasal ala) →
EX-HN8 (external, bone-cartilage junction) → EX-HN9 (INTERNAL, nasal
mucosa). Traditional indications: eye disorders (counterintuitively)
and nasal conditions.

#### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): on the nasal mucosa, at the
junction of the inferior nasal concha (inferior turbinate) and the
nasal septum.

Anatomical landmarks: accessed through the nostril. The inferior
turbinate is the largest turbinate, projecting medially from the
lateral nasal wall. EX-HN9 is where the inferior turbinate's medial
edge meets the nasal septum — deep within the nasal cavity.

Technique: insert fine needle through the nostril onto the nasal
mucosal surface at the inferior turbinate-septum junction. Intranasal
needling — unique among all 361+48 standard acupoints.

Needle depth: 0.2–0.3 cun into nasal mucosa. Superficial pricking
is standard — deep insertion risks vascular damage.

#### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve: anterior ethmoidal nerve (V1 nasociliary branch) — primary
sensory nerve of anterior nasal mucosa. Also nasopalatine nerve (V2)
at the septum. CN I (olfactory nerve) fibers in superior nasal mucosa
(not directly at EX-HN9 level but in the same cavity).
Spinal segment: none (cranial nerve V1/V2).
Vascular: sphenopalatine artery branches (from maxillary artery).
Anterior ethmoidal artery contributes to anterior septum. Kiesselbach
plexus (Little's area) — the anterior-inferior septal vascular
anastomosis — is nearby, making epistaxis a significant risk.
Tissue: nasal mucosa (respiratory epithelium). Richly vascularized,
highly innervated. No muscle or fascia — mucosa directly overlies
periosteum/perichondrium.
→ Cross-ref: A01 S1.4

#### Phân loại huyệt

Extra point (EX-HN9). No meridian category memberships.

The only intranasal standard acupoint. Distinguished from:
- LI20 (external, nasal ala, V2)
- EX-HN8 (external, bone-cartilage junction, V1/V2 boundary)
- GV25 Suliao (nasal tip, external, V1)
- EX-HN9 (internal, mucosal surface, V1 anterior ethmoidal)

#### Cross-tradition mapping

TCM: EX-HN9 (Neiyingxiang 内迎香). Indications: eye disorders (via V1
nasociliary → long ciliary nerves → eyeball sensory innervation of
cornea, iris, ciliary body), nasal obstruction, epistaxis, anosmia.
The eye indication is attributed to the nasociliary nerve serving BOTH
nasal mucosa and eyeball through different branches. ✓
Trigger point: ✗ (nasal mucosa, no muscle trigger points)
Ashi: ✗ (patients do not identify intranasal tenderness as a clinical
complaint site)
Marma: ✗ (no intranasal Marma in Ayurvedic system)
Head zone: V1 anterior ethmoidal territory, directly ON nasal mucosa.
The nerve supplies the target tissue itself, but this is direct local
access rather than segmental convergence. ✗
Anatomy Trains: ✗ (mucosal surface)
Neurogenic spot: ✗ (Kim 2017 = trunk only)
Cupping: ✗ (intranasal)

**Convergence score: 1/8** (TCM ✓ only). Expected for a uniquely
intranasal point — no other medical tradition describes needle
insertion onto nasal mucosa.

#### Chỉ định truyền thống

Classical and modern sources:
- Eye disorders: acute conjunctivitis, eye pain. Attributed to V1
  nasociliary pathway — the nasociliary nerve innervates BOTH nasal
  mucosa (via anterior ethmoidal) and eyeball (via long ciliary
  nerves). Stimulating the nasal branch may reflexively affect the
  ophthalmic branch through shared trigeminal pathways.
- Nasal obstruction, rhinitis, epistaxis
- Anosmia
- Nasal polyps (historical)

#### Bằng chứng hiện đại

No systematic review or RCT specifically studying EX-HN9 as standalone
point. Li et al. 2024 (Medicine, 14 RCTs, 1,009 participants) studied
intranasal acupuncture for allergic rhinitis using intranasal points
including Neiyingxiang — superior to control for nasal symptom scores,
but EX-HN9-specific contribution not isolated.

#### An toàn

**Epistaxis (nosebleed):** PRIMARY RISK. Nasal mucosa is highly
vascularized. Kiesselbach plexus (anterior-inferior septum) nearby —
most common epistaxis site. Use very fine needle, superficial pricking
only.

**Infection risk:** nasal mucosa is a non-sterile surface. Strict
aseptic technique with sterile needles essential.

**Patient discomfort:** intranasal needling is significantly more
uncomfortable than external needling. Patient cooperation and
stillness essential — sudden movement during intranasal needling
risks mucosal tearing and uncontrolled bleeding.

**Anticoagulants:** avoid intranasal needling in anticoagulated
patients (high epistaxis risk).

**Pregnancy:** NOT specifically contraindicated but caution warranted.

---

*Phase 2 Batch 2 COMPLETE. 4 entries added: EX-HN6 (Erjian, Tier B,
1/8 convergence — premier bloodletting point, Cochrane + 3 MAs),
EX-HN7 (Qiuhou, Tier B, 1/8 — DANGEROUS retrobulbar, 80% globe
perforation in AE), EX-HN8 (Shangyingxiang, Tier B, 4/8 — 39-RCT
allergic rhinitis MA, V1/V2 boundary), EX-HN9 (Neiyingxiang, Tier C,
1/8 — only intranasal acupoint). Total entries in AP-15: 9/48. No new
⚡ framework notes (running total: 41). Key findings: EX-HN6 is the
most systematically studied bloodletting point (4 SRs/MAs) despite
lowest convergence (1/8) — technique-driven value vs convergence-driven
value; EX-HN7 has the most comprehensive safety documentation of any
dangerous acupoint (cadaver depth thresholds + 14-case AE review);
EX-HN8 V1/V2 boundary position may explain dual-pathway nasal
efficacy.*

---

### EX-HN10 — Tụ Tuyền (聚泉, Jùquán)

**Evidence tier: C (Minimum Viable Entry)**

"Gathering Spring" (聚 = gather, 泉 = spring/source). On the tongue
dorsal surface midline, at the center of the tongue. A tongue-surface
point for tongue disorders, speech difficulty, and salivation
abnormalities. Part of the 4-point sublingual/oral cluster: EX-HN10
(tongue surface) + EX-HN11 (sublingual frenulum) + EX-HN12+13
(sublingual veins). Used alongside Haiquan (EX-HN11) in tongue
acupuncture protocols for post-stroke dysphagia.

#### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): on the tongue, at the midpoint
of the dorsal surface, on the midline.

Anatomical landmarks: patient protrudes the tongue; the point is at
the center of the dorsal (upper) tongue surface, on the median raphe.

Needle depth: 0.1–0.2 cun oblique or transverse. Shallow insertion
into the tongue mucosa/musculature. Bloodletting with three-edged
needle is also used.

#### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve: lingual nerve (V3 mandibular division) — general sensation
(touch, pain, temperature) to the anterior 2/3 of the tongue. CN IX
(glossopharyngeal) — taste and sensation to the posterior 1/3. CN XII
(hypoglossal) — motor to all intrinsic and extrinsic tongue muscles
(except palatoglossus). CN VII (chorda tympani via lingual nerve) —
taste to the anterior 2/3.
Spinal segment: none (cranial nerve territory).
Vascular: lingual artery branches (from external carotid). The tongue
is highly vascularized.
Tissue: tongue mucosa (stratified squamous epithelium with papillae)
→ intrinsic tongue muscles (longitudinal, transverse, vertical).
→ Cross-ref: A01 S1.4

#### Phân loại huyệt

Extra point (EX-HN10). No meridian category memberships.

The only standard acupoint on the tongue dorsal surface. Distinguished
from: EX-HN11 Haiquan (sublingual frenulum, ventral surface),
EX-HN12+13 Jinjin-Yuye (sublingual veins, ventral surface).

#### Cross-tradition mapping

TCM: EX-HN10 (Juquan 聚泉). Tongue stiffness, tongue swelling,
speech difficulty, salivation disorders (both excess and deficiency),
loss of taste. Used for "opening the tongue orifice" — TCM regards
the tongue as the external opening of the Heart. ✓
Trigger point: ✗ (tongue musculature not part of trigger point system)
Ashi: ✗ (tongue center not a self-identified tender point)
Marma: ✗ (no tongue surface Marma)
Head zone: ✗ (V3+CN IX+CN XII cranial territory, no viscerotome)
Anatomy Trains: ✗ (tongue musculature)
Neurogenic spot: ✗ (Kim 2017 = trunk only)
Cupping: ✗ (tongue surface)

**Convergence score: 1/8** (TCM ✓ only). Expected for an intraoral
tongue point.

#### Chỉ định truyền thống

Classical and modern sources: tongue stiffness and swelling, slurred
speech, aphasia, salivation disorders, loss of taste, halitosis.

Clinical: used alongside EX-HN11 (Haiquan) in tongue acupuncture
protocols.
→ Cross-ref: EX-HN11 (Haiquan) for combined protocol evidence

#### Bằng chứng hiện đại

No systematic review or RCT specifically studying EX-HN10 as standalone
point. Two RCTs used Juquan + Haiquan together for dysphagia: Cai et al.
2015 (n=180, post-stroke, p<0.01) and Gao et al. 2024 (n=64, Parkinson's,
p<0.05). EX-HN10-specific contribution cannot be isolated from the pair.
→ Cross-ref: EX-HN11 Bằng chứng hiện đại

#### An toàn

**Bleeding:** the tongue is highly vascularized. Bloodletting
produces visible bleeding — apply pressure with gauze. Avoid in
anticoagulated patients.

**Gag reflex:** tongue needling may trigger gag reflex in sensitive
patients. Require patient to protrude tongue fully and remain still.

**Infection risk:** oral cavity is non-sterile. Use sterile needles.

**Pregnancy:** NOT contraindicated.

---

### EX-HN11 — Hải Tuyền (海泉, Hǎiquán)

**Evidence tier: B (Standard Entry)**

"Sea Spring" (海 = sea, 泉 = spring/source). On the sublingual
frenulum linguae midpoint, between Jinjin (EX-HN12, left) and Yuye
(EX-HN13, right). **GHOST POINT #13 (鬼封 Gui Feng, "Ghost Seal") —
the LAST of Sun Si-miao's 13 Ghost Points.** The "Seal" (封) that
CLOSES the entire Ghost Point treatment sequence. The ONLY Ghost
Point that is an extra-meridian point — all 12 preceding Ghost Points
are on standard meridians. Its sublingual location is the MOST
INTERIOR of all 13 Ghost Points (hidden under the tongue, concealed
within the oral cavity). Dual identity: "Sea Spring" (海泉) for its
fluid-generating TCM function, and "Ghost Seal" (鬼封) as the
psychiatric protocol closer. Three cranial nerves accessible: V3
lingual (most superficial), CN XII hypoglossal (motor), CN IX
glossopharyngeal (deeper). **COMPLETES 13/13 GHOST POINT INVENTORY.**

#### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): on the sublingual surface, at the
midpoint of the frenulum linguae.

Anatomical landmarks: patient lifts the tongue to the palate,
exposing the sublingual area. The frenulum linguae is the midline
mucosal fold connecting the ventral tongue surface to the floor of
the mouth. EX-HN11 is at its midpoint. Flanked by the deep lingual
veins visible through the mucosa (sites of EX-HN12 Jinjin on the
left and EX-HN13 Yuye on the right).

Needle depth: 0.1–0.2 cun. Primary technique is bloodletting (prick
with three-edged needle) rather than retained needle insertion.
For dysphagia protocols: 20 mm depth at ~70° angle with filiform
needle (Gao et al. 2024).

#### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- **Lingual nerve (V3 mandibular division):** the most superficially
  accessible nerve at the frenulum. Provides general sensation to the
  anterior 2/3 of the tongue, floor of mouth, and lingual gingiva.
  Loops around the submandibular duct and ascends into the tongue,
  passing close to the frenulum. Has communicating branches with
  CN XII.
- **Hypoglossal nerve (CN XII):** courses superficial to the
  hyoglossus muscle. Motor innervation to ALL intrinsic and extrinsic
  tongue muscles (except palatoglossus, CN X). The deep lingual veins
  form the "vena comitans of the hypoglossal nerve" — traveling
  together in intimate anatomical relationship. CN XII damage →
  tongue paralysis/dysarthria.
- **Glossopharyngeal nerve (CN IX):** enters the tongue inferiorly
  to styloglossus and hyoglossus. Provides taste and sensation to the
  posterior 1/3. Deeper and more posterior than V3/XII — less directly
  accessible from the anterior frenulum.

Spinal segments: none (cranial nerve territory).

Vascular:
- **Deep lingual veins:** visible through the sublingual mucosa,
  flanking the frenulum bilaterally. The veins targeted by Jinjin-Yuye
  bloodletting.
- **Lingual frenal artery:** terminal branch of the sublingual artery,
  coursing close to the frenulum. Classical texts (Ling Shu) warn
  against excessive needling here due to hemorrhage risk from this
  artery.
- **Sublingual artery:** branch of the lingual artery.

Adjacent structures:
- **Submandibular duct (Wharton's duct):** opens at the sublingual
  papilla on each side of the frenulum — very close to EX-HN11.
- **Sublingual gland:** in the floor of the mouth lateral to the
  frenulum.
→ Cross-ref: A01 S1.4

#### Phân loại huyệt (Point Category Membership)

Extra point (经外奇穴): EX-HN11 in WHO nomenclature. Not on any
meridian.

Five Shu: not applicable. Yuan-Source: not applicable.
Luo-Connecting: not applicable. Xi-Cleft: not applicable.
Back-Shu: not applicable. Front-Mu: not applicable.
Eight Confluent: not applicable. Hui-Meeting: not applicable.

Special designations:

**GHOST POINT #13 — 鬼封 Gui Feng ("Ghost Seal")**

The 13th and FINAL Ghost Point in Sun Si-miao's sequence (Qianjin
Yaofang, 652 CE). The name 鬼封 means "Ghost Seal" — the seal (封)
that closes the entire treatment sequence after the preceding 12
points have progressively addressed escalating levels of Shen
(spirit/mental) disturbance. The 13 Ghost Points are organized as
4 trinities + 1 seal:

- **1st Trinity (#1-3):** GV26 鬼宫 + LU11 鬼信 + SP1 鬼垒 — initial
  Shen wandering, confusion, inappropriate emotional responses
- **2nd Trinity (#4-6):** PC7 鬼心 + BL62 鬼路 + GV16 鬼枕 — deeper
  disturbance affecting Heart and brain, obsession, rigidity
- **3rd Trinity (#7-9):** ST6 鬼床 + CV24 鬼市 + PC8 鬼窟 — addiction
  and dependency, mania, hallucinations
- **4th Trinity (#10-12):** GV23 鬼堂 + CV1 鬼藏 + LI11 鬼臣 — the
  deepest level, catatonia, dissociation, constitutional breakdown.
  Spans the widest anatomical range (crown → perineum → elbow).
- **Seal (#13):** EX-HN11 鬼封 — closes the sequence, prevents
  return of "ghosts" (pathological patterns)

**Structural observations:**
- **Yin-Yang symmetry:** 6 Yang points (GV26, GV16, GV23, ST6, BL62,
  LI11) + 6 Yin points (LU11, SP1, PC7, CV24, PC8, CV1) = 12
  balanced on standard channels. The 13th point (EX-HN11, extra
  meridian) transcends this Yin-Yang channel symmetry.
- **Channel distribution:** GV appears 3× (most of any channel), CV
  2×, PC 2×, all others 1× each. Heavy Du-Ren presence underscores
  treatment of central axis and consciousness.
- **ONLY extra-meridian Ghost Point:** all 12 other Ghost Points are
  on standard meridians. EX-HN11 stands outside the channel system
  as the deliberate extra-channel closure point.
- **Most interior location:** the sequence arguably progresses from
  exterior (#1 GV26 philtrum, exposed skin) to increasingly interior
  locations, culminating in the sublingual frenulum (#13) — hidden
  under the tongue within the oral cavity.

**Yintang substitution:** due to the difficult sublingual location,
some later texts substituted Yintang (EX-HN3) as the 13th ghost
point. The original Sun Si-miao text specifies Haiquan/Gui Feng.

**FINAL 13/13 GHOST POINT INVENTORY — Complete Atlas Table:**

| # | Point | Channel | Ghost Name | Trinity | Atlas |

| 1 | GV26 Renzhong | GV | 鬼宫 Ghost Palace | 1st | AP-13 |
| 2 | LU11 Shaoshang | LU | 鬼信 Ghost Faith | 1st | AP-01 |
| 3 | SP1 Yinbai | SP | 鬼垒 Ghost Fortress | 1st | AP-04 * |
| 4 | PC7 Daling | PC | 鬼心 Ghost Heart | 2nd | AP-09 |
| 5 | BL62 Shenmai | BL | 鬼路 Ghost Road | 2nd | AP-07 |
| 6 | GV16 Fengfu | GV | 鬼枕 Ghost Pillow | 2nd | AP-13 |

| 9 | PC8 Laogong | PC | 鬼窟 Ghost Cave | 3rd | AP-09 |
| 10 | GV23 Shangxing | GV | 鬼堂 Ghost Hall | 4th | AP-13 |
| 11 | CV1 Huiyin | CV | 鬼藏 Ghost Store | 4th | AP-14 |
| 12 | LI11 Quchi | LI | 鬼臣 Ghost Minister | 4th | AP-02 |

completed 2026-08-20).

→ Cross-ref: AP-13 GV26 (#1), GV16 (#6), GV23 (#10); AP-14 CV24
(#8), CV1 (#11); AP-09 PC7 (#4), PC8 (#9); AP-07 BL62 (#5); AP-03
ST6 (#7); AP-02 LI11 (#12); AP-01 LU11 (#2); AP-04 SP1 (#3);
B01 S1.3.3

#### Cross-tradition mapping

**1. TCM acupoint:** EX-HN11 (Haiquan 海泉, "Sea Spring" / Ghost
name: Gui Feng 鬼封, "Ghost Seal"). Functions: generates fluids
(生津), clears tongue orifice (利舌), calms the spirit (安神 — via
Ghost Point function). Primary indications: tongue swelling,
salivation disorders (excess or deficiency), aphasia, speech
difficulty, thirst, post-stroke dysphagia. As Ghost Point #13:
mania, psychosis, "ghost possession" (the final step in the
13-Ghost-Point psychiatric protocol). ✓

**2. Trigger point:** no correspondence. The sublingual frenulum
has no skeletal muscle to develop trigger points. ✗

**3. Ashi correspondence:** no correspondence. Patients do not
identify tenderness at the sublingual frenulum. ✗

**4. Marma point:** no sublingual Marma point in Ayurvedic anatomy.
The nearest Marma is Shringataka (श्रृंगाटक), associated with the
palate and nasal cavity rather than the sublingual space. ✗

**5. Head zone:** V3 lingual nerve + CN XII + CN IX. Cranial nerve
territory with no viscerotome projection. ✗

**6. Anatomy Trains:** no correspondence. Sublingual mucosa and
frenulum are not part of myofascial meridian chains. ✗

**7. Neurogenic spot:** no data (Kim 2017 = trunk only). ✗

**8. Cupping point:** not applicable (sublingual). ✗

**Convergence score: 1/8** (TCM ✓ only). Expected for a sublingual
point. Like EX-HN6 (Erjian) and EX-HN7 (Qiuhou), Haiquan's value
derives from SPECIALIZED ACCESS (sublingual space, Ghost Point
protocol) rather than anatomical convergence across traditions.

#### Chỉ định truyền thống (DATA — ghi nhận, không endorse as mechanism)

Nguồn cổ:
- Qianjin Yaofang (Sun Si-miao, 652 CE): Ghost Point #13 for
  "sealing" the psychiatric treatment sequence. Sun Si-miao notably
  reinterpreted "ghosts" (gui) not as literal spirits but as "excess
  phlegm obstructing the Heart orifices and clouding consciousness"
  — a pivotal shift from supernatural to physiological framework.
- Ling Shu (Spiritual Pivot): warned against needling this point "to
  excess" due to risk of "incessant bleeding and loss of voice."
- Lei Jing Tu Yi: described the point location.

Nguồn hiện đại (Deadman, WHO):
- Tongue swelling and stiffness
- Salivation disorders (excess — sialorrhea; deficiency — xerostomia)
- Aphasia, speech difficulty
- Thirst (消渴 wasting-thirst, historical diabetes context)
- Post-stroke dysphagia

Kinh nghiệm lâm sàng nổi bật:
- **Post-stroke dysphagia protocol:** Haiquan (EX-HN11) + Juquan
  (EX-HN10) is the standard tongue acupuncture pair for dysphagia.
  Two RCTs explicitly using this combination:
  - Cai et al. 2015 (n=180): tongue acupuncture effective rate
    96.67% vs 66.67% (p<0.01), NIHSS improvement superior.
  - Gao et al. 2024 (n=64, Parkinson's disease): effective rate
    84.38% vs 53.13% (p<0.05), complication rate 12.5% vs 34.38%.
- **Ghost Point psychiatric protocol:** used as the FINAL point in
  the 13-Ghost-Point sequence for severe mental disturbance. Classical
  instruction: "Not necessarily must all 13 be needled; stopping at
  5-6 points is usually sufficient."
→ Cross-ref: AP-13 GV26 (Ghost #1, emergency), AP-09 PC7+PC8
(Ghost #4+#9 pair), AP-14 CV1+CV24 (Ghost #11+#8)

#### Bằng chứng hiện đại

**① Post-stroke dysphagia — 🟡 Plausible**

MA (Li et al. 2023, Frontiers in Neuroscience, 15 RCTs, 1,094
participants): tongue acupuncture for post-stroke dysphagia.
Clinical efficacy MD 3.83 (95% CI 2.61–5.62, p<0.00001). SSA
improvement MD −1.65 (p<0.00001). Haiquan used in multiple included
trials but individual contribution not isolated.

RCT (Cai et al. 2015, n=180): Juquan+Haiquan vs GB20+PC6. VFSS
improvement significant (p<0.05). Pneumonia incidence 3.3% vs 8.9%.

RCT (Gao et al. 2024, n=64): Juquan+Haiquan for Parkinson's
dysphagia. Grade 1-2 swallowing 78.13% vs 31.26% (p<0.05).

**② Post-stroke aphasia — 🟡 Plausible**

SR (Yang et al. 2022, 20 RCTs, 1,355 participants): tongue
acupuncture (including sublingual points) for post-stroke aphasia.
ABC score improvement MD 11.48 (95% CI 2.20–20.75, p<0.00001).
Haiquan falls within the "tongue acupoints" category though not
individually named in all studies.

**③ Psychiatric (Ghost Point indication) — 🔴 Unvalidated**

No modern controlled evidence for the Ghost Point psychiatric
protocol. The 13-Ghost-Point system is a classical framework with
no RCT validation.

#### Dynamic acupoint properties

No disease-state sensitization data for the sublingual frenulum.
Sublingual vein engorgement (varicose sublingual veins) is a known
clinical sign in elderly patients and certain conditions, but this
is a vascular finding rather than neural sensitization at EX-HN11
specifically.
→ Cross-ref: C04 S4 (Dynamic Acupoint Phenomenon)

#### Cơ chế giải thích (BSA framework attribution)

**Triple cranial nerve access:**

EX-HN11 accesses three cranial nerves from a single sublingual site:

```
V3 lingual nerve (SENSORY — most superficial)
→ Trigeminal sensory nuclear complex
→ NTS, PAG → pain modulation, autonomic integration

CN XII hypoglossal (MOTOR)
→ Hypoglossal nucleus (medulla)
→ ALL tongue muscles → swallowing, speech
→ Direct motor pathway for dysphagia mechanism

CN IX glossopharyngeal (SENSORY + TASTE — deeper)
→ NTS (nucleus tractus solitarius)
→ Swallowing reflex center, taste processing
→ Parasympathetic → salivary gland secretion
```

**Dysphagia mechanism (Haiquan+Juquan combination):**

Post-stroke dysphagia involves damaged corticobulbar pathways from
motor cortex to CN IX/X/XII nuclei in the medulla. Tongue
acupuncture at Haiquan+Juquan may work through:
1. Direct CN XII motor stimulation → facilitates tongue motor
   retraining via peripheral nerve activation
2. CN IX afferent stimulation → NTS → swallowing reflex center
   modulation
3. Nociceptive input → descending modulation → neuroplasticity at
   the medullary level

fNIRS neuroimaging (Sun et al. 2025): tongue acupuncture enhanced
fronto-temporo-parietal connectivity and interhemispheric
sensorimotor connections. Right dorsolateral prefrontal cortex showed
elevated betweenness centrality.

**Comparison with CV23 (Lianquan):**
- CV23 accesses CN XII from BELOW the jaw (suprahyoid region,
  external approach)
- EX-HN11 accesses CN XII from ABOVE the tongue (sublingual,
  intraoral approach)
- Both target the same motor nucleus but from opposite anatomical
  directions

Gap: whether peripheral CN XII stimulation produces central motor
cortex plasticity is unconfirmed. No molecular effector identified.
→ Cross-ref: AP-14 CV23 (CN XII external access), A01 S1.4

#### Kỹ thuật kích thích + dose-response

Thích huyết (bloodletting, classical technique): three-edged needle
prick at the frenulum midpoint. Express small amount of blood.
Classical Ghost Point technique — "sealed" the treatment.

Châm (B01, dysphagia protocol): 0.30 mm × 40 mm filiform needle,
20 mm depth at 70° angle (Gao et al. 2024). Daily 30-minute
sessions for 30 consecutive days. Both Juquan (tongue surface) and
Haiquan (sublingual) needled in the same session.

Bấm huyệt (B02): not practical (sublingual frenulum not accessible
to sustained pressure).

Cứu (B08): not applicable (intraoral).

Dry needling (B11): not applicable.
→ Cross-ref: B01 S3.1

#### An toàn

**Hemorrhage:** the sublingual area is richly vascularized (deep
lingual veins, lingual frenal artery, sublingual artery). The Ling
Shu specifically warns against excessive needling here: risk of
"incessant bleeding and loss of voice." Apply firm gauze pressure
after needling.

**CN XII damage:** the hypoglossal nerve courses in intimate
relationship with the deep lingual veins. Aggressive or deep needling
risks CN XII injury → tongue paralysis on the affected side →
dysarthria. This is the basis of the Ling Shu "loss of voice" warning.

**Infection risk:** the oral cavity is non-sterile. Use sterile
needles. Post-procedure oral rinse recommended.

**Gag reflex:** sublingual needling requires patient to hold tongue
elevated. May trigger gag reflex or swallowing reflex.

**Anticoagulants:** avoid sublingual needling/bloodletting in
anticoagulated patients.

**Pregnancy:** NOT contraindicated specifically, though intraoral
procedures carry inherent discomfort.

### EX-HN12 — Kim Tân (金津, Jīnjīn)

**Evidence tier: C (Minimum Viable Entry)**

"Golden Fluid" (金 = gold, 津 = fluid/saliva). On the sublingual
surface, on the vein to the LEFT of the frenulum linguae. Paired
with EX-HN13 Yuye ("Jade Fluid") on the right — the Jinjin-Yuye
pair is ALWAYS treated together, never individually. Bloodletting at
the sublingual veins for tongue/oral disorders, aphasia, and
traditionally for diabetes-related thirst. The deep lingual veins
visible through the sublingual mucosa are the targets.

#### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): on the sublingual surface, on the
vein to the left of the frenulum linguae.

Anatomical landmarks: patient lifts tongue to the palate. The deep
lingual veins are visible as blue-purple vessels through the thin
sublingual mucosa, flanking the frenulum on both sides. EX-HN12 is
on the LEFT vein; EX-HN13 is on the RIGHT vein. EX-HN11 (Haiquan)
sits at the frenulum BETWEEN them.

Technique: bloodletting exclusively. Prick the visible sublingual
vein with a three-edged needle or lancet. Express small amounts of
dark venous blood. NOT filiform needle retention.

#### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve: lingual nerve (V3), CN XII (hypoglossal) — same sublingual
innervation as EX-HN11 Haiquan. The deep lingual veins form the vena
comitans of CN XII.
Spinal segment: none (cranial nerve).
Vascular: deep lingual vein (target vessel for bloodletting) +
sublingual artery branches. The veins are superficial here — visible
through mucosa — making them ideal bloodletting targets.
→ Cross-ref: A01 S1.4

#### Phân loại huyệt

Extra point (EX-HN12). No meridian category memberships.

Part of the **Jinjin-Yuye pair** — ALWAYS treated bilaterally (left +
right sublingual veins). Part of the 4-point sublingual cluster:
EX-HN10 (tongue surface) + EX-HN11 (frenulum) + EX-HN12 (left vein)
+ EX-HN13 (right vein).

#### Cross-tradition mapping

TCM: EX-HN12 (Jinjin 金津). Functions: clears Heat (清热), reduces
tongue swelling (消舌肿), generates fluids (生津). Indications:
tongue swelling with difficulty speaking, tongue stiffness, aphasia,
severe morning sickness, diabetes-related thirst, salivary gland
disorders. Always used as a pair with EX-HN13 Yuye. ✓
Trigger point: ✗ (sublingual vein, no muscle)
Ashi: ✗ (not a self-identified tender site)
Marma: ✗ (no sublingual Marma)
Head zone: ✗ (cranial nerve, no viscerotome)
Anatomy Trains: ✗
Neurogenic spot: ✗
Cupping: ✗

**Convergence score: 1/8** (TCM ✓ only).

#### Chỉ định truyền thống

Classical and modern sources (Jinjin-Yuye pair):
- Tongue swelling and stiffness
- Aphasia, speech difficulty (post-stroke)
- Severe morning sickness (hyperemesis — classical indication)
- Diabetes-related thirst (消渴, historical)
- Salivary gland disorders
- Oral ulcers

#### Bằng chứng hiện đại

No systematic review or RCT specifically studying the Jinjin-Yuye pair.
One clinical case series: Zheng et al. 2011 (n=32, PMID 21644299):
sublingual bloodletting at Jinjin-Yuye combined with Sishencong
acupuncture for brain concussion sequelae. Published in Chinese.

#### An toàn

**Hemorrhage:** the deep lingual veins are the target — bleeding is
EXPECTED and intentional. Control blood volume (small amount of dark
venous blood). Apply gauze pressure after expression. Excessive
bloodletting risks significant venous hemorrhage in the sublingual
space.

**Sublingual hematoma:** if venous bleeding tracks into the
sublingual tissues rather than expressing externally, a sublingual
hematoma can form → potential airway compromise (floor of mouth
swelling). Monitor after procedure.

**Anticoagulants:** CONTRAINDICATED in patients on anticoagulants or
with bleeding disorders.

**Pregnancy:** caution warranted despite historical use for morning
sickness — bloodletting procedures carry inherent risks.

---

### EX-HN13 — Ngọc Dịch (玉液, Yùyè)

**Evidence tier: C (Minimum Viable Entry)**

"Jade Fluid" (玉 = jade, 液 = fluid/liquid). On the sublingual
surface, on the vein to the RIGHT of the frenulum linguae. The RIGHT
counterpart of EX-HN12 Jinjin ("Golden Fluid") — the Jinjin-Yuye
pair is ALWAYS treated together as a bilateral sublingual venous
bloodletting procedure.

#### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): on the sublingual surface, on the
vein to the right of the frenulum linguae.

Same sublingual anatomy as EX-HN12 Jinjin. RIGHT deep lingual vein,
flanking the frenulum on the right side.

Technique: bloodletting — identical to EX-HN12.

#### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Identical sublingual innervation and vasculature as EX-HN12: V3
lingual nerve, CN XII, deep lingual vein (right side).
→ Cross-ref: EX-HN12 (same anatomy, opposite side)

#### Phân loại huyệt

Extra point (EX-HN13). No meridian category memberships.

RIGHT counterpart of EX-HN12 Jinjin. Part of the Jinjin-Yuye pair
and the 4-point sublingual cluster.

#### Cross-tradition mapping

Same as EX-HN12. ✓ for TCM only.

**Convergence score: 1/8** (TCM ✓ only).

#### Chỉ định truyền thống

Same indications as EX-HN12 Jinjin. Always treated as a pair.
→ See EX-HN12 for full indication details.

#### Bằng chứng hiện đại

→ See EX-HN12 Bằng chứng hiện đại. Always treated as a pair.

#### An toàn

Same safety considerations as EX-HN12: hemorrhage control,
sublingual hematoma risk, anticoagulant contraindication.
→ See EX-HN12 for full safety details.

Si-miao Ghost Points now fully documented across the Atlas (GV×3,
CV×2, PC×2, LU, SP, BL, ST, LI, Extra). Ghost Point channel
distribution: 6 Yang + 6 Yin + 1 Extra-meridian = perfect Yin-Yang
symmetry with extra-channel seal. EX-HN11 triple cranial nerve access
(V3+CN XII+CN IX) provides the anatomical basis for tongue
acupuncture dysphagia protocols (2 RCTs with Haiquan explicitly
named). Sublingual 4-point cluster (EX-HN10+11+12+13) represents the
densest intraoral point concentration in the 361+48 system.*

---

### EX-HN14 — Ế Minh (翳明, Yìmíng)

**Evidence tier: B (Standard Entry)**

"Shielded Brightness" (翳 = screen/shield + 明 = brightness/clarity).
1 cun posterior to TE17 (Yifeng), behind the mastoid process. Shares
the 翳 character with TE17 翳风 (Yifeng, "Wind Screen") — both in
the sheltered retromastoid region, but with different functional
emphasis: TE17 treats Wind (风) conditions (facial paralysis), while
EX-HN14 restores Brightness (明) — vision, clarity, wakefulness.
Primary traditional emphasis: vision disorders (明 = brightness → eye
clarity), insomnia (calming the spirit), tinnitus. Located in the
transition zone between periauricular (TE17/CN VII) and occipital
(GB20/C2) neural territories.

**Yiming vs Anmian:** Yiming (翳明, EX-HN14, WHO-standardized) and
Anmian (安眠, "Peaceful Sleep", NOT WHO-standardized) are separate
but closely related posterior cervical points often conflated in
clinical literature:

| Point | Location | Name meaning | Primary emphasis |
|-------|----------|-------------|------------------|
| **Yiming (EX-HN14)** | 1 cun posterior to TE17 | "Shielded Brightness" | Vision, ears |
| **Anmian** | Midpoint TE17–GB20 | "Peaceful Sleep" | Insomnia |

Both share C2-C3 neural territory (~1-2 cm apart). Many studies cite
"Anmian" without specifying which anatomical location was needled.
Some texts designate Anmian I = EX-HN14 location, Anmian II = closer
to GB20. This nomenclatural ambiguity is clinically significant: the
mechanistic literature (electroacupuncture-induced sleep via NTS)
studied Anmian specifically, but the findings likely apply to the
shared C2-C3 neural territory that both points occupy.

#### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): posterior to the mastoid process,
1 cun posterior to Yifeng (TE17).

Anatomical landmarks: palpate the mastoid process (bony prominence
behind the earlobe). TE17 is in the depression between the
mandibular angle and the anterior mastoid surface. Move 1 cun
(~1.5 cm) directly posterior from TE17, onto or just behind the
posterior surface of the mastoid process.

Spatial relationships:
- TE17 (Yifeng): anterior to mastoid, at stylomastoid foramen.
  CN VII exit. 1 cun ANTERIOR to EX-HN14.
- GB12 (Wangu): posterior and inferior to the mastoid tip.
- GB20 (Fengchi): in the depression between trapezius and SCM at
  the occiput, significantly more posterior-inferior.
- Anmian (non-WHO): midpoint between TE17 and GB20, ~2-3 cm
  posterior-inferior to EX-HN14.

Needle depth: 15–25 mm perpendicular or slightly inferiorly directed.
The mastoid bone provides a hard limit superficially — angle away
from bone toward the posterior cervical soft tissues. Oblique
insertion directed inferomedially toward C1-C2 area (classical).

#### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- **Greater occipital nerve (C2 dorsal ramus):** the LARGEST purely
  sensory nerve of the scalp (~8 mm² cross-section). Emerges between
  C1 and C2, pierces semispinalis capitis, and ascends over the
  posterior skull. At EX-HN14's level, the nerve or its branches are
  in the deep muscular layers behind the mastoid. C2 dorsal ramus
  afferents converge with trigeminal (V1) afferents in the
  trigeminocervical complex (spinal trigeminal nucleus, C1-C3 levels)
  — the validated neural basis for cervicogenic headache referral to
  the frontal/periorbital region and the target of greater occipital
  nerve blocks for migraine.
- **Lesser occipital nerve (C2, cervical plexus):** sensory to the
  skin behind the ear and posterior scalp. Ascends along the posterior
  border of SCM. Directly at EX-HN14's superficial location.
- **Great auricular nerve (C2-C3, cervical plexus):** posterior branch
  innervates the mastoid skin and posterior auricle. Ascending from
  Erb's point toward the ear — passes through or near EX-HN14.
- **Posterior auricular nerve (CN VII branch):** motor to posterior
  auricular muscle and occipitalis. Exits immediately after CN VII
  main trunk emerges at stylomastoid foramen (TE17).

Spinal segments: C2-C3 (cervical plexus sensory) + CN VII (motor,
indirect via posterior auricular branch).

The C2 afferent convergence is the key anatomical feature. C2 dorsal
ramus → trigeminocervical complex → descending modulation of
brainstem arousal/autonomic centers. This pathway is the validated
basis for greater occipital nerve blocks and occipital
neurostimulation for migraine — interventions that target the SAME C2
territory as EX-HN14.
→ Cross-ref: A01 S2.3, A07 S2.1, AP-10 TE17

Vascular: posterior auricular artery (from external carotid),
ascending behind the ear. Occipital artery branches at this level.

Muscle layers (superficial → deep):
- Skin → subcutaneous tissue
- SCM (posterior border/insertion at mastoid)
- Splenius capitis (inserts at lateral superior nuchal line and
  mastoid)
- Semispinalis capitis (deeper, between superior and inferior nuchal
  lines)
- Mastoid process periosteum (bony limit)

#### Phân loại huyệt (Point Category Membership)

Extra point (经外奇穴): EX-HN14 in WHO nomenclature. Not on any
meridian.

Five Shu: not applicable. Yuan-Source: not applicable.
Luo-Connecting: not applicable. Xi-Cleft: not applicable.
Back-Shu: not applicable. Front-Mu: not applicable.
Eight Confluent: not applicable. Hui-Meeting: not applicable.

Special designations:
- **Primary insomnia extra point** (when conflated with Anmian): among
  the 3 most frequently selected acupoints in insomnia acupuncture
  protocols, alongside HT7 (Shenmen) and GV20 (Baihui).
- **Yiming-Anmian nomenclatural ambiguity:** many clinical studies cite
  "Anmian" without specifying whether the location corresponds to
  EX-HN14 (Yiming) or the non-WHO Anmian (midpoint TE17–GB20). Some
  older numbering systems: Anmian I (Ex-8) ≈ EX-HN14, Anmian II
  (Ex-9) = closer to GB20. The systematic review literature typically
  groups both under "Anmian" due to this ambiguity.
→ Cross-ref: B01 S1.3.3

#### Cross-tradition mapping

**1. TCM acupoint:** EX-HN14 (Yiming 翳明, "Shielded Brightness").
Functions: benefits the eyes (明目), calms the spirit (安神),
benefits the ears (聪耳). Indications: insomnia (primary modern use,
especially as Anmian), visual disorders (myopia, optic atrophy, night
blindness — 翳明 = restoring brightness behind the "screen"),
tinnitus, vertigo, headache. The dual function profile — eyes +
sleep — reflects two C2 afferent pathways: trigeminocervical →
periorbital (vision), and brainstem arousal modulation (sleep). ✓

**2. Trigger point:** splenius capitis trigger point — located near
the mastoid process and lateral nuchal line, refers pain to the vertex
and behind the eye (Travell & Simons). SCM deep clavicular trigger
point refers to the ear (deep ear pain, fullness). Both are in the
muscular layers adjacent to EX-HN14. Moderate correspondence — the
trigger points are in muscle bellies near the point, not at the exact
bony landmark. 🟡
→ Cross-ref: B11

**3. Ashi correspondence:** the retromastoid/suboccipital region is
commonly tender in cervicogenic headache, chronic tension headache,
and insomnia-related cervical tension. Patients frequently identify
tenderness behind the ear when presenting with head/neck complaints.
🟡

**4. Marma point:** Vidhura Marma (विधुर, "Widow/Bereaved") — behind
the ear, near the mastoid process. Injury consequence: deafness
(Sushruta Samhita). One of 107 Marma points. Location overlaps
broadly with the TE17-to-EX-HN14 zone. Shared auditory indication
(tinnitus/deafness). Partial correspondence — Vidhura is described at
the retro-auricular depression (closer to TE17), EX-HN14 is 1 cun
further posterior, but within the same anatomical zone (~2 cm). 🟡
→ Cross-ref: AP-10 TE17 (same Marma noted)

**5. Head zone:** C2 dermatome (greater occipital nerve territory).
C2 is the pivotal segment for cervicogenic headache — the
trigeminocervical complex is the validated convergence zone where C2
afferents synapse with V1 trigeminal neurons. Greater occipital nerve
blocks at C2 are a first-line treatment for cervicogenic headache and
some migraines. Genuine dermatomal significance. 🟡

**6. Anatomy Trains:** SCM (Lateral Line) inserts at the mastoid
process — EX-HN14 is immediately posterior to this insertion.
Splenius capitis (deep cervical myofascial system) also inserts at
mastoid/lateral nuchal line. The point sits at the junction of two
major myofascial line terminations on the skull. 🟡

**7. Neurogenic spot:** no data (Kim 2017 = trunk only). ✗

**8. Cupping point:** not applicable. The retromastoid area is too
small and irregular for cup application. ✗

**Convergence score: 6/8** (TCM ✓, Trigger point 🟡, Ashi 🟡, Marma
🟡, Head zone 🟡, Anatomy Trains 🟡). HIGH — the retromastoid region
is a genuine multi-system convergence zone where cervical plexus
(C2-C3 sensory), myofascial attachments (SCM + splenius capitis), and
the C2 trigeminocervical pathway all physically overlap. Matches
EX-HN1 Sishencong (6/8) for second-highest extra point convergence.
The dual clinical utility — vision (trigeminocervical → periorbital)
and sleep (brainstem arousal modulation) — is consistent with C2's
multi-target brainstem connectivity.

#### Chỉ định truyền thống (DATA — ghi nhận, không endorse as mechanism)

Nguồn cổ:
- Extra point collections: Yiming for "restoring brightness, calming
  the spirit, benefiting the ears." The name 翳明 emphasizes VISUAL
  disorders — "brightness (明) behind the screen (翳)" suggests
  restoring vision through a posterior cervical approach.
- Anmian (安眠, "Peaceful Sleep"): closely related non-WHO point
  with primary insomnia indication. Name explicitly designates
  function.

Nguồn hiện đại (Deadman, WHO):
- Insomnia (primary modern use, especially when treated as Anmian)
- Visual disorders: myopia, optic atrophy, night blindness, eye pain
- Tinnitus, vertigo
- Headache (occipital)
- Mental disorders (historical, via "calming the spirit")

Kinh nghiệm lâm sàng nổi bật:
- **Standard insomnia protocol:** GV20 + HT7 + Anmian/EX-HN14 + SP6.
  Each point accesses a different pathway: GV20 (vertex/cerebral),
  HT7 (cardiac/emotional), Anmian/Yiming (brainstem/arousal), SP6
  (Yin convergence/endocrine).
- **Insomnia frequency:** Anmian/Yiming appears in ~46% of insomnia
  acupuncture study protocols — 3rd most frequently used after HT7
  Shenmen (~67%) and GV20 Baihui (~62%).
- **Self-acupressure for sleep:** commonly taught bedtime self-care
  technique — press behind the mastoid bilaterally for 1–3 minutes.
  One of the most recommended acupressure points for sleep
  improvement.
→ Cross-ref: AP-05 HT7, AP-13 GV20, AP-04 SP6, AP-08 KI6 (L1948)

#### Bằng chứng hiện đại

**① Insomnia — 🟡 Plausible (as protocol component)**

Anmian/Yiming is a CORE point in insomnia acupuncture protocols but
has NOT been isolated in any controlled study. All evidence is for
multi-point protocols.

Network meta-analysis (Frontiers in Psychiatry 2026, 63 RCTs):
multiple acupoint stimulation therapies (acupuncture,
electroacupuncture, acupoint catgut embedding, acupoint application)
all showed effectiveness for primary insomnia. Anmian among
most-used points. Electroacupuncture showed advantage over manual
acupuncture for some outcomes.

Meta-analysis for perimenopausal insomnia (2025, 40+ RCTs):
acupuncture protocols frequently include Anmian. Effective for
Pittsburgh Sleep Quality Index improvement over sham.

Case series (Vanamoorthy et al. 2026, SAGE): Anmian I (Ex-8) and
Anmian II (Ex-9) needling for insomnia. Significant PSQI
improvement. Non-controlled.

Mechanistic evidence (rat, Anmian-specific):
- Cheng et al. 2011 (Evidence-Based Complementary and Alternative
  Medicine): electroacupuncture at Anmian → cholinergic activation in
  caudal nucleus tractus solitarius (NTS) → β-endorphin release →
  μ-opioid receptor activation → NREM sleep enhancement. Blocked by
  naloxone (opioid antagonist) and scopolamine (muscarinic antagonist)
  injected into NTS.
- Cheng et al. 2012: 100 Hz electroacupuncture → κ-opioid receptor
  in caudal NTS → slow-wave sleep enhancement. Dose-dependently
  blocked by κ-receptor antagonist.

This is the most detailed frequency-dependent mechanistic model for
any insomnia acupoint: low-frequency (10 Hz) → enkephalinergic
(μ-receptor), high-frequency (100 Hz) → dynorphinergic (κ-receptor).
Parallels the general electroacupuncture frequency–opioid relationship
(B01: 2 Hz → enkephalin, 100 Hz → dynorphin).

**② Optic disorders — 🔴 Unvalidated**

Traditional primary indication (明 "brightness" in name). No modern
controlled evidence for Yiming-specific vision effects. The C2 →
trigeminocervical → V1 convergence provides anatomical rationale for
periorbital referral but no direct optic nerve pathway exists at C2.

**③ Tinnitus — 🔴 Unvalidated**

Traditional indication. No point-specific evidence. Used alongside
TE17 + TE3 + SI19 + GB2 in tinnitus protocols.

#### Dynamic acupoint properties

Cervical muscle tension in insomnia: chronic insomnia is associated
with elevated cervical-occipital muscle tension (elevated EMG in
upper trapezius and suboccipital muscles, Fernández-de-las-Peñas
2006). The retromastoid region, as an attachment zone for SCM and
splenius capitis, may develop palpable tenderness and increased tone
in insomnia patients. Consistent with the Dynamic Acupoint Phenomenon
(C04 §4): the clinical significance of EX-HN14 increases when
cervical tension accompanies sleep disturbance — the point becomes
MORE tender and MORE responsive to needling in the pathological state.

Similarly, patients with cervicogenic headache typically show point
tenderness behind the mastoid (greater occipital nerve territory) —
the "activated" C2 dermatome sensitizes this region.
→ Cross-ref: C04 S4

#### Cơ chế giải thích (BSA framework attribution)

**Primary pathway: C2 afferents → trigeminocervical complex →
brainstem arousal modulation.**

```
Greater occipital nerve (C2) + lesser occipital (C2-C3)
→ dorsal horn C1-C3
→ Trigeminocervical complex (TCC)
→ Two downstream pathways:
  1. → Trigeminal sensory nuclei → periorbital/visual referral
     (explains 明 "brightness" traditional indication)
  2. → NTS (nucleus tractus solitarius) → brainstem sleep centers
     (explains insomnia therapeutic effect)
```

**NTS-opioidergic mechanism (Anmian-specific data):**

Electroacupuncture at Anmian (C2-C3 territory) → cholinergic
activation in caudal NTS → stimulates opioidergic neurons →
β-endorphin release → opioid receptor activation → NREM sleep
enhancement.

Frequency-dependent pathway:
- 10 Hz → μ-opioid receptor → NREM enhancement
- 100 Hz → κ-opioid receptor → slow-wave sleep enhancement

Both blocked by NTS-targeted antagonists (naloxone, scopolamine,
κ-antagonist respectively). The NTS is the central relay for vagal
afferents — C2-C3 input to NTS may represent a non-vagal pathway to
the same brainstem autonomic hub that vagal stimulation targets.

**C2-NTS anatomical basis:** C2 afferent input to NTS may occur via:
1. Direct projections from upper cervical dorsal horn to caudal NTS
2. Indirect via V1 trigeminal convergence at TCC → trigeminal
   sensory nuclei → NTS
3. Great auricular nerve (C2-C3) → cervical afferents adjacent to
   vagal territory

Gap: whether stimulation at EX-HN14 (Yiming, on mastoid) produces
the same NTS activation as Anmian (midpoint TE17–GB20). Both are
~1-2 cm apart in the same C2-C3 territory, but direct comparison
has not been performed.
→ Cross-ref: A01 S2.3, A02 S3.5, B01 S3.1

#### Kỹ thuật kích thích + dose-response

Châm (B01): 15–25 mm perpendicular or directed slightly inferiorly
toward C1-C2 level. De Qi: distension and heaviness behind the ear,
may radiate to the occiput or temporal region. Retention: 20–30
minutes. Standard gauge (0.30 mm).

Điện châm (EA): low frequency (2–10 Hz) preferred for sleep
promotion based on the NTS-opioidergic mechanism. Low-frequency
continuous wave → μ-opioid receptor → NREM enhancement. Dense-sparse
alternating (2/100 Hz) releases both enkephalin and dynorphin.
Duration: 20–30 minutes, early evening or before bedtime.

Bấm huyệt (B02): self-applicable bedtime technique. Press behind
the mastoid with thumb or fingertip, circular pressure for 1–3
minutes bilaterally before sleep. One of the most commonly taught
acupressure self-care points for insomnia.

Cứu (B08): indirect moxa applicable. Not the primary modality.

Dry needling (B11): suboccipital/retromastoid area is a standard dry
needling target for cervicogenic headache. SCM insertion and splenius
capitis at the mastoid are recognized dry needling sites for cervical
myofascial pain. Technique overlaps with acupuncture at EX-HN14.
→ Cross-ref: B01 S3.1, B02, B11

#### An toàn

**Vertebral artery:** deep, forceful insertion directed
anteromedially could approach the vertebral artery within the
transverse foramen of C1 or the vertebral artery loop between C1 and
the occiput. Risk is LOW at standard depths (15–25 mm) as the artery
is deep and medial, but depth and direction must be controlled.
Direction: perpendicular or posterior, NOT anteromedial.

**CN VII proximity:** facial nerve main trunk exits at TE17, 1 cun
anterior. Standard needling at EX-HN14 stays posterior to the
stylomastoid foramen. However, if the needle is directed anteriorly
toward TE17, CN VII damage is theoretically possible.

**Posterior auricular artery:** superficial vessel behind the ear.
Minor bruising possible.

**Pregnancy:** NOT specifically contraindicated.

---

### EX-HN15 — Kính Bách Lao (颈百劳, Jǐngbǎiláo)

**Evidence tier: C (Minimum Viable Entry)**

"Neck Hundred Labors" (颈 = neck + 百 = hundred + 劳 = labor/toil).
Named for treating consumptive exhaustion (百劳 = "hundred labors" =
chronic overwork/wasting diseases). 2 cun above GV14 (Dazhui), 1 cun
lateral to the posterior midline — in the posterior cervical
musculature at approximately C5 level. Historical primary indication:
tuberculosis/consumptive diseases (phthisis). Modern use: neck pain,
thyroid disorders, scrofula (cervical lymphadenopathy).

#### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): 2 cun above Dazhui (GV14), 1 cun
lateral to the posterior midline.

Anatomical landmarks: GV14 is below the spinous process of C7.
Measure 2 cun superiorly along the posterior midline (approximately
to the C5 spinous process level), then 1 cun (~1.5 cm) laterally.
The point lies in the posterior cervical musculature, in the groove
between the upper trapezius (superficial) and splenius capitis (deep).

Needle depth: 0.5–0.8 cun perpendicular. The posterior cervical
muscles are thick at this level.

Spatial relationships:
- GV14 (Dazhui): 2 cun inferior, on the midline
- BL10 (Tianzhu): at the occipital margin, ~1.3 cun lateral to GV15
  — higher and more lateral
- Jiaji C5 (EX-B2): at C5, 0.5 cun lateral — same level, more medial

#### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve: medial branches of C5-C6 dorsal rami (deep cervical
musculature) + cutaneous branches of cervical plexus C3-C4
(superficial). Accessory nerve (CN XI) — motor to trapezius, passes
through or deep to the upper trapezius at this level. Deep cervical
plexus at C3-C6 innervates semispinalis, multifidus, and rotatores.
Spinal segment: C3-C6 (cervical plexus + dorsal rami).
Vascular: branches of the transverse cervical artery (from
thyrocervical trunk) and deep cervical artery. Occipital artery
branches may reach this level.
Tissue layers: skin → subcutaneous tissue → trapezius (upper fibers)
→ splenius capitis → semispinalis capitis → semispinalis cervicis →
multifidus.
→ Cross-ref: A01 S2.3

#### Phân loại huyệt

Extra point (EX-HN15). No meridian category memberships.

Located in the posterior cervical region between the GV midline and
the BL inner line (1.5 cun lateral). At the SAME lateral distance as
the Jiaji points (EX-B2, 0.5 cun lateral) but in the cervical spine
region above the Jiaji series (which starts at T1). Functionally
analogous to cervical Jiaji but classified separately as a named
extra point with specific traditional indications.

#### Cross-tradition mapping

TCM: EX-HN15 (Jingbailao 颈百劳). Functions: nourishes Yin (滋阴),
clears Heat (清热), resolves phlegm (化痰), stops cough (止咳).
Indications: tuberculosis/consumptive diseases (historical primary),
neck stiffness and pain, cough, sore throat, night sweats, scrofula
(cervical lymphadenopathy), thyroid disorders. The name reflects the
consumptive disease paradigm — chronic wasting from TB or
overwork. ✓
Trigger point: splenius capitis trigger point at C5-C6 level refers
to vertex headache (Travell & Simons). Semispinalis capitis trigger
point at mid-cervical level refers to occipital headband pattern.
Upper trapezius medial fibers have trigger points that refer to the
posterolateral neck. EX-HN15 sits among these documented cervical
muscle trigger points. 🟡
Ashi: upper cervical/posterior neck tenderness is among the most
common patient-identified pain sites. Palpable tenderness at the
posterior cervical musculature is ubiquitous in neck pain
presentations. 🟡
Marma: Manya Marma is at the carotid region (anterior neck).
Krikatika Marma is at the atlantooccipital junction (higher). Neither
corresponds to C5 level posterior cervical. ✗
Head zone: C3-C4 dermatome (cervical). Cervical spondylosis territory.
The posterior cervical musculature at C5 level receives cutaneous
innervation from C3-C4 and deep innervation from C5-C6 — a multi-
segmental zone. 🟡
Anatomy Trains: upper trapezius is a key station on the Superficial
Back Line and the Lateral Line (SCM-trapezius continuity). EX-HN15
is in the upper trapezius fibers — directly on a major myofascial
pathway. 🟡
Neurogenic spot: ✗ (Kim 2017 = trunk only)
Cupping: posterior cervical/upper back is a standard cupping area in
both TCM and Hijama traditions. Upper trapezius cupping for neck
stiffness, headache, and respiratory complaints is well
documented. 🟡

**Convergence score: 5/8** (TCM ✓, Trigger point 🟡, Ashi 🟡, Head
zone 🟡, Anatomy Trains 🟡, Cupping 🟡). Moderately high — the
posterior cervical/upper trapezius region is a recognized clinical
site across multiple independent traditions. The upper trapezius
trigger point is among the most robustly documented in the entire
trigger point literature. Tier C assignment reflects limited
point-specific EVIDENCE, not low anatomical convergence.

#### Chỉ định truyền thống

Classical and modern sources:
- Neck pain and stiffness (primary modern use)
- Tuberculosis / consumptive diseases (historical primary — 百劳 =
  exhaustion from chronic disease; name origin from TB treatment era)
- Scrofula (cervical lymphadenopathy, historically TB-associated)
- Thyroid disorders: goiter, hyperthyroidism. Anatomical proximity to
  thyroid gland at C5-C6 level provides local rationale.
- Night sweats (Yin deficiency / TB association in TCM)
- Cough, sore throat (cervical sympathetic and recurrent laryngeal
  nerve proximity)

Moxa is traditionally emphasized for 百劳 disorders (consumptive
conditions = Yin/Yang deficiency → warming technique).

#### Bằng chứng hiện đại

No systematic review or RCT specifically studying EX-HN15. The historical
tuberculosis indication is now managed by pharmacotherapy. Modern use is
primarily for cervical pain — treated via adjacent meridian points (GV14,
BL11, SI15) in most protocols.

#### An toàn

**Spinal cord:** the cervical spinal cord is directly deep and medial
at C5 level. At 1 cun lateral and standard needle depth (0.5–0.8 cun
≈ 12–20 mm), the trajectory should NOT approach the spinal canal.
Do NOT angle needles medially toward the midline.

**Vertebral artery:** runs through the transverse foramina of C6-C1.
At 1 cun lateral to midline, the artery is more anterior (in the
transverse foramen) than the needle trajectory. Deep medial angulation
must be avoided.

**CN XI (accessory nerve):** passes through the posterior cervical
triangle entering trapezius from its deep surface. Damage would cause
trapezius weakness. Risk is low at standard depths with perpendicular
insertion.

**Pregnancy:** NOT contraindicated.

*Phase 2 Batch 4 COMPLETE. 2 entries added: EX-HN14 (Yiming, Tier B,
6/8 convergence — retromastoid insomnia point, C2 trigeminocervical
pathway, EA→NTS→β-endorphin sleep mechanism, Yiming/Anmian
nomenclatural distinction documented), EX-HN15 (Jingbailao, Tier C,
5/8 — posterior cervical, TB historical, upper trapezius trigger point
zone). **EX-HN REGION 15/15 COMPLETE** — all Head & Neck extra points
documented. Total entries in AP-15: 15/48. No new ⚡ framework notes
(running total: 41). Key findings: EX-HN14 has the most detailed
frequency-dependent mechanistic model for any insomnia acupoint
(10 Hz → μ-opioid via NTS, 100 Hz → κ-opioid via NTS, Cheng 2011-
2012); the Yiming/Anmian distinction (WHO-standardized vs clinical
tradition, ~1-2 cm apart, shared C2-C3 territory) is a significant
nomenclatural finding. Posterior cervical convergence: EX-HN14 6/8
and EX-HN15 5/8 — both high for extra points — confirming the
retromastoid and upper trapezius regions as genuine multi-system
convergence zones. Neural progression of all 15 EX-HN entries:
cranial V1 (EX-HN1-4) → V1/V2 junction (EX-HN5, EX-HN8) → V2
(EX-HN7) → V3+auricular (EX-HN6) → V3+CN XII+CN IX sublingual
(EX-HN10-13) → V1/V2/cranial (EX-HN9) → C2-C3 cervical plexus
(EX-HN14) → C3-C6 cervical (EX-HN15). A complete cranial-to-
cervical neural gradient across the EX-HN series.*

## EX-B — Back (背部)

### EX-B1 — Định Suyễn (定喘, Dìngchuǎn)

**Evidence tier: B (Standard Entry)**

"Calm Dyspnea" (定 = calm/stabilize + 喘 = wheezing/dyspnea). 0.5 cun
lateral to GV14 (Dazhui, below C7 spinous process), bilateral. The name
directly declares function — one of the most descriptively named
acupoints in the system. The most frequently used extra point for asthma,
appearing in 58% of 183 qualified asthma acupuncture studies (2nd only to
BL13 Feishu). Located at the cervicothoracic junction (C7-T1) in
immediate proximity to the stellate ganglion (cervicothoracic sympathetic
ganglion), which provides sympathetic innervation to the lungs, heart,
and upper body. The 0.5 cun lateral position places Dingchuan at the
same distance from midline as the Jiaji (EX-B2) series — essentially the
cervicothoracic "Jiaji position" — but NOT part of the WHO-standardized
Jiaji series (which begins at T1).

#### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): 0.5 cun lateral to the posterior
midline, at the level of the lower border of the spinous process of C7
(vertebra prominens). Bilateral.

Anatomical landmarks: identify C7 (vertebra prominens — the most
prominent spinous process at the base of the neck, palpable with the
neck flexed). GV14 is on the midline in the interspinous space between
C7 and T1. Dingchuan is 0.5 cun (~8-10 mm) lateral to this midpoint,
in the laminar groove.

Spatial relationships:
- GV14 (Dazhui): midline, directly medial. The principal point of the
  cervicothoracic junction. Same spinal level.
- BL11 (Dashu): 1.5 cun lateral to T1 spinous process — the first
  Back-Shu level point, one segment below and more lateral.
- EX-B2 Jiaji T1: 0.5 cun lateral to T1 — one segment below Dingchuan.
  Dingchuan occupies the "C7 Jiaji level" but is separately named for
  its specific respiratory function.

Needle depth: 12-20 mm (0.5-0.8 cun), perpendicular or slightly oblique
toward the spine. At this depth in the cervicothoracic junction, the
needle reaches the deep paraspinal musculature (multifidus, rotatores)
but should NOT approach the spinal canal (lamina provides bony barrier).

#### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- **C8 dorsal ramus, medial branch:** primary sensory and motor
  innervation of the deep paraspinal muscles (multifidus, rotatores) at
  the C7-T1 level. At 0.5 cun lateral, the needle accesses the dorsal
  ramus trunk or its proximal medial branch in the laminar groove — the
  most direct segmental nerve access point.
- **T1 dorsal ramus contribution:** the cervicothoracic junction receives
  innervation from both C8 and T1 segments. T1 is the uppermost segment
  of the thoracic sympathetic outflow to the lungs.
- **Stellate ganglion proximity:** the stellate (cervicothoracic)
  ganglion — formed by fusion of the inferior cervical and first thoracic
  sympathetic ganglia (~80% of population) — lies ANTERIOR to the C7
  transverse process, deep to the prevertebral fascia. It provides
  sympathetic fibers to the head, neck, upper limbs, heart, AND lungs.
  Needling at Dingchuan does not directly reach the stellate ganglion
  (which is anterior), but C8-T1 dorsal ramus afferents converge with
  the sympathetic pathway at the spinal cord level through somatovisceral
  reflexes.
  → Cross-ref: AP-13 GV14

Spinal segments: C8-T1 (cervicothoracic junction).

Vascular: dorsal branch of the deep cervical artery, transverse cervical
