artery branches. Vertebral artery runs through the transverse foramina
of C6-C1, ANTERIOR to the needle trajectory at this level.

Muscle layers (superficial → deep):
- Skin → subcutaneous tissue
- Trapezius (upper fibers — origin at C7 and T1 spinous processes)
- Rhomboid minor (origin at C7-T1 spinous processes)
- Splenius cervicis (passes through this level; origin T3-T6,
  inserts C1-C3)
- Semispinalis capitis / cervicis
- Multifidus / rotatores (deepest — on lamina)
→ Cross-ref: A01 S2.3, A03 S2.2

Fascial transition: C7 is the inferior attachment of the ligamentum
nuchae — a major fascial anchor point. Below C7, the thoracolumbar
fascia begins. Dingchuan sits at this cervical-to-thoracic fascial
transition zone.

#### Phân loại huyệt (Point Category Membership)

Extra point (经外奇穴): EX-B1 in WHO nomenclature. Not on any meridian.

Five Shu: not applicable. Yuan-Source: not applicable.
Luo-Connecting: not applicable. Xi-Cleft: not applicable.
Back-Shu: not applicable. Front-Mu: not applicable.
Eight Confluent: not applicable. Hui-Meeting: not applicable.

Special designations:
- **Premier respiratory extra point:** the most frequently selected
  extra point for asthma in clinical practice and research. In a data
  mining analysis of 183 controlled clinical trials for asthma,
  Dingchuan appeared in 58% of studies — 2nd only to BL13 Feishu
  (Wang et al. 2022, Complementary Medicine Research). Association rule
  mining identified the combination BL13 + BL23 + BL20 + Dingchuan as
  a priority acupoint combination.
- **Cervicothoracic "Jiaji-adjacent" position:** at 0.5 cun lateral
  to midline, Dingchuan occupies the same distance as the Jiaji (EX-B2)
  series, but at C7 level — one segment above the Jiaji T1 starting
  point. Functionally distinguished by its specific respiratory name and
  indication.
→ Cross-ref: B01 S1.3.3

#### Cross-tradition mapping

**1. TCM acupoint:** EX-B1 Dingchuan (定喘, "Calm Dyspnea"). Functions:
calms wheezing and dyspnea (定喘), descends Lung Qi, stops cough,
disperses Wind. Primary indication: asthma, wheezing, bronchospasm.
Secondary: cough, dyspnea, chest oppression, neck stiffness. The name
is purely functional — 定 calm + 喘 wheezing — making it one of the
most transparently named acupoints (compare: most acupoints use poetic/
anatomical metaphors). ✓

**2. Trigger point:** C7-T1 paraspinal trigger points — multifidus and
rotatores at the cervicothoracic junction have documented trigger points
(Travell & Simons). These TrPs cause local and referred pain in the
upper back and neck region. The referred pain pattern is musculoskeletal
(not respiratory), so the TrP correspondence relates to Dingchuan's
SECONDARY indication (neck stiffness) rather than its primary
respiratory function. 🟡
→ Cross-ref: B11

**3. Ashi correspondence:** the C7-T1 junction area is commonly tender
in patients with cervical stiffness and upper back tension. In patients
with chronic respiratory conditions, this area may show somatovisceral
reflex-mediated sensitization. Partial — Dingchuan is located by
measurement from GV14 rather than by palpation, but tenderness at this
site is clinically common. 🟡

**4. Marma point:** no corresponding Marma point at this specific
cervicothoracic paravertebral location. Krikatika Marma is at the neck
junction but corresponds more closely to the upper cervical region
(C1-C2), not C7-T1. ✗

**5. Head zone:** C8-T1 dermatome. T1 represents the uppermost thoracic
sympathetic segment. The stellate ganglion at C7-T1 is the sympathetic
gateway — preganglionic fibers from T1-T4/T5 traverse through the
stellate ganglion to reach the pulmonary sympathetic plexus. T1 is at
the upper edge of the lung Head zone (primary pulmonary viscerotome
T2-T5 via BL13 Feishu at T3), making this a partial match for
respiratory visceral correspondence. 🟡
→ Cross-ref: A07 S2.1

**6. Anatomy Trains:** not applicable at this specific paravertebral
depth. The Superficial Back Line runs through erector spinae, but this
is generic for all posterior spinal points. ✗

**7. Neurogenic inflammatory spot:** skin impedance at Dingchuan is
significantly altered in asthma patients: 29.4±21 kΩ (asthma) vs
13.8±7.9 kΩ (healthy), with impedance negatively correlated to FEV1
(Ngai & Jones 2009). This disease-state change in electrical properties
at a specific acupoint location is consistent with the dynamic acupoint
phenomenon (C04 §4) and the neurogenic inflammatory spot model's
prediction that organ disease produces measurable changes at
segmentally-linked skin sites. 🟡
→ Cross-ref: C04 S4

**8. Cupping point:** the GV14 area (including bilateral Dingchuan) is
one of the most widely used cupping sites in TCM for respiratory
conditions — common cold, asthma, bronchitis, and fever. A standard
cup (~5 cm diameter) placed on the GV14 area covers both GV14 and
bilateral Dingchuan simultaneously. Upper back cupping for respiratory
disorders is also practiced in Hijama (Islamic cupping tradition). ✓

**Convergence score: 6/8** (TCM ✓, Trigger point 🟡, Ashi 🟡, Head
zone 🟡, Neurogenic 🟡, Cupping ✓). HIGH — the cervicothoracic
junction (C7-T1) is a genuine multi-system convergence zone at the
transition between cervical and thoracic anatomy. The stellate ganglion
provides sympathetic gateway access to the lungs. The skin impedance
evidence provides rare direct validation of disease-state acupoint
sensitization at an extra point, correlating with objective lung
function (FEV1).

#### Chỉ định truyền thống (DATA — ghi nhận, không endorse as mechanism)

Nguồn cổ:
- Dingchuan first documented in extra point collections (Ming-Qing era),
  NOT in classical canonical texts (Neijing, Nanjing). Characteristic of
  extra points — emerging from clinical empiricism rather than channel
  theory.
- Primary: asthma (喘), wheezing, dyspnea, cough
- Secondary: neck stiffness, shoulder pain (cervicothoracic junction
  musculoskeletal complaints)

Modern textbook (Deadman, WHO):
- Cough, asthma, bronchitis, chest oppression, shortness of breath
- Neck stiffness, pain of the upper back
- Appears in virtually all comprehensive asthma acupuncture protocols

Classical combinations:
- **Dingchuan + BL13 (Feishu) + CV22 (Tiantu):** "respiratory triangle"
  accessing cervicothoracic (Dingchuan, T1 sympathetic), mid-thoracic
  (BL13, T3 lung Back-Shu), and anterior throat (CV22, tracheal) —
  three distinct approaches to the respiratory system simultaneously.
- **BL13 + BL23 + BL20 + Dingchuan:** most frequent combination in
  data mining of 183 asthma clinical trials (Wang et al. 2022).
- **Sanfu herbal patching triad:** Dingchuan + BL13 + BL23 — the
  standard acupoint combination for seasonal herbal patch therapy.

#### Bằng chứng hiện đại

**Asthma — aggregate evidence (combination protocols):**
Data mining analysis of 183 controlled clinical trials for asthma:
Dingchuan appeared in 58% of studies, making it the 2nd most frequently
selected acupoint after BL13 Feishu (Wang et al. 2022, Complementary
Medicine Research). Dingchuan was identified as a "key node acupoint" in
the acupoint interconnection network.
🟡 Plausible (high frequency of use confirms clinical consensus, but no
point-specific isolation — always used in combination)

**Acu-TENS at Dingchuan — near-point-specific evidence:**
Acu-TENS applied to bilateral Dingchuan for 45 minutes × 3 sessions/week
× 4 weeks improved lung function (FEV1, FVC) in patients with chronic
bronchial disease. One of the few near-point-specific interventions
documented for Dingchuan.
🟡 Plausible (small study, limited sample, but rare point-specific
intervention data)

**Sanfu acupoint herbal patching (三伏贴 sān fú tiē):**
External application of processed herbal preparations (white mustard
seed, rhizoma corydalis, radix kansui) at Dingchuan + BL13 + BL23
during the three hottest "sanfu" periods of summer — based on the
"winter disease treated in summer" (冬病夏治) principle.
- Stable asthma: 34 RCTs, n=3,313. Sanfu patching + conventional
  therapy decreased mean frequency of asthma exacerbations vs
  conventional therapy alone (Ji et al. 2017, Complementary Therapies
  in Medicine). Methodological quality concerns noted.
- Pediatric asthma: 11 RCTs, n=882. Adjunctive herbal patching
  reduced frequency of acute asthma attacks (MD −1.62 attacks/year)
  (Medicine 2020).
🔴 Unvalidated for point-specific effect (multi-point combination +
multi-component herbal intervention — cannot isolate Dingchuan's
individual contribution or distinguish acupoint-specific from
pharmacological effects)

**Skin impedance — dynamic acupoint evidence:**
Ngai & Jones 2009 (Chinese Journal of Rehabilitation Medicine):
skin impedance at Dingchuan significantly HIGHER in asthma subjects
(29.4±21 kΩ) vs healthy controls (13.8±7.9 kΩ). Negatively correlated
with FEV1 (p<0.05) — worse lung function → higher impedance.
🟢 Confirmed (for impedance change as disease-state marker, not for
therapeutic efficacy)
→ Cross-ref: C04 S4 (dynamic acupoint phenomenon)

**Overall assessment:** Dingchuan has strong USAGE evidence (high
frequency across clinical studies, consistent expert consensus) but
limited point-specific efficacy evidence. Most studies use multi-point
combinations, preventing isolation of Dingchuan's individual
contribution. The strongest point-specific data is the skin impedance
study, which validates the acupoint's dynamic responsiveness rather
than its therapeutic efficacy.

#### Cơ chế giải thích

**Proposed segmental-sympathetic pathway:**
Needling at Dingchuan → C8-T1 dorsal ramus afferents → dorsal horn
convergent neurons → (1) somatovisceral reflex via T1 preganglionic
sympathetic neurons → stellate ganglion → pulmonary sympathetic plexus
→ bronchial smooth muscle relaxation (bronchodilation); (2) descending
modulation via spinal-brainstem circuitry → NTS → vagal tone
modulation → attenuation of parasympathetic bronchoconstriction.

**Stellate ganglion connection:** the stellate ganglion (anterior to
C7 transverse process) is the principal sympathetic relay for the
lungs, heart, and upper limbs. All T1-T4 preganglionic sympathetic
fibers to the lungs pass through or synapse at the stellate ganglion.
Needling at the C7-T1 dorsal ramus level creates afferent input that
converges with this sympathetic outflow at the spinal cord level —
the segmental equivalent of approaching the stellate ganglion's
territory from the posterior (somatovisceral reflex) rather than the
anterior (direct ganglion block).
→ Cross-ref: A02 S3.5, AP-13 GV14

**GV14 anti-inflammatory evidence (indirect):** EA at GV14 has been
shown to activate the vagus-adrenal axis to inhibit systemic
inflammation in animal models of allergic asthma (documented in AP-13
GV14 entry). Dingchuan at 0.5 cun lateral to GV14 accesses the same
C7-T1 spinal level and may engage similar anti-inflammatory pathways,
though no Dingchuan-specific mechanistic study has been published.

**Confidence:** 🟡 Synthesis. The somatovisceral reflex pathway is
established (Sato 1997), and the stellate ganglion's role in pulmonary
sympathetic innervation is validated anatomy. The specific pathway from
Dingchuan needling → bronchodilation has not been directly demonstrated.

#### Kỹ thuật kích thích

**Needling:** perpendicular or slightly oblique toward the spine, 12-20
mm (0.5-0.8 cun). De Qi sensation typically described as aching and
heaviness in the upper back, occasionally radiating toward the shoulder.

**Electroacupuncture:** used in asthma protocols, typically 2-15 Hz
(mixed or low frequency), often paired bilaterally or with BL13.

**Acupoint herbal patching (穴位贴敷):** Dingchuan is a core point for
Sanfu herbal patching — the largest non-needle evidence base for this
point. Herbal patch (white mustard seed, Yan Hu Suo, Gan Sui) applied
bilaterally at Dingchuan + BL13 + BL23 during the three hottest 10-day
periods of summer (三伏 Sanfu). Treatment follows the 冬病夏治
("winter disease treated in summer") principle.

**Cupping:** commonly applied at the GV14 area, with cups covering both
GV14 and bilateral Dingchuan simultaneously. Dry or wet cupping for
respiratory conditions, common cold, and fever.

**Moxibustion:** direct or indirect moxa for chronic asthma, especially
in Yang-deficiency patterns.

**TENS/Acu-TENS:** transcutaneous electrical nerve stimulation at
bilateral Dingchuan — the only modality with near-point-specific
evidence for lung function improvement.

#### Dynamic acupoint properties

**Skin impedance changes in asthma (Ngai & Jones 2009):**
One of the clearest direct demonstrations of the dynamic acupoint
phenomenon at any extra point. Skin impedance at Dingchuan is
significantly altered in asthma patients (29.4±21 kΩ vs healthy
13.8±7.9 kΩ), AND the impedance correlates with objective disease
severity (FEV1). Triple validation:
1. Disease-state specificity: impedance changes with respiratory disease
2. Magnitude correlation: worse disease → more altered impedance
3. Location specificity: measured at the specific acupoint associated
   with the respiratory condition

Direction of change: impedance INCREASES in asthma. Higher resistance
to current flow in disease may reflect sympathetic dysfunction
(vasoconstriction → decreased tissue hydration), neurogenic
inflammatory changes in connective tissue, or altered local ion
channel activity at the C8-T1 dermatome.

This finding supports Dingchuan as an acupoint that literally BECOMES
MORE ELECTRICALLY DISTINCT as asthma worsens — consistent with the
dynamic acupoint phenomenon (C04 §4) but measured by an objective
electrophysiological parameter with disease severity correlation.
→ Cross-ref: C04 S4

#### An toàn

**Pleural dome / pneumothorax:** the lung apex extends to about 2.5 cm
above the first rib. At 0.5 cun lateral to C7-T1, the pleural dome is
lateral and anterior to the needle trajectory. At standard depth (0.5-
0.8 cun ≈ 12-20 mm) with perpendicular or slightly medial angulation,
pneumothorax risk is LOW. Do NOT angle needles laterally or deeply at
this level — always direct perpendicular to skin or slightly toward
the spine.

**Spinal cord:** the cervical spinal cord is directly deep at midline.
At 0.5 cun lateral, the lamina provides a bony barrier. Do NOT angle
needles medially toward the spinal canal.

**Vertebral artery:** runs through the transverse foramina of C6-C1,
ANTERIOR to the transverse processes. At 0.5 cun lateral to the
spinous process, the needle trajectory is posterior — vertebral artery
is not at risk at standard depths with perpendicular insertion.

**Pregnancy:** NOT specifically contraindicated.

### EX-B2 — Giáp Tích / Hoa Đà Giáp Tích (夹脊 / 华佗夹脊, Jiājǐ / Huátuó Jiājǐ)

**Evidence tier: A (Deep Analysis)**

"Clamped Spine / Hua Tuo's Clamped Spine" (夹 = clamp/pinch + 脊 =
spine). The world's largest standardized acupoint group — 17 bilateral
pairs (34 individual points) spanning the entire thoracolumbar spine
from T1 to L5, each 0.5 cun (~8-10 mm) lateral to the lower border of
the spinous process. Named after Hua Tuo (华佗, ~145-208 CE), the
legendary Eastern Han dynasty physician who pioneered systematic
paravertebral needling.

The Jiaji system represents the most DIRECT spinal nerve access points
in the entire acupuncture system — positioned at the dorsal ramus trunk
or proximal medial branch in the laminar groove, closer to the spinal
nerve root than any Back-Shu or Bladder channel point. Each pair
accesses its corresponding segment's nerve root, creating a segmentally-
specific neuromodulation system spanning the entire thoracolumbar
sympathetic outflow (T1-L2) and upper lumbar somatic territory (L3-L5).

Together with the Back-Shu points (1.5 cun) and outer Bladder line
(3.0 cun), the Jiaji system completes a three-line paravertebral
architecture organized by dorsal ramus branching: trunk/proximal
(0.5 cun) → medial branch (1.5 cun) → lateral branch (3.0 cun).
The system's anatomical logic converges with Western pain medicine's
medial branch blocks and radiofrequency ablation — different modalities
targeting the SAME neural structure at the SAME anatomical position.

Most evidence-rich multi-point extra point system, with systematic
reviews for lumbar disc herniation (30 RCTs, n=3,503), herpes zoster
(19 RCTs), postherpetic neuralgia (13 trials, n=858), spinal cord
injury (22 RCTs, n=1,644), and ankylosing spondylitis (21 RCTs,
n=1,884).

#### Lịch sử và danh pháp (History and Nomenclature)

**Hua Tuo (华佗, ~145-208 CE):** Eastern Han dynasty physician,
contemporary of Zhang Zhongjing (张仲景, 150-219 CE). Documented in
the Hou Han Shu (后汉书, Book of the Later Han, compiled 5th century
by Fan Ye) and San Guo Zhi (三国志, Records of the Three Kingdoms, 3rd
century by Chen Shou). Known for pioneering surgical anesthesia using
Mafeisan (麻沸散) and creating the Five Animal Frolics (五禽戏,
Wuqinxi) therapeutic exercise system. Executed by Cao Cao (曹操) around
208 CE; his primary medical text, the Qing Nang Shu (青囊书, Book of
the Green Bag), was lost — an irreplaceable loss to Chinese medical
history.

**Textual history:** the Hou Han Shu biography describes Hua Tuo's
practice of needling "one or two points" (针灸不过数处) along the
spine — a proto-reference to segmental paravertebral needling but
without formal naming or systematic cataloging. The points themselves
are NOT named "Jiaji" in Hua Tuo's era; the designation developed
through centuries of subsequent attribution.

Key textual milestones:
- **Hou Han Shu** (~5th century): Hua Tuo's spinal needling practice
  described but no formal point system named
- **Bei Ji Qian Jin Yao Fang** (Sun Simiao, 652 CE): references Hua
  Tuo's teachings, includes paravertebral needling protocols
- **Ishizaka Sotetsu** (石坂宗哲, Edo period Japan): systematized the
  bilateral paravertebral point set with segmental specificity
- **Cheng Dan'an** (承淡安, 20th century): introduced the modern
  designation "Huatuo Jiaji Points" (华佗夹脊穴) as formalized extra
  points with anatomical descriptions
- **WHO-WPRO** (1991): standardized as EX-B2, 17 pairs from T1 to L5

**Naming:** 夹脊 (Jiaji) = "clamped spine" or "flanking the spine" —
describes the bilateral position clamping the spinous processes. The
prefix 华佗 (Huatuo) is an honorific attribution to the legendary
physician, not a claim of discovery.

#### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): 17 bilateral pairs (34 individual
points), each 0.5 cun (~8-10 mm) lateral to the lower border of the
spinous process, from T1 to L5.

Anatomical landmarks: identify each spinous process by palpation or
surface landmark (C7 vertebra prominens → count down for thoracic;
iliac crest → L4 for lumbar). The 0.5 cun measurement places the
point in the laminar groove — the paravertebral sulcus between the
spinous process and the articular pillar/transverse process.

**Segmental map:**

| Region | Levels | Pairs | Key organ viscerotomes |
|--------|--------|-------|----------------------|
| Upper thoracic | T1-T4 | 4 | Lung (T2-T4), heart/pericardium (T1-T5), upper limb sympathetic |
| Mid-thoracic | T5-T8 | 4 | Heart (T5), diaphragm (T7), hepatobiliary transition (T8) |
| Lower thoracic | T9-T12 | 4 | Liver (T9), gallbladder (T10), spleen (T11), stomach (T12) |
| Lumbar | L1-L5 | 5 | Kidney (L1-L2), large intestine (L4), urogenital, lower limb |
| **Total** | **T1-L5** | **17** | **34 individual points** |

Spatial relationships:
- GV (midline): Jiaji is 0.5 cun lateral to GV points at the same level
- BL inner line (1.5 cun): Back-Shu points are 1.0 cun further lateral
- BL outer line (3.0 cun): 2.5 cun further lateral than Jiaji
- EX-B1 Dingchuan: at C7 level, 0.5 cun lateral — occupies the
  "cervicothoracic Jiaji position" one segment above T1. Separately
  named for its specific respiratory function; NOT part of the
  WHO-standardized Jiaji series (which begins at T1).

**Note on cervical extension:** some modern clinical protocols extend
Jiaji above T1 to include C4-C7 or even C1-C7 for cervical conditions.
WHO standardization (T1-L5) excludes the cervical spine. This entry
documents the WHO standard range only.

Needle depth varies by region:
- **Thoracic (T1-T12):** 15-25 mm (0.5-1.0 cun), perpendicular or
  slightly oblique toward the spine. Shallower in upper thoracic
  (T1-T4: 15-20 mm), deeper in lower thoracic (T9-T12: 20-25 mm).
- **Lumbar (L1-L5):** 25-40 mm (1.0-1.5 cun), perpendicular. Thicker
  musculature allows deeper access. Some protocols use up to 50 mm at
  L4-L5.

#### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm (Histological Structure at Point)

**Neural architecture — the three-position dorsal ramus gradient:**

The dorsal ramus of each spinal nerve exits the intervertebral foramen
and divides into a medial branch and a lateral branch. The position of
the needle along this branching pattern defines three paravertebral
lines of acupuncture:

| Distance | System | Nerve access | Target tissue | Clinical function |
|----------|--------|-------------|---------------|-------------------|
| **0.5 cun** | **Jiaji (EX-B2)** | Dorsal ramus trunk / proximal medial branch | Rotatores, deep multifidus | Most direct segmental access |
| **1.5 cun** | **Back-Shu (BL inner)** | Medial branch (further along course) | Multifidus, facet joints | Visceral (organ-specific) |
| **3.0 cun** | **Outer BL line** | Lateral branch | Longissimus, iliocostalis | Musculoskeletal / constitutional |

At 0.5 cun lateral, the needle accesses the dorsal ramus TRUNK or its
proximal medial branch in the laminar groove — before the nerve has
branched significantly. This is the most PROXIMAL access point to the
segmental spinal nerve in any acupuncture system. Cadaver studies
(Shuang et al. 2015, Medicine, 12 cadavers 24 sides) mapped the medial
branch running through a fibro-osseous canal bounded by the accessory
process, mammillary process, and mammilloaccessory ligament. Distance
from dorsal ramus bifurcation to transverse process root: 3.4-3.6 mm
at lumbar levels. Depth from body surface: 3.1-4.6 cm (lumbar region).

**Critical anatomical clarification:** both Jiaji (0.5 cun) AND
Back-Shu (1.5 cun) access the MEDIAL branch system of the dorsal
ramus. The LATERAL branch is accessed only at 3.0 cun (outer BL line).
The Jiaji-Back-Shu difference is PROXIMITY along the medial branch:
Jiaji intercepts the nerve more proximally (closer to the trunk/root),
while Back-Shu accesses the medial branch further along its course at
the level of multifidus and facet joints.
→ Cross-ref: AP-07 Bladder (structural summary table, mechanistic
interpretation — medial branch at 1.5 cun, lateral branch at 3.0 cun)

Spinal segments: T1 through L5 (17 consecutive segments).

Vascular: segmental posterior intercostal arteries (thoracic), lumbar
arteries (lumbar) — dorsal branches paralleling the dorsal rami.

Muscle layers (superficial → deep, thoracic levels):
- Skin → subcutaneous tissue
- Trapezius (upper-mid thoracic) / latissimus dorsi (lower thoracic)
- Rhomboids (T2-T5) or serratus posterior (T2-T5 superior, T11-L2
  inferior)
- Erector spinae (longissimus, iliocostalis) — crossed at depth
- Semispinalis thoracis
- **Multifidus** (deepest spanning layer at all levels)
- **Rotatores brevis and longus** (deepest — on lamina, the primary
  needle target at 0.5 cun)
→ Cross-ref: A01 S2.3, A03 S2.2

**Motor point overlap:** Jiaji points represent motor points of the
rotatores and multifidus muscles, all innervated by the medial branch
of the dorsal ramus (Callison 2019, Sports Medicine Acupuncture). This
motor point correspondence explains why needling at Jiaji positions
reliably produces local muscle twitch responses — the same phenomenon
targeted in dry needling.
→ Cross-ref: B11

#### Phân loại huyệt (Point Category Membership)

Extra point (经外奇穴): EX-B2 in WHO nomenclature. Not on any meridian.

Five Shu: not applicable. Yuan-Source: not applicable.
Luo-Connecting: not applicable. Xi-Cleft: not applicable.
Back-Shu: not applicable. Front-Mu: not applicable.
Eight Confluent: not applicable. Hui-Meeting: not applicable.

Special designations:
- **Largest multi-point group in the Atlas:** 34 individual points (17
  bilateral pairs) — exceeding Shixuan (10), Qiduan (10), Baxie (8),
  and Bafeng (8). The Jiaji system contains more individual points
  than many complete meridians (Heart = 9, Pericardium = 9, Liver = 14).
- **Only segmentally-organized extra point system:** each pair
  corresponds to a specific spinal segment. No other extra point group
  has this segmental specificity.
- **Parallel system to Back-Shu:** 17 Jiaji pairs run alongside the
  inner BL line points (BL11-BL26 at T1-L5). Of these, 10 are organ
  Back-Shu points → each Jiaji pair at those levels accesses the same
  segment as the corresponding organ's Back-Shu.
→ Cross-ref: B01 S1.3.3, AP-07 Bladder (Back-Shu)

#### Cross-tradition mapping

**1. TCM acupoint:** EX-B2 Jiaji / Huatuo Jiaji (华佗夹脊). Functions:
regulate the corresponding organ segment (segmental organ regulation),
unblock the meridians and collaterals (通经活络), relieve pain,
regulate Qi and Blood. The Jiaji system's TCM function follows
segmental logic — T3 Jiaji for lung, T5 for heart, T9 for liver —
mirroring Back-Shu but from a more medial position. These segmental
associations exist independently of meridian theory: Jiaji points are
NOT on any meridian, yet their organ associations follow the same
segmental pattern as the Back-Shu points that ARE on the Bladder
meridian. ✓

**2. Trigger point:** multifidus and rotatores trigger points are
extensively documented at paravertebral locations at all spinal levels
(Travell & Simons, Myofascial Pain and Dysfunction Vol. 2). Multifidus
trigger points cause deep paraspinal aching and segmental stiffness.
The Jiaji positions correspond to the deepest paraspinal trigger
points — the same muscles (rotatores brevis/longus, multifidus) that
are the primary motor targets at 0.5 cun lateral. The correspondence
is STRUCTURAL — the needle accesses the same muscle belly and motor
point. ✓
→ Cross-ref: B11

**3. Ashi correspondence:** paravertebral tender points are among the
most universally recognized palpation findings across ALL manual
medicine traditions. Physical examination of the paravertebral muscles
for segmental tenderness is standard in Western medicine (segmental
facilitation), osteopathy (somatic dysfunction), chiropractic
(subluxation complex), and TCM (Ashi points). The Jiaji positions
represent the most commonly tender paravertebral sites — deep
paraspinal tenderness at the laminar groove. ✓

**4. Marma point:** Prishtha Marmas (back Marmas) in both Ayurvedic
(107-point system) and Siddha Varmam (1,008-point system) include
paravertebral vital points along the thoracic and lumbar spine.
Brihati Marma at mid-thoracic levels, Kateekataruna Marma at the low
back, and Kukundara/Nitamba at the lumbopelvic zone. These correspond
to zones within the Jiaji corridor but with less segmental precision —
Marma/Varmam therapy identifies REGIONS rather than individual segmental
pairs. Case series for cervical spondylosis and lumbar conditions show
clinical benefit from Siddha Varmam therapy targeting paravertebral
zones. 🟡

**5. Head zone:** each Jiaji pair sits at its OWN dermatome — the
strongest possible Head zone match. The Jiaji system IS a dermatome-
viscerotome map expressed as acupuncture points. Every level from T1
to L5 has a defined dermatome (Beissner & Henke 2011), and each pair
accesses its corresponding dermatome's deep tissue. The Head zone
concept (cutaneous-visceral reflex through shared segmental innervation)
is the exact mechanism by which Jiaji achieves organ effects. ✓
→ Cross-ref: A07 S2.1

**6. Anatomy Trains:** the Superficial Back Line runs through erector
spinae, traversing the entire paravertebral corridor. However, this
correspondence is generic for ALL posterior spinal points — the
Superficial Back Line does not discriminate between individual Jiaji
levels or between Jiaji and Back-Shu positions. ✗

**7. Neurogenic inflammatory spot:** the neurogenic inflammation model
(Kim 2017) predicts that organ disease produces measurable changes at
segmentally-linked skin sites. Paravertebral zones at organ-specific
segments (T3 for lung, T5 for heart, T9 for liver) are expected
locations for neurogenic inflammatory spots during organ disease. The
dynamic acupoint phenomenon documented at Dingchuan (EX-B1, C8-T1,
impedance change in asthma, Ngai 2009) demonstrates this principle at
the cervicothoracic level; the same mechanism applies across the entire
Jiaji system for their respective organ segments. 🟡
→ Cross-ref: C04 S4

**8. Cupping point:** the paravertebral corridor from upper thoracic
to lumbar is one of the most extensively used cupping zones in BOTH
TCM and Hijama (Islamic cupping) traditions. Sliding cupping (走罐)
along the Bladder channel and paravertebral muscles is standard for
back pain, respiratory conditions, and general wellness. Multiple cups
placed bilaterally along the spine cover the Jiaji corridor. ✓

**Convergence score: 7/8** (TCM ✓, Trigger point ✓, Ashi ✓, Marma 🟡,
Head zone ✓, Neurogenic 🟡, Cupping ✓; Anatomy Trains ✗). VERY HIGH —
the paravertebral corridor is one of the most universally recognized
therapeutic zones across all manual medicine traditions. Five independent
systems (TCM, trigger points, Ashi palpation, Head zones, cupping
traditions) converge on the same anatomical territory. The Jiaji
system's segmental organization mirrors the Head zone concept so
precisely that the two are essentially the same map expressed in
different terminology.

**Cross-discipline convergence (not part of 8-tradition score):**
- **Chiropractic meric system:** maps vertebral levels to specific
  organs for spinal manipulation — same segmental logic as Jiaji organ
  associations, developed independently in 19th-century Western manual
  medicine. Hua Tuo's system has been described as "remarkably similar
  to chiropractic, but 2,000 years prior" (Dynamic Chiropractic).
- **Medial branch blocks / radiofrequency ablation:** Western pain
  medicine targets the SAME medial branch of the dorsal ramus at the
  SAME anatomical position for facet-mediated pain (Lin et al. 2010,
  Acupuncture & Electrotherapeutics Research — pulsed radiofrequency
  vs EA comparison at the same targets). Different modality (chemical
  blockade / thermal ablation vs needle stimulation), identical neural
  target. RAPID study: radiofrequency ablation responder rates up to
  79%, durable pain relief at 24 months.
- **Dry needling of paraspinal muscles:** targets the same rotatores/
  multifidus motor points, eliciting local twitch responses. Electrical
  dry needling potentiates opiate pathways via the same dorsal rami.
→ Cross-ref: B11

#### Chỉ định truyền thống (DATA — ghi nhận, không endorse as mechanism)

Nguồn cổ:
- Hua Tuo's practice: needling "one or two points" along the spine for
  various organ complaints (Hou Han Shu biography). The clinical
  observation that needling specific vertebral levels affects
  corresponding organs — a pattern later codified as the Jiaji system.
- Bei Ji Qian Jin Yao Fang (Sun Simiao, 652 CE): paravertebral
  needling for organ-specific disorders following the segmental
  principle.

Segmental indications (traditional):
- **T1-T4 (upper thoracic):** respiratory disorders (cough, asthma,
  dyspnea), cardiac conditions, upper limb disorders
- **T5-T8 (mid-thoracic):** chest pain, intercostal neuralgia, upper
  abdominal disorders, diaphragmatic hiccup
- **T9-T12 (lower thoracic):** hepatobiliary disorders, gastric
  conditions, digestive complaints, abdominal pain
- **L1-L5 (lumbar):** lower back pain, kidney disorders, urogenital
  conditions, lower limb disorders, sciatica

Modern textbook (Deadman, WHO):
- Primary: disorders of the corresponding vertebral segment — spinal
  pain, radiculopathy, segmental organ dysfunction
- Secondary: back stiffness, muscle spasm, intercostal neuralgia
- System-wide: chronic pain conditions affecting the spine

Classical combinations:
- **Jiaji + corresponding Back-Shu:** dual-depth approach combining
  0.5 cun and 1.5 cun access for reinforced segmental stimulation
- **Dermatome-matching Jiaji (herpes zoster):** needling at the
  dermatome corresponding to the rash + flanking segments above and
  below — the "surround and suppress" (围刺) strategy
- **Segmental Jiaji + distal points:** T5 Jiaji + PC6 for cardiac
  conditions; L4-L5 Jiaji + BL40 for lumbar pain

#### Bằng chứng hiện đại

**Lumbar disc herniation — strongest indication:**
Tang et al. (2018, Acupuncture in Medicine): systematic review and
meta-analysis of **30 RCTs, n=3,503**. Acupuncture with Jiaji among
most commonly used points showed higher total effective rate than
lumbar traction, ibuprofen, diclofenac, and meloxicam. A dedicated
review (Journal of Acupuncture and Tuina Science, 2015) concluded
that lumbar disc herniation is the MOST FAVORABLE indication for Jiaji
points. Feng (2026, SAGE): retrospective analysis of **106 patients**,
EA at Jiaji + physical therapy vs physical therapy alone — VAS
reduction MD = -1.84 at Week 4 (95% CI: -2.34 to -1.34, p<0.001),
sustained through Month 3 (MD = -2.41, 95% CI: -2.93 to -1.89).
🟡 Plausible (multiple systematic reviews positive, but Jiaji rarely
tested in isolation — usually combined with Bladder meridian points)

**Herpes zoster and postherpetic neuralgia:**
Zhou et al. (2025, Medicine): systematic review of **19 RCTs** for
herpes zoster. Jiaji as primary treatment acupoint. VAS pain reduction
SMD = -2.55; postherpetic neuralgia incidence dramatically decreased
(OR = 0.11); total effective rate OR = 4.25. CD4 count significantly
elevated (SMD 2.81), suggesting immunomodulatory effect. Trial
sequential analysis confirmed sufficient evidence. Zhu et al. (2011):
meta-analysis of **13 trials, n=858** for postherpetic neuralgia.
Jiaji + surrounding needling: total effective rate RR = 1.26 (95% CI:
1.19-1.34), clinical cure rate RR = 1.93, VAS WMD = 3.13.
🟡 Plausible (consistent positive findings, strong effect sizes;
segmental Jiaji selection at affected dermatome provides the clearest
segmental specificity signal in the evidence base)

**Spinal cord injury:**
Xiong et al. (2019, Evidence-Based Complementary and Alternative
Medicine): network meta-analysis of **22 RCTs, n=1,644**. Back-region
acupuncture (GV + Jiaji + BL meridian) ranked first for motor function
recovery (SMD = 21; 95% CrI: 11-33) and daily living improvement.
Safety: only 5.4% mild adverse effects.
🟡 Plausible (largest network meta-analysis for spinal cord injury
acupuncture; Jiaji combined with GV and BL, individual contribution
unclear)

**Ankylosing spondylitis:**
Zhang et al. (2025, SAGE): updated systematic review of **21 RCTs,
n=1,884**. Huatuojiaji as primary acupoints in warm needle protocols.
PEDro score ≥6 inclusion criterion. Positive direction for pain and
functional outcomes.
🟡 Plausible (large evidence base, positive direction, quality
limitations)

**Cardiac protection — preclinical evidence:**
Xiao et al. (2020, Evidence-Based Complementary and Alternative
Medicine): **120 Wistar rats**. EA preconditioning at **T4-T5 Jiaji**
significantly reduced cardiac tissue damage from ischemia-reperfusion
injury, matching the protective effects of PC6 (Neiguan). Mechanisms:
steroid hormone biosynthesis, anti-inflammatory pathways, GABA-mediated
neurohumoral regulation. T4-T5 = cardiac sympathetic outflow segments,
demonstrating segmental specificity: cardiac protection from
cardiac-segment Jiaji, consistent with the somatovisceral reflex model.
🔴 Unvalidated (preclinical only, but provides the most direct
mechanistic validation of Jiaji segmental specificity)

**Overall assessment:** Jiaji has the broadest evidence base of any
extra point system, with systematic reviews across at least 4 major
conditions. The evidence pattern is consistent: positive effect
direction, large effect sizes, but limited point-specific isolation
(Jiaji almost always combined with other acupoints). The strongest
point-specific evidence comes from the herpes zoster literature,
where dermatome-matched Jiaji selection provides a clear segmental
specificity signal. The cardiac protection study (Xiao 2020) provides
the most direct mechanistic validation — T4-T5 Jiaji protecting the
heart, matching PC6 — confirming that the LEVEL determines which
organ is affected.

#### So sánh ba đường cạnh cột sống (Three Paravertebral Lines Comparison)

The Jiaji system completes a three-line paravertebral architecture
spanning 2,500 years of acupuncture practice. Together with the
Back-Shu points (documented in the Neijing, ~2nd century BCE) and
the outer BL line (formalized in later classical texts), these three
concentric lines represent three distinct approaches to the same
spinal segments:

| Property | Jiaji (0.5 cun) | Back-Shu (1.5 cun) | Outer BL (3.0 cun) |
|----------|----------------|--------------------|--------------------|
| **System** | EX-B2 (extra point) | BL inner line | BL outer line |
| **Dorsal ramus access** | Trunk / proximal medial branch | Medial branch | Lateral branch |
| **Target muscle** | Rotatores, deep multifidus | Multifidus, facet joints | Longissimus, iliocostalis |
| **Clinical profile** | Direct segmental nerve access | Visceral (organ-specific) | Musculoskeletal / constitutional |
| **Organ specificity** | By segment (empirical) | By designation (12 organ Back-Shu) | None (spirit names at Zang levels) |
| **Sympathetic proximity** | Closest | Moderate | Most distant |
| **Evidence volume** | Moderate (SRs for pain/zoster) | High (individual points studied) | Low (mostly adjunctive) |
| **Clinical usage** | Moderate-high | Highest | Low |
| **Named points (T1-L5)** | 34 (17 pairs) | 16 (BL11-BL26) | 12 (BL41-BL52, T2-L2) |

**Functional gradient hypothesis:** the three concentric lines may
represent a gradient from most-specific-segmental (0.5 cun) to
most-diffuse-constitutional (3.0 cun). Jiaji accesses the nerve root
most directly → strongest segmental signal. Back-Shu accesses the
medial branch at the facet/muscle level → strong somatovisceral reflex
(12 organ designations). Outer BL accesses the lateral branch in the
long paraspinal muscles → broader, more constitutional effects (spirit
names, general tonification). This gradient parallels neural
architecture: proximal nerve stimulation produces more focused effects
than distal branch stimulation. (Cabioglu & Arslan 2008, American
Journal of Chinese Medicine — neurophysiologic basis comparison)
→ Cross-ref: AP-07 Bladder (structural summary table, mechanistic
interpretation)

**Jiaji–Back-Shu segmental correspondence:**

| Level | Jiaji pair | BL inner line point | Organ Back-Shu |
|-------|-----------|--------------------|----|
| T1 | EX-B2 T1 | BL11 Đại Trữ (Dashu) | — (Hui-Meeting of Bones) |
| T2 | EX-B2 T2 | BL12 Phong Môn (Fengmen) | — |
| T3 | EX-B2 T3 | **BL13 Phế Du (Feishu)** | **Lung** |
| T4 | EX-B2 T4 | **BL14 Quyết Âm Du (Jueyinshu)** | **Pericardium** |
| T5 | EX-B2 T5 | **BL15 Tâm Du (Xinshu)** | **Heart** |
| T6 | EX-B2 T6 | BL16 Đốc Du (Dushu) | — |
| T7 | EX-B2 T7 | BL17 Cách Du (Geshu) | — (Hui-Meeting of Blood) |
| T8 | EX-B2 T8 | *[T8 gap — no BL point]* | — |
| T9 | EX-B2 T9 | **BL18 Can Du (Ganshu)** | **Liver** |
| T10 | EX-B2 T10 | **BL19 Đởm Du (Danshu)** | **Gallbladder** |
| T11 | EX-B2 T11 | **BL20 Tỳ Du (Pishu)** | **Spleen** |
| T12 | EX-B2 T12 | **BL21 Vị Du (Weishu)** | **Stomach** |
| L1 | EX-B2 L1 | **BL22 Tam Tiêu Du (Sanjiaoshu)** | **Triple Burner** |
| L2 | EX-B2 L2 | **BL23 Thận Du (Shenshu)** | **Kidney** |
| L3 | EX-B2 L3 | BL24 Khí Hải Du (Qihaishu) | — |
| L4 | EX-B2 L4 | **BL25 Đại Trường Du (Dachangshu)** | **Large Intestine** |
| L5 | EX-B2 L5 | BL26 Quan Nguyên Du (Guanyuanshu) | — |

10 of the 12 organ Back-Shu points fall within the Jiaji range. The
2 sacral Back-Shu (BL27 Small Intestine at S1, BL28 Bladder at S2)
are below the Jiaji system's T1-L5 range. Note the T8 gap: the only
thoracic level without a BL inner line point, also the only Jiaji level
without a BL counterpart.

#### Cơ chế giải thích

**Direct segmental spinal nerve access:**
The primary mechanism is the somatovisceral reflex (Sato 1997):
skin/muscle stimulus at segment X → dorsal ramus afferents → dorsal
horn → convergent wide-dynamic-range neurons (lamina V) → preganglionic
sympathetic neurons → organ in segment X. This is the SAME mechanism
documented for Back-Shu points but accessed at a more proximal point
on the dorsal ramus.

**Proximal access advantage:**
At 0.5 cun lateral, the needle accesses the dorsal ramus closer to
its trunk — before significant branching. This more proximal access
point may stimulate a greater proportion of the total afferent fiber
population, creating a stronger gate-control effect (A01) and a more
robust somatovisceral reflex. Back-Shu at 1.5 cun accesses the medial
branch further along its course, where fibers have already branched
to target specific structures (facet joints, muscle fascicles).

**Segmental specificity validation:**
The cardiac protection study (Xiao et al. 2020) demonstrates that EA
at T4-T5 Jiaji — the cardiac sympathetic outflow segments — produces
cardiac-specific protective effects equivalent to PC6. This is the
most direct evidence that Jiaji's organ effects are mediated by
segmental specificity: the LEVEL determines which organ is affected,
because each level accesses a specific sympathetic outflow territory.
→ Cross-ref: A01 S3.4, A02 S3.5, A07 S1.6

**Sympathetic chain proximity:**
The sympathetic chain lies ANTERIOR to the transverse processes. The
Jiaji position at 0.5 cun lateral, directly on the lamina, is the
closest posterior approach to the sympathetic chain in any acupuncture
system. Although the needle does not directly contact the chain, dorsal
ramus afferents converge with sympathetic neurons at the spinal cord
level — the somatovisceral reflex. The more proximal the dorsal ramus
access, the stronger the convergent signal.

**Confidence:** 🟡 Synthesis. The somatovisceral reflex mechanism is
established (Sato 1997), segmental specificity is validated by Xiao
2020 cardiac protection. The specific advantage of 0.5 cun over
1.5 cun for the somatovisceral reflex has not been directly compared.

#### Kỹ thuật kích thích

**Needling — regional depth and angle:**
- **Thoracic (T1-T12):** perpendicular, 15-25 mm. NEVER angle laterally
  (pneumothorax risk). Slightly toward the spinous process (medial
  oblique) is safe — the lamina provides a bony backstop.
- **Lumbar (L1-L5):** perpendicular, 25-40 mm. Angle slightly medial
  to target the laminar groove. Deeper permitted due to thicker
  musculature.
- De Qi: deep aching, local heaviness, segmental muscle twitch response
  (confirms multifidus/rotatores engagement).

**Electroacupuncture (dominant modern technique):**
- **Herpes zoster protocol:** EA at Jiaji pairs corresponding to the
  affected dermatome, 2-100 Hz (dense-disperse wave), 30 minutes.
  Bilateral at 2-3 segmental levels.
- **Lumbar disc herniation:** EA at L4-L5 Jiaji (or affected level),
  2-15 Hz, paired bilaterally. Combined with BL40, GB34.
- **Ankylosing spondylitis:** warm needle acupuncture at multiple Jiaji
  levels + BL points (needle + moxa on handle).

**Moxibustion:** indirect moxa for chronic conditions, especially Yang
deficiency patterns. Warm needling (needle + moxa on handle) is the
most common combined technique at Jiaji.

**Cupping:** sliding cupping (走罐) along the paravertebral corridor
covers the Jiaji zone. Individual cups at specific levels for
segmental conditions.

#### Dynamic acupoint properties

**Segmental sensitization principle:**
Although no study has directly measured skin impedance or temperature
at individual Jiaji points during organ disease (as documented at
Dingchuan for asthma, Ngai 2009), the Jiaji system is predicted to
exhibit dynamic acupoint properties based on established
neurophysiology:

1. **Somatovisceral reflex sensitization:** organ disease at segment X →
   facilitated segment → lowered threshold at corresponding Jiaji pair
   → palpable tenderness / altered electrical properties. Same mechanism
   as diagnostic tender points at EX-LE6 Dannangxue and EX-LE7
   Lanweixue.
2. **Clinical validation:** palpation for segmental paraspinal tenderness
   is standard in osteopathic, chiropractic, and Western medical
   examination — all recognizing that organ disease creates detectable
   changes at corresponding paravertebral levels.
3. **Herpes zoster as natural experiment:** the rash follows a specific
   dermatome, and Jiaji needling at the corresponding segment (not
   adjacent segments) is most effective — direct evidence that SEGMENTAL
   SPECIFICITY determines therapeutic relevance.
→ Cross-ref: C04 S4

#### An toàn

**Pneumothorax (thoracic levels — CRITICAL):**
The principal safety concern for thoracic Jiaji (T1-T12). At 0.5 cun
lateral, the needle is in the laminar groove — the lamina provides a
BONY BARRIER between the needle and the pleural cavity. However, the
lamina narrows at certain levels, and angulation errors can cause the
needle to slip past the bone.
- **Risk mitigation:** perpendicular or slightly medial toward the
  spinous process. NEVER angle laterally or deeply.
- **Highest risk levels:** T1-T3 (narrow laminae, proximity to pleural
  apex) and T10-T12 (narrow laminae, less muscle coverage).
- **Depth limit:** do NOT exceed 30 mm at thoracic levels without
  imaging guidance.
- **Safety data:** Xiong et al. 2019 (22 RCTs, n=1,644) reported only
  5.4% mild adverse effects across all Jiaji-inclusive protocols.

**Spinal cord:**
The lamina is between the needle and the spinal canal. Spinal cord risk
is LOWER than midline GV needling (which approaches the interspinous
space). Do NOT angle needles medially from the Jiaji position toward
the midline.
- **Cervical extension caution:** clinical protocols extending Jiaji
  above T1 to cervical levels face higher spinal cord risk (cervical
  cord larger relative to canal). The WHO limitation to T1-L5 partly
  reflects this safety consideration.

**Lumbar safety:**
Lumbar Jiaji (L1-L5) are the SAFEST levels: no pneumothorax risk
(below the lungs), and the spinal cord ends at L1-L2 (conus
medullaris). L3-L5 Jiaji face only cauda equina nerve roots, not the
cord. Deeper insertion permitted (25-50 mm).

**Vascular:**
Segmental arteries run along the inferior border of each rib/transverse
process. Monitor for hematoma, especially in patients on
anticoagulants.

**Pregnancy:** NOT specifically contraindicated. Avoid aggressive
stimulation at lumbosacral levels (L4-L5) in early pregnancy.

---

*Phase 2 Batch 5 COMPLETE. 2 entries added: EX-B1 (Dingchuan, Tier B,
6/8 convergence — premier respiratory extra point, 58% of 183 asthma
studies, stellate ganglion sympathetic gateway, skin impedance dynamic
acupoint evidence), EX-B2 (Huatuojiaji, Tier A, 7/8 convergence —
34-point segmental spinal system T1-L5, three-position dorsal ramus
gradient established, largest multi-point group in Atlas, systematic
reviews for LDH 30 RCTs n=3,503 + herpes zoster 19 RCTs + SCI 22
RCTs n=1,644 + AS 21 RCTs n=1,884, cardiac protection T4-T5=PC6
equivalence Xiao 2020, cross-discipline convergence with medial branch
blocks/RFA/dry needling). **EX-B REGION 2/8.** Total entries in
AP-15: 17/48 (including Phase 1 Yintang). No new ⚡ framework notes
(running total: 41). Key findings: (1) three-position dorsal ramus
gradient (0.5 cun trunk → 1.5 cun medial branch → 3.0 cun lateral
branch) completes the paravertebral architecture with the BL file's
medial-outer pairing; (2) critical correction — both Jiaji and
Back-Shu access the medial branch, NOT medial vs lateral as sometimes
stated; (3) Jiaji-Back-Shu-outer BL functional gradient from
most-specific-segmental to most-diffuse-constitutional; (4)
cross-discipline convergence of Jiaji, medial branch blocks, RFA,
and dry needling at identical anatomical target is the strongest
multi-modality validation for any acupoint location.*

### EX-B3 — Vị Quản Hạ Du (胃脘下俞, Wèiwǎn Xiàshū)

**Evidence tier: B (Standard Entry)**

"Below the Stomach Controller" (胃脘 = stomach/epigastrium + 下 = below +
俞 = transporting point). Alternative name: Yishu (胰俞, Yíshū,
"Pancreas Transporting Point") — a modern designation reflecting the
anatomical correspondence to the pancreas. Located 1.5 cun lateral to the
lower border of T8 spinous process, bilateral. Architecturally unique
among extra points: occupies the EXACT position of the Back-Shu line
(1.5 cun lateral) at the ONLY thoracic level without a standard BL
inner-line point — the T8 gap between BL17 Geshu (T7, Diaphragm) and
BL18 Ganshu (T9, Liver). T8 corresponds to the pancreatic viscerotome
(greater splanchnic nerve T5-T9), making EX-B3 the functional "Pancreas
Back-Shu" — the Back-Shu that was never assigned because TCM did not
recognize the pancreas as a separate Zang-Fu organ. Primary indication:
diabetes (消渴 Xiao Ke, "wasting thirst"), documented since Sun Simiao
652 CE. The strongest preclinical evidence among all extra points: EA at
EX-B3 upregulates PI3K phosphorylation and promotes GLUT4 membrane
translocation for glucose uptake in skeletal muscle (Cao et al. 2016),
with sham EA showing no effect — establishing acupoint specificity at
the molecular level.

#### Lịch sử và danh pháp (History and Nomenclature)

The point name 胃脘下俞 (Wèiwǎn Xiàshū) literally means "transporting
point below the stomach controller." The name references its position
relative to BL21 Weishu (胃俞, Stomach Back-Shu) at T12 — EX-B3 at T8
is anatomically ABOVE, not below BL21, but the 下 (below) refers to its
classical conceptual placement below the epigastric system (胃脘 wèiwǎn),
not vertical anatomy. The alternative name Yishu (胰俞, "Pancreas Shu")
uses the character 胰 (yí, pancreas), which was not used in classical TCM
texts — the pancreas was subsumed under the Spleen (脾, Pí) organ system.

Textual milestones:
- **Longxian Suzhen Jing (龙衔素针经):** first recorded the point name
  and located it at the 8th thoracic vertebra. Date uncertain (likely
  Song-Yuan era).
- **Sun Simiao, Beiji Qianjin Yaofang (备急千金要方), 652 CE:** earliest
  clear therapeutic prescription — "For diabetes (消渴 Xiao Ke) with dry
  throat, apply 100 moxa cones to each of the three Weiwanxiashu points."
  Establishes the diabetes connection from the Tang dynasty.
- **Dunhuang Moxibustion Chart (敦煌灸法图):** references the point for
  "chest fullness, food reversal, vomiting, abdominal fullness" — broader
  gastrointestinal indications.
- **The Nanjing (Classic of Difficulties):** described "散膏" (sàn gāo,
  "scattered fat"), which modern scholars identify as the pancreas, but
  it was never granted independent Zang-Fu status. This conceptual gap
  is why EX-B3 became an "extra" point rather than a standard Back-Shu.

#### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): 1.5 cun lateral to the lower border
of the spinous process of T8 (eighth thoracic vertebra). Bilateral.

Anatomical landmarks: patient prone, identify T8 spinous process by
counting down from C7 (vertebra prominens) or up from T12 (at the level
of the 12th rib). Measure 1.5 cun (~30-35 mm) lateral from the lower
border of T8 spinous process.

Spatial relationships:
- **BL17 Geshu (T7):** one segment above, same lateral distance (1.5 cun).
  Diaphragm Back-Shu, Hui-Meeting of Blood.
- **BL18 Ganshu (T9):** one segment below, same lateral distance (1.5 cun).
  Liver Back-Shu.
- **T8 gap:** EX-B3 occupies the ONLY gap in the thoracic Back-Shu
  sequence (T1-T12). The BL file documents this gap extensively (AP-07
  Observation 11 L3960: "the ONLY thoracic level without a named BL point
  between T1 and T12"; Observation 26 L9144: gap mirrored on the outer BL
  line — BL46 at T7, no point at T8, BL47 at T9).
- **EX-B2 Jiaji T8:** 0.5 cun lateral to T8 — 1.0 cun medial to EX-B3.
  Jiaji accesses the dorsal ramus trunk/proximal medial branch; EX-B3
  accesses the medial branch further along its course, at the standard
  Back-Shu position.
→ Cross-ref: AP-07 BL17, BL18

Needle depth: 7-15 mm (0.3-0.5 cun), oblique insertion directed medially
toward the spine at approximately 45 degrees. Shallow insertion mandatory
at thoracic levels due to pneumothorax risk.

#### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm (Histological Structure at Point)

Nerve supply:
- **T8 dorsal ramus, medial branch:** primary innervation. At 1.5 cun
  lateral, the needle reaches the medial branch as it courses through
  the multifidus, innervating deep paraspinal muscles and facet joints.
  Same nerve branch as all standard Back-Shu points (AP-07 L2484-2487:
  "innervated by the medial branch of the dorsal ramus of the
  corresponding spinal nerve").
- **T8 ventral ramus (intercostal nerve):** runs along the inferior
  border of the 8th rib. Carries both somatic sensory and visceral
  afferent fibers from the T8 dermatome/viscerotome.
- **Greater splanchnic nerve origin:** T5-T9 preganglionic sympathetic
  fibers form the greater splanchnic nerve, which pierces the diaphragm
  and synapses in the celiac ganglion. From there, postganglionic fibers
  reach the pancreas via the pancreatic plexus. T8 is near the CENTER of
  this range — optimal segmental access to the pancreas.

Spinal segments: T8.

Muscle layers (superficial → deep):
- Skin → subcutaneous tissue
- Trapezius (lower fibers) / latissimus dorsi
- Erector spinae (longissimus thoracis, iliocostalis)
- Multifidus / rotatores (deepest, on lamina)
→ Cross-ref: A01 S2.3, A03 S2.2

Vascular: posterior intercostal artery (dorsal branch), consistent with
all thoracic Back-Shu positions.

#### Phân loại huyệt (Point Category Membership)

Extra point (经外奇穴): EX-B3 in WHO nomenclature. Not on any meridian.

Five Shu: not applicable. Yuan-Source: not applicable.
Luo-Connecting: not applicable. Xi-Cleft: not applicable.
Back-Shu: NOT a standard Back-Shu, but occupies the exact Back-Shu
position (1.5 cun lateral) — functionally the "missing Pancreas
Back-Shu." Front-Mu: not applicable. Eight Confluent: not applicable.
Hui-Meeting: not applicable.

Special designations:
- **Functional Pancreas Back-Shu:** the ONLY extra point that fills a
  specific gap in the standard Back-Shu system. Occupies the Back-Shu
  position at the pancreatic viscerotome level. The T8 gap exists because
  TCM merged the pancreas into the Spleen organ system — a classificatory
  gap, not an anatomical one.
- **Strongest preclinical mechanism evidence among extra points:**
  PI3K/GLUT4 pathway with sham-controlled acupoint specificity (Cao
  et al. 2016).
→ Cross-ref: B01 S1.3.3

#### Cross-tradition mapping

**1. TCM acupoint:** EX-B3 Weiwanxiashu (胃脘下俞, "Below the Stomach
Controller") / Yishu (胰俞, "Pancreas Shu"). Functions: clears Heat,
generates fluids (生津 shēng jīn), benefits the pancreas/spleen.
Primary indication: 消渴 (Xiao Ke, "wasting thirst" = diabetes).
Secondary: gastric pain, vomiting, chest fullness. ✓

**2. Trigger point:** T8 paraspinal trigger points in multifidus and
rotatores are documented (Travell & Simons). These TrPs cause local and
referred pain in the mid-thoracic region — musculoskeletal indications,
not metabolic. The TrP correspondence relates to anatomy (paraspinal
muscles at this location) but not to EX-B3's primary indication
(diabetes). 🟡

**3. Ashi correspondence:** T8 paravertebral tenderness may develop in
pancreatic disease through viscerosomatic reflexes — the same mechanism
underlying Back-Shu sensitization. Pancreatic pain (pancreatitis,
pancreatic cancer) is classically referred to the mid-back at T6-T10.
In chronic pancreatic pathology, the T8 paravertebral area may become a
genuine tender point. For diabetes specifically (chronic metabolic
condition), tenderness is subtler than in pancreatitis. 🟡

**4. Marma point:** no specific Marma at the T8 paravertebral location.
The Prishtha (posterior) Marmas include paravertebral points but are
concentrated at the lumbar and cervical regions. ✗

**5. Head zone:** T8 dermatome corresponds to the pancreatic viscerotome
via the greater splanchnic nerve (T5-T9). The pancreas, as a foregut
derivative, refers pain to T6-T9, with T8 at the center. This is one of
the strongest Head zone matches in the Atlas — the point IS at the
precise dermatome-viscerotome level of its primary target organ. Parallel
to the Back-Shu/Head zone alignment (Beissner & Henke 2011), but for an
organ that lacks a standard Back-Shu. ✓
→ Cross-ref: A07 S2.1

**6. Anatomy Trains:** Superficial Back Line through erector spinae. Too
generic for the specific paravertebral depth. Consistent with EX-B1 and
EX-B2 scoring. ✗

**7. Neurogenic inflammatory spot:** the neurogenic inflammation model
(Kim 2017) predicts organ pathology → measurable changes at segmentally
linked skin sites. For pancreatic disease, neurogenic inflammatory spots
at T8 are predicted but not directly studied. The viscerosomatic reflex
producing mid-back pain in pancreatitis is consistent with this model. 🟡
→ Cross-ref: C04 S4

**8. Cupping point:** the mid-thoracic back (T7-T12) is a common cupping
area in both TCM and Hijama for digestive and abdominal complaints. Cups
at the BL17-BL20 area include the T8 position. Partial — cupping in this
area targets general digestive conditions, not diabetes specifically. 🟡

**Convergence score: 6/8** (TCM ✓, Trigger point 🟡, Ashi 🟡, Head zone
✓, Neurogenic 🟡, Cupping 🟡). HIGH — despite "extra" classification,
EX-B3 shows significant cross-tradition convergence. The Head zone match
is particularly strong: T8 IS the pancreatic viscerotome. The convergence
is lower than EX-B2 Jiaji (7/8) primarily because Marma and Anatomy
Trains have poor coverage of the mid-thoracic paravertebral region.

#### Chỉ định truyền thống (DATA — ghi nhận, không endorse as mechanism)

Nguồn cổ:
- **Sun Simiao (652 CE):** diabetes (消渴 Xiao Ke) with dry throat — 100
  moxa cones. The earliest and most specific prescription.
- **Dunhuang Moxibustion Chart:** chest fullness, food reversal (反胃),
  vomiting, abdominal fullness.

TCM functions: clears Heat (清热), generates fluids (生津), harmonizes
the Middle Jiao (和中焦).

Modern textbook (Deadman, WHO):
- Primary: diabetes mellitus (消渴)
- Secondary: gastric pain, epigastric pain, pancreatitis, vomiting
- Tertiary: intercostal neuralgia (combined with BL17, BL18)

Classical combinations:
- **EX-B3 + BL13 (Feishu) + BL20 (Pishu) + BL23 (Shenshu):** classical
  Xiao Ke protocol addressing upper-middle-lower 消 framework.
- **EX-B3 + BL20 + ST36 + SP6 + BL23 + BL21 + KI3:** core combination
  identified by data mining of acupoint catgut embedding studies for
  pre-diabetes (Frontiers in Public Health 2023).

#### Bằng chứng hiện đại

**Preclinical — PI3K/GLUT4 pathway (LANDMARK):**
Cao et al. 2016 (Evidence-Based Complementary and Alternative Medicine,
PMC5021857): EA at bilateral EX-B3 (2 Hz, 2 mA, 20 min/day, 4 weeks)
in T2DM rats (high-fat diet + STZ). Skeletal muscle results: phospho-
PI3K (Y607) increased 112% (EA 1.25 vs model 0.59, P<0.01); membrane
GLUT4 increased 71% (EA 0.29 vs model 0.17, P<0.01); GLUT4 translocation
ratio increased 75% (0.14 vs 0.08, P<0.01). Total GLUT4 expression was
UNCHANGED — EA promoted translocation of existing GLUT4 to the cell
membrane, mimicking insulin's post-receptor signaling pathway (insulin →
IRS-1 → PI3K → Akt → GLUT4 translocation). Sham EA at non-acupoint
showed NO effect, establishing acupoint specificity. Metabolic outcomes:
OGTT AUC decreased 22% (P<0.01), HOMA-IR decreased 37% (P<0.01),
HOMA-β (beta-cell function) increased 382% (P<0.05).
🔴 Preclinical — well-designed with sham control and acupoint specificity,
but animal study only.

**Preclinical — HPA axis regulation:**
Gao et al. 2014 (Zhongguo Zhen Jiu, PMID 25675573): EA at EX-B3 vs EA
at BL20 (Pishu) vs EA at BL23 (Shenshu) vs medication in T2DM rats.
EX-B3 reduced CRH and cortisol more than BL20 and BL23, with higher
insulin sensitivity index than the medication group. EX-B3 was SUPERIOR
to both standard Back-Shu points for HPA-specific outcomes — supporting
the pancreas-specific T8 level vs the spleen-composite T11 level.
🔴 Preclinical

**Preclinical — GLP-1 receptor and islet morphology:**
Cao et al. 2017 (Zhen Ci Yan Jiu, PMID 29071956): EA at EX-B3 restored
pancreatic islet morphology, increased beta-cell nuclei count, alleviated
compensatory beta-cell enlargement, and increased pancreatic GLP-1R
expression in T2DM rats.
🔴 Preclinical

**Preclinical — gut microbiota:**
Zhang et al. 2025 (Frontiers in Microbiology, PMC11743489): EA at
EX-B3 + ST36 + SP6 (combination) modified gut microbiota (increased
Firmicutes/Lachnospiraceae/Blautia, decreased Proteobacteria), increased
short-chain fatty acids and GLP-1. Antibiotic administration reversed
effects, suggesting gut microbiota mediates part of the mechanism.
🔴 Preclinical (multi-point combination, cannot isolate EX-B3)

**Clinical — acupuncture + moxibustion for T2DM:**
Liao et al. 2007 (Zhongguo Zhen Jiu, PMID 17722822): 79 T2DM patients,
3 groups (acupuncture only, moxibustion only, acupuncture + moxibustion),
all at EX-B3 only. All groups improved FBG, 24h urinary glucose, HbA1c,
TC, TG, LDL (all P<0.05); HDL increased. Combined acupuncture +
moxibustion showed best results.
🟡 Plausible (small RCT, no blinding, Chinese-language journal — but
POINT-SPECIFIC: only EX-B3 was used, providing rare single-point data)

**Data mining — pre-diabetes catgut embedding:**
Frontiers in Public Health 2023: meta-analysis + data mining. EX-B3
identified as a high-frequency acupoint (>50% of included studies).
🟡 Plausible (pattern of use, not point-specific efficacy)

**Overall assessment:** EX-B3 has the STRONGEST preclinical evidence base
of any extra point — multiple controlled studies with sham comparison
demonstrating specific molecular mechanisms (PI3K/GLUT4, HPA axis,
GLP-1R, gut microbiota). The PI3K/GLUT4 study is notable for acupoint
specificity. However, clinical evidence remains limited (one small
unblinded RCT). The preclinical work is predominantly from one research
group (Li Rui/Cao BY et al.), awaiting independent replication. No
large-scale multi-center RCTs or systematic reviews for point-specific
EX-B3 diabetes treatment exist. Confidence for diabetes indication:
🟡 Plausible (strong preclinical, weak clinical).

**Evidence quality note:** preclinical ≠ clinical validation. The
PI3K/GLUT4 finding is mechanistically elegant but translational
uncertainty remains.

#### Cơ chế giải thích

**Segmental-sympathetic pathway:**
Needling at EX-B3 (T8, 1.5 cun lateral) → T8 dorsal ramus afferents →
dorsal horn convergent neurons (lamina V) → somatovisceral reflex →
modulation of T8 preganglionic sympathetic neurons → greater splanchnic
nerve → celiac ganglion → pancreatic plexus → pancreatic function.

**Pancreatic sympathetic innervation:**
The greater splanchnic nerve (T5-T9) provides primary sympathetic
innervation to the pancreas. Sympathetic activation inhibits insulin
release and stimulates glucagon release. EA-mediated modulation of
sympathetic tone at T8 could shift the balance toward insulin promotion.
The preclinical data showing restored beta-cell function (HOMA-β +382%)
and improved islet morphology is consistent with reduced sympathetic
inhibition of insulin release.

**Post-receptor insulin signaling mimicry:**
The PI3K/GLUT4 mechanism (Cao 2016) engages the SAME intracellular
pathway activated by insulin itself: insulin → IRS-1 → PI3K → Akt →
GLUT4 translocation. EA at EX-B3 enhances this pathway in skeletal
muscle without increasing total GLUT4 protein — it promotes translocation
of existing GLUT4 to the membrane, augmenting insulin sensitivity at the
post-receptor level.

**The "Missing Pancreas Back-Shu" interpretation:**
EX-B3 functions identically to a Back-Shu point: same position (1.5 cun
lateral), same nerve branch (medial dorsal ramus), same somatovisceral
reflex mechanism (Sato 1997). Classified as "extra" solely because TCM's
Zang-Fu system did not recognize the pancreas as separate from the
Spleen. The T8 gap (AP-07 Observation 26) is not an anatomical gap but
a CLASSIFICATORY gap. The Spleen Back-Shu (BL20) at T11 has a viscerotome
range of T6-T8 (AP-07 L4046), overlapping with T8 — reflecting
embryological and segmental overlap between pancreas and spleen
innervation via the greater splanchnic nerve.

**Comparison: EX-B3 (T8) vs BL20 Pishu (T11):**
Gao et al. 2014 demonstrated EX-B3 was superior to BL20 for specific
HPA outcomes in T2DM — suggesting the T8 pancreas-specific level
provides more targeted access than the T11 spleen-composite level for
diabetes.

**Confidence:** 🟡 Synthesis. Segmental pathway is anatomically
validated. PI3K/GLUT4 mechanism is preclinically demonstrated. The
"missing Pancreas Back-Shu" concept is an anatomical inference.

#### Kỹ thuật kích thích

**Needling:** oblique insertion directed medially toward the spine, 7-15
mm (0.3-0.5 cun), approximately 45 degrees. Shallow depth mandatory.
De Qi: local aching and distention in mid-back. Patient prone.

**Electroacupuncture:** primary modality in preclinical research. Protocol
(Cao et al. 2016): 2 Hz continuous wave, 2 mA, 20 min/day, 6 days/week,
4 weeks. Needle: 0.16 mm × 7 mm, oblique 45°, depth 4 mm. Bilateral
EX-B3 paired.

**Moxibustion:** historically the FIRST recorded technique (Sun Simiao:
"100 moxa cones"). Liao et al. 2007 tested moxibustion alone and combined
with acupuncture — combined was most effective. Moxa at EX-B3 for
diabetes has a 1,374-year documented history (652 CE → present).

**Acupoint catgut embedding (穴位埋线):** sustained-stimulation technique
identified as high-frequency application for pre-diabetes (Frontiers in
Public Health 2023).

#### Dynamic acupoint properties

**Viscerosomatic sensitization at T8:**
In pancreatic disease states (pancreatitis, diabetic autonomic
neuropathy), the T8 paravertebral area may exhibit sensitization through
viscerosomatic reflex — the same pathway underlying Back-Shu point
dynamics. Pancreatic afferents via the greater splanchnic nerve converge
with somatic afferents from the T8 dorsal ramus at the dorsal horn,
facilitating cross-talk that produces hyperalgesia and altered electrical
properties at the skin/muscle level.

This predicted sensitization has not been directly measured at EX-B3
(unlike Dingchuan EX-B1, where skin impedance changes were documented
by Ngai & Jones 2009). The clinical phenomenon of epigastric-to-back
pain radiation in pancreatitis (T6-T10 referred pain) provides indirect
evidence that the T8 paravertebral area becomes dynamically altered in
pancreatic pathology.

**Diagnostic potential:** if viscerosomatic sensitization at EX-B3 could
be objectively measured (skin impedance, pressure pain threshold), it
might serve as a non-invasive indicator of pancreatic function —
analogous to EX-LE6 Dannangxue (tenderness = gallbladder pathology).
→ Cross-ref: C04 S4

#### An toàn

**Pneumothorax (CRITICAL):**
EX-B3 lies directly over the thoracic cavity at T8 level. Deep or
perpendicular insertion MUST be avoided. Always use oblique insertion
directed medially toward the spine. Maximum safe depth: 0.5 cun (15 mm)
with oblique angle. Same precautions as all thoracic Back-Shu points
(BL11-BL21).
→ Cross-ref: AP-07 Back-Shu safety

**Kidney proximity:** NOT a concern at T8 (kidneys are at T12-L3 level).

**Pregnancy:** NOT specifically contraindicated.

---

### EX-B4 — Bĩ Căn (痞根, Pǐgēn)

**Evidence tier: C (Minimum Viable Entry)**

"Root of Masses" (痞 = abdominal mass/fullness + 根 = root). 3.5 cun
lateral to the lower border of L1 spinous process, bilateral. Located
below the free end of the 12th rib. The name reflects the historical
indication — treating 痞 (pǐ), palpable abdominal masses including
hepatosplenomegaly and other visceral enlargements. At 3.5 cun lateral,
this point lies BEYOND the outer BL line (3.0 cun), in the lateral
abdominal musculature.

#### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location: 3.5 cun lateral to the lower border of L1 spinous
process, at the inferior border of the free end of the 12th rib.
Bilateral.

Spatial relationships:
- **BL22 Sanjiaoshu (L1):** 1.5 cun lateral, same level. 2.0 cun medial.
- **BL51 Huangmen (L1):** 3.0 cun lateral, outer BL line. EX-B4 is 0.5
  cun further lateral.
- **Free end of 12th rib:** key landmark — EX-B4 is at or just below
  this bony reference.

Needle depth: 15-25 mm (0.5-1.0 cun), perpendicular. Safer than thoracic
levels (below pleural cavity at L1).

#### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- **L1 dorsal ramus, lateral branch:** at 3.5 cun lateral, innervates
  iliocostalis and longissimus.
- **Subcostal nerve (T12 ventral ramus):** runs below the 12th rib.
- **L1 ventral ramus:** iliohypogastric/ilioinguinal nerves originate.

Spinal segments: T12-L1.

Muscle layers: latissimus dorsi, external oblique, internal oblique,
quadratus lumborum (deep).

#### Phân loại huyệt (Point Category Membership)

Extra point: EX-B4. Not on any meridian. No standard category
memberships. No special designations.

#### Cross-tradition mapping

**1. TCM acupoint:** EX-B4 Pigen (痞根, "Root of Masses"). Function:
resolves masses (消痞), softens hardness. Primary: palpable abdominal
masses, hepatosplenomegaly. Secondary: lumbar pain. ✓

**2. Trigger point:** quadratus lumborum TrPs at this approximate location
(Travell & Simons), with referred pain to hip and lower abdomen. The TrP
match is musculoskeletal, not visceral. 🟡

**3-8:** No correspondence for Ashi (not a common spontaneous tender
point), Marma, Head zone (L1 has no specific visceral mass target),
Anatomy Trains, Neurogenic, or Cupping. ✗

**Convergence score: 2/8** (TCM ✓, Trigger point 🟡). LOW.

#### Chỉ định truyền thống

- Primary: abdominal masses (痞块), hepatosplenomegaly
- Secondary: gastric ptosis, lumbar pain
- Combined with BL22, BL23, ST36, SP6

#### Bằng chứng hiện đại

No systematic review, meta-analysis, or major RCT specifically studying
EX-B4. Clinical use has declined with modern diagnostic imaging. The
concept of treating palpable masses through a distal-from-mass acupoint
reflects traditional reasoning (resolving the "root" of the condition)
rather than direct anatomical targeting.

#### An toàn

**Kidney proximity:** posterior pole of the kidney (especially right) may
extend to L1 level. Deep insertion at 3.5 cun lateral risks approaching
posterior renal fascia. Limit depth to 1.0 cun.

**Pleural recess:** costophrenic recess may extend to 12th rib level.
Avoid angling the needle superiorly.

**Pregnancy:** NOT specifically contraindicated.

---

### EX-B5 — Thập Thất Chùy Hạ (十七椎下, Shíqī Zhuī Xià)

**Evidence tier: C (Minimum Viable Entry)**

"Below the Seventeenth Vertebra" (十七 = seventeen + 椎 = vertebra + 下 =
below). On the posterior MIDLINE, in the depression below the spinous
process of L5 — the lumbosacral junction. The "17th vertebra" count
starts from T1: T1-T12 (12) + L1-L5 (5) = 17th = L5. Located on the GV
(Governing Vessel) line position but NOT a standard GV point — analogous
to EX-B3 occupying the Back-Shu position without being a Back-Shu point.

#### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location: on the posterior midline, in the depression below
the spinous process of L5. At the L5-S1 lumbosacral junction.

Spatial relationships:
- **GV3 Yaoyangguan (L4):** one segment above, same midline. The lowest
  standard GV point on the lumbar spine.
- **GV2 Yaoshu (sacral hiatus):** below, on the sacrum.
- **BL26 Guanyuanshu (L5):** 1.5 cun lateral, same level.

Needle depth: 25-40 mm (1.0-1.5 cun), perpendicular. Safe — spinal cord
ends at L1-L2. At L5-S1, only cauda equina nerve roots are present.

#### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- **L5 dorsal ramus, medial branch:** via interspinous and supraspinous
  ligaments.
- **S1 contribution:** L5-S1 junction receives bilateral innervation.

Spinal segments: L5-S1.

Fascial: supraspinous ligament, interspinous ligament. Dense connective
tissue at the lumbosacral junction.

#### Phân loại huyệt (Point Category Membership)

Extra point: EX-B5. Not on any meridian (despite GV midline position).
No standard category memberships. No special designations.

#### Cross-tradition mapping

**1. TCM acupoint:** EX-B5 Shiqizhuixia (十七椎下, "Below the 17th
Vertebra"). Functions: regulates menstruation, stops pain. Primary:
dysmenorrhea, lower back pain. Secondary: leg paralysis, labor pain. ✓

**2. Ashi correspondence:** the L5-S1 junction is one of the most common
sites of lower back tenderness. Interspinous ligament tenderness at
L5-S1 is a standard clinical finding in disc disease and facet syndrome. ✓

**3-8:** No correspondence for Trigger point (no TrPs at midline
ligamentous structures), Marma, Head zone (L5-S1 dermatome maps to lower
limb/perineum, not uterus), Anatomy Trains, Neurogenic, or Cupping. ✗

**Convergence score: 2/8** (TCM ✓, Ashi ✓). LOW.

#### Chỉ định truyền thống

- Primary: dysmenorrhea (痛经), irregular menstruation
- Secondary: lower back pain, leg paralysis
- Tertiary: labor pain (historical — moxa to facilitate delivery)
- Combined with SP6, CV3, CV4 for gynecological conditions
- Moxa is the traditional primary technique

#### Bằng chứng hiện đại

No systematic review or major RCT specifically studying EX-B5. The point
appears in some dysmenorrhea protocols but not as a specifically studied
intervention.

#### An toàn

**Spinal cord:** NOT at risk — conus medullaris ends at L1-L2. At L5-S1,
only cauda equina roots are present.

**Pregnancy:** exercise CAUTION. Historically used for labor induction
(moxa). Avoid stimulation during early pregnancy.

---

### EX-B6 — Yêu Nhãn (腰眼, Yāoyǎn)

**Evidence tier: C (Minimum Viable Entry)**

"Lumbar Eyes" (腰 = lumbar/waist + 眼 = eye). 3.5 cun lateral to the
lower border of L4 spinous process, bilateral. Named for the visible
depressions on either side of the lumbar spine — the "eyes" of the lower
back, visible as indentations at the lateral border of the erector spinae
musculature. One of the most universally palpated landmarks in lumbar
clinical examination.

#### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location: in the depression 3.5 cun lateral to the lower
border of L4 spinous process. Bilateral.

Spatial relationships:
- **BL25 Dachangshu (L4):** 1.5 cun lateral, same level. Large Intestine
  Back-Shu. 2.0 cun medial.
- **EX-B4 Pigen (L1):** same lateral distance (3.5 cun), three segments
  above.

Needle depth: 25-40 mm (1.0-1.5 cun), perpendicular. Safe zone — thick
lumbar musculature, below pleural cavity, below spinal cord termination.

#### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- **L4 dorsal ramus, lateral branch:** at 3.5 cun, innervates
  iliocostalis lumborum and longissimus.
- **Superior cluneal nerves (L1-L3 dorsal rami lateral branches):**
  cross this area superficially.

Spinal segments: L4.

Muscle layers: lumbar aponeurosis (thoracolumbar fascia), erector spinae
(lateral portion), quadratus lumborum (deep).

#### Phân loại huyệt (Point Category Membership)

Extra point: EX-B6. Not on any meridian. No standard category
memberships. No special designations.

#### Cross-tradition mapping

**1. TCM acupoint:** EX-B6 Yaoyan (腰眼, "Lumbar Eyes"). Functions:
strengthens the lumbar region, benefits the kidneys. Primary: lower
back pain, lumbar strain. Secondary: kidney disorders (TCM concept). ✓

**2. Trigger point:** quadratus lumborum and iliocostalis lumborum TrPs
at this approximate location (Travell & Simons), with referred pain to
iliac crest, hip, and lower abdomen. ✓

**3. Ashi correspondence:** the lumbar depressions are among the most
commonly tender palpation sites in lower back pain. Universal clinical
finding in lumbar pathology. ✓

**4-8:** No specific correspondence for Marma, Head zone (L4 maps to
lower limb, no specific organ), Anatomy Trains, Neurogenic, or
Cupping. ✗

**Convergence score: 3/8** (TCM ✓, Trigger point ✓, Ashi ✓). MODERATE —
a genuine musculoskeletal convergence zone.

#### Chỉ định truyền thống

- Primary: lower back pain (腰痛), chronic lumbar strain
- Secondary: kidney conditions (TCM kidney deficiency), frequent urination
- Commonly treated with moxibustion for chronic conditions
- Combined with BL23, BL25, GV4 (Mingmen)

#### Bằng chứng hiện đại

No systematic review or major RCT specifically studying EX-B6. The
anatomical area corresponds to common lumbar myofascial pain sites,
extensively studied under orthopedic and physical therapy frameworks.

#### An toàn

**Low risk.** L4 is below the pleural cavity and spinal cord termination.
At 3.5 cun lateral, the needle approaches the lateral abdominal wall —
avoid excessive depth or anterolateral angulation.

**Pregnancy:** NOT specifically contraindicated.

---

### EX-B7 — Yêu Kỳ (腰奇, Yāoqí)

**Evidence tier: C (Minimum Viable Entry)**

"Lumbar Oddity" (腰 = lumbar + 奇 = odd/unusual). At the sacral hiatus
(裂孔), approximately 2 cun above the tip of the coccyx. On the posterior
MIDLINE. The name 奇 (qí, "odd/unusual") may reference the unusual
location (the sacral hiatus is a natural opening in the sacral canal) or
the unusual primary indication — epilepsy, a cerebral condition treated
through a sacral point. Like EX-B5, this midline point occupies the GV
position without being a standard GV point. GV2 Yaoshu (腰俞) is also
described at the sacral hiatus — the relationship between GV2 and EX-B7
is debated; some sources treat them as identical, others differentiate by
precise location or indication emphasis.

#### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location: at the sacral hiatus, in the depression
approximately 2 cun above the tip of the coccyx. Posterior midline.

The sacral hiatus is the opening at the inferior end of the sacral canal,
formed by failure of fusion of the S5 (and sometimes S4) laminae. Covered
by the sacrococcygeal ligament.

Spatial relationships:
- **GV2 Yaoshu (sacral hiatus):** essentially the SAME location. The
  distinction may be nomenclatural rather than anatomical.
- **GV1 Changqiang (coccyx tip):** ~2 cun inferior, between coccyx tip
  and anus.
- **BL31-34 Baliao (sacral foramina):** access sacral nerves through
  lateral foramina. EX-B7 accesses the sacral canal from its inferior
  opening (hiatus).

Needle depth: 15-25 mm (0.5-1.0 cun), perpendicular or slightly
superior. May enter the sacral canal through the sacrococcygeal ligament.

#### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- **S4-S5 dorsal rami and coccygeal nerve:** emerge from the sacral
  hiatus.
- **Sacral canal contents:** at the hiatus level, the canal contains
  coccygeal nerve roots, filum terminale, and epidural fat. The dural sac
  ends at approximately S2, so dural puncture risk is minimal.

Spinal segments: S4-S5, coccygeal.

Fascial: sacrococcygeal ligament — dense fibrous membrane covering the
hiatus.

#### Phân loại huyệt (Point Category Membership)

Extra point: EX-B7. Not on any meridian (despite GV-line position).
Possible overlap with GV2 Yaoshu.
No standard category memberships. No special designations.

#### Cross-tradition mapping

**1. TCM acupoint:** EX-B7 Yaoqi (腰奇, "Lumbar Oddity"). Functions:
calms the spirit (安神), regulates lower orifices. Primary (historical):
epilepsy (癫痫). Secondary: hemorrhoids, lower back pain, constipation. ✓

**2-8:** No specific correspondence for Trigger point (no paraspinal
muscle at sacral hiatus), Ashi (not commonly tender), Marma (Guda Marma
is at anus/perineum, not sacral hiatus), Head zone (S4-S5 = perineal,
not epilepsy-related), Anatomy Trains, Neurogenic, or Cupping. ✗

**Convergence score: 1/8** (TCM ✓ only). LOW — the sacral hiatus is a
unique anatomical structure with no parallel in other traditions. The
epilepsy indication has no segmental neuroanatomical rationale (epilepsy
is cortical; S4-S5 is sacral).

#### Chỉ định truyền thống

- Primary (historical): epilepsy (癫痫), mania (癫狂)
- Secondary: hemorrhoids, rectal prolapse, lower back pain, constipation
