- The epilepsy indication is unusual for a sacral point and lacks modern
  anatomical rationale
- GV2 shares similar indications (epilepsy, hemorrhoids)
- Combined with GV14 (Dazhui), GV20 (Baihui) for epilepsy protocols

#### Bằng chứng hiện đại

No systematic review or major RCT specifically studying EX-B7. The
epilepsy indication is managed through pharmaceutical and neurosurgical
approaches. Caudal epidural access through the sacral hiatus is an
established anesthetic procedure, but distinct from acupuncture.

#### An toàn

**Sacral canal entry:** the needle may pass through the sacrococcygeal
ligament into the sacral canal. Dural sac ends at S2, so dural puncture
risk is minimal at S5 level. Strict aseptic technique essential.

**Infection risk:** sacral canal is a semi-enclosed space. Aseptic
technique critical.

**Pregnancy:** exercise CAUTION in sacral/coccygeal area during pregnancy.

---

### EX-B8 — Bát Hoa (八花, Bāhuā)

**Evidence tier: C (Minimum Viable Entry)**

"Eight Flowers" (八 = eight + 花 = flower). A HISTORICAL multi-point set
consisting of 4 pairs (8 individual points) located near the BL
inner line in the mid-to-lower thoracic paravertebral region, at levels
approximately corresponding to BL17 (T7), BL18 (T9), BL19 (T10), and
BL20 (T11). Primarily a historical designation for tuberculosis (TB)
treatment before the antibiotic era. Rarely used in modern clinical
practice. The exact positions and inter-point spacing vary between
textual sources.

#### Vị trí giải phẫu

Location: 4 pairs of points near the BL inner line (1.5 cun lateral),
at spinal levels approximately T7-T11. The exact positions vary between
sources — some describe measurement from GV14 (Dazhui) downward with
specific inter-point distances, others reference proximity to BL17-BL20.

Notable: the 8 points largely OVERLAP with existing BL inner-line points
(BL17-BL20). The Bahua designation is a PRESCRIPTION SET — a named
combination of existing or near-existing points — rather than a set of
anatomically distinct new locations.

Needle depth: 7-15 mm (0.3-0.5 cun), oblique insertion directed medially.
Same as thoracic Back-Shu points. Pneumothorax precautions apply.

#### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply: T7-T11 dorsal rami, medial branches — same as the
corresponding BL inner-line points. No unique anatomical features beyond
what is documented for BL17-BL20 (AP-07).

Spinal segments: T7-T11.

#### Phân loại huyệt (Point Category Membership)

Extra point: EX-B8. A GROUP designation (4 pairs = 8 points), not an
individual point. The constituent points overlap with standard BL
inner-line positions. No standard category memberships.

#### Cross-tradition mapping

**1. TCM acupoint:** EX-B8 Bahua (八花, "Eight Flowers"). Function:
nourishes Yin, clears Heat, stops cough. Primary (historical): pulmonary
tuberculosis (肺痨, fèi láo, "lung consumption"). ✓

**2. Cupping point:** the mid-thoracic back (T7-T12) is one of the most
common cupping zones for respiratory and pulmonary conditions in both TCM
and Hijama. The Bahua area corresponds to a standard cupping zone. 🟡

**3-8:** No specific correspondence for Trigger point, Ashi, Marma, Head
zone (T7-T11 spans too many dermatomes for a specific match), Anatomy
Trains, or Neurogenic. ✗

**Convergence score: 2/8** (TCM ✓, Cupping 🟡). LOW.

#### Chỉ định truyền thống

- Primary (HISTORICAL): pulmonary tuberculosis (肺痨, "lung consumption")
- Secondary: chronic cough, hemoptysis, night sweats, afternoon fever
  (classic TB symptoms)
- Technique: primarily moxibustion — direct moxa at all 8 points.
  Historical context: before streptomycin (1944) and rifampicin (1968),
  TB was managed through supportive measures including Bahua moxibustion.
  The point set's decline in modern practice directly correlates with
  effective anti-TB chemotherapy.

#### Bằng chứng hiện đại

No systematic review or modern RCT studying EX-B8. TB is treated with
standardized multi-drug antibiotic regimens (DOTS strategy). The
constituent BL points (BL17-BL20) have individual evidence documented in
AP-07.

#### An toàn

**Pneumothorax (CRITICAL):** all 8 points are at thoracic levels (T7-T11).
Standard thoracic BL precautions apply. Particularly important: Bahua
involves MULTIPLE thoracic needling sites in a single session —
cumulative pneumothorax risk increases with the number of thoracic points
needled simultaneously.

**Moxa safety:** traditional Bahua used DIRECT (scarring) moxibustion,
causing intentional tissue damage. Modern practice should use indirect
or gentle techniques. Direct scarring moxibustion requires informed
consent.

**Pregnancy:** NOT specifically contraindicated.

---

*Phase 2 Batch 6 COMPLETE. 6 entries added: EX-B3 (Weiwanxiashu, Tier B,
6/8 convergence — functional "Pancreas Back-Shu" filling the T8 gap
between BL17 Geshu and BL18 Ganshu, PI3K/GLUT4 molecular mechanism
Cao 2016 with sham-controlled acupoint specificity, HPA axis EX-B3
superior to BL20/BL23 Gao 2014, GLP-1R/islet morphology Cao 2017,
clinical RCT n=79 Liao 2007, 1,374-year diabetes treatment history since
Sun Simiao 652 CE), EX-B4 (Pigen, Tier C, 2/8 — abdominal masses, 3.5
cun lateral to L1), EX-B5 (Shiqizhuixia, Tier C, 2/8 — lumbosacral
midline, dysmenorrhea/labor pain), EX-B6 (Yaoyan, Tier C, 3/8 — lumbar
depressions, universal lower back pain tender point), EX-B7 (Yaoqi,
Tier C, 1/8 — sacral hiatus, historical epilepsy, GV2 overlap), EX-B8
(Bahua, Tier C, 2/8 — historical TB treatment set, 8 points overlapping
BL17-BL20). **EX-B REGION 8/8 COMPLETE.** Total entries in AP-15: 23/48
(including Phase 1 Yintang). No new ⚡ framework notes (running total:
41). Key findings: (1) EX-B3 is the functional "missing Pancreas
Back-Shu" at the only thoracic gap in the BL inner line, filling a
classificatory gap created by TCM merging the pancreas into the Spleen
organ system; (2) PI3K/GLUT4 translocation is the strongest molecular
mechanism evidence for any extra point, with sham-controlled acupoint
specificity; (3) EX-B3 was superior to BL20 Pishu for HPA outcomes in
T2DM (Gao 2014), supporting pancreas-specific vs spleen-composite
segmental targeting; (4) the 8 EX-B points span 3 distinct lateral
positions (0.5 cun: EX-B1/B2, 1.5 cun: EX-B3, 3.5 cun: EX-B4/B6) and 2
midline points (EX-B5/B7), demonstrating the full lateral spectrum of the
dorsal ramus branching pattern plus the midline ligamentous approach.*

---

## EX-CA — Chest & Abdomen (胸腹部)

### EX-CA1 — Tử Cung (子宫, Zǐgōng)

**Evidence tier: B (Standard Entry)**

"Palace of the Child" (子 = child/offspring + 宫 = palace/womb). 3 cun
lateral to CV3 (Zhongji), bilateral. The only extra point in the
chest/abdomen region. The name directly identifies the target organ —
one of the few acupoints named after a specific anatomical structure
rather than using metaphorical language. Located in the lower abdominal
inguinal region over the path of the round ligament of the uterus,
in the L1 (ilioinguinal/iliohypogastric) segmental territory — the
caudal boundary of the uterine sympathetic viscerotome (T10-L1).

**⚠ NAMING CAUTION:** 子宫 (Zǐgōng, EX-CA1, "Uterus/Palace of the
Child") uses DIFFERENT characters from CV19 紫宫 (Zǐgōng, "Purple
Palace"). Despite identical Pinyin romanization: 子 (child/offspring)
≠ 紫 (purple). CV19 is on the sternum at the 2nd intercostal space
with respiratory indications — it has NO connection to the uterus.
This homophone confusion is a well-documented pitfall in
English-language acupuncture texts.
→ Cross-ref: AP-14 CV19

#### Lịch sử và danh pháp (History and Nomenclature)

First documented in the **Zhenjiu Dacheng** (针灸大成, "Great
Compendium of Acupuncture and Moxibustion," Yang Jizhou 杨继洲,
1601 CE, Ming dynasty): "Zigong: two points, located 3 cun lateral
to Zhongji on each side. Needle 2 cun, moxa 14 cones. Treats women
who have long been without children" (子宫二穴,在中极旁开三寸。
针二寸,灸十四壮。治妇人久无子嗣).

Possibly recorded earlier in the Zhenjiu Daquan (针灸大全, Xu Feng
徐凤, ~1439 CE, Ming dynasty), though the specific passage has not
been independently verified in that text's contents.

NOT found in the Qianjin Yaofang (Sun Simiao, 652 CE) despite that
text's extensive three-juan gynecology section — indicating that
EX-CA1 is a relatively late addition (Ming dynasty, 15th-17th century)
to the extra point corpus, not an ancient Han or Tang era point.

The classical citation establishes infertility (久无子嗣, "long
without children") as the PRIMARY historical indication — the
point's name "Palace of the Child" directly reflects this
reproductive focus.

Hán-Việt: Tử Cung (子宫). Tử = child/offspring, Cung = palace/womb.
The Vietnamese term tử cung is the standard anatomical term for the
uterus, identical to the Chinese usage.

#### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): 3 cun lateral to CV3 (Zhongji),
at the level of 4 cun below the umbilicus. Bilateral.

Anatomical landmarks: CV3 is on the anterior midline, 4 cun below
the umbilicus, 1 cun above the upper border of the pubic symphysis.
EX-CA1 is 3 cun (~6-7 cm) lateral to this point, placing it in the
inguinal region of the lower abdomen, medial to the anterior superior
iliac spine (ASIS).

Spatial relationships:
- **CV3 (Zhongji):** 3 cun medial, on the anterior midline. Front-Mu
  point of the Bladder. Same spinal segmental level (T12-L1).
- **CV4 (Guanyuan):** 3 cun below the umbilicus, on midline — one
  proportional cun superior. Front-Mu of the Small Intestine and a
  major gynecological point.
- **ST29 (Guilai):** 4 cun below the umbilicus, 2 cun lateral to
  midline — 1 cun medial to EX-CA1 at the same level. Stomach
  meridian point with gynecological indications (irregular menses,
  hernia).
- **Deep inguinal ring:** approximately at or near this lateral
  position. The round ligament of the uterus exits through the deep
  inguinal ring, traverses the inguinal canal, and terminates in the
  labia majora.

Needle depth: 0.8-1.2 cun (~2-3 cm) perpendicular. Classical depth
(Zhenjiu Dacheng): up to 2 cun. Some sources describe oblique needling
toward the pubic symphysis for uterine prolapse (1-2 cun oblique).

#### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- **Ilioinguinal nerve (L1):** primary sensory innervation to the
  inguinal region, medial thigh, and external genitalia. Enters the
  abdominal wall medial and inferior to the ASIS. Motor branches to
  internal oblique and transversus abdominis.
- **Iliohypogastric nerve (L1, with T12 contribution):** innervates
  the suprapubic skin and lower abdominal wall. Motor to internal
  oblique and transversus abdominis. The anterior cutaneous branch
  supplies the suprapubic region.
- **Genitofemoral nerve (L1-L2):** deeper structure, runs on the
  psoas major surface and exits near the inguinal ligament. Genital
  branch enters the inguinal canal — less directly accessible at
  standard needling depths but anatomically nearby.

Spinal segments: L1 (primary), T12 (contribution via iliohypogastric).

Vascular: superficial circumflex iliac artery and vein (branches of
femoral/external iliac). Inferior epigastric artery runs deeper,
within the rectus sheath. Superficial epigastric vessels medially.

Muscle layers (superficial → deep):
- Skin → subcutaneous fat
- External oblique aponeurosis (at this level, tendinous)
- Internal oblique muscle
- Transversus abdominis muscle
- Transversalis fascia
- Preperitoneal fat → parietal peritoneum

Local structural landmark: the round ligament of the uterus passes
through the inguinal canal at approximately this level, running
parallel to the ilioinguinal nerve. The round ligament is a
smooth-muscle-containing structure continuous with the uterine
myometrium — providing a direct anatomical connection between this
needling site and the uterus.
→ Cross-ref: A01 S2, A03, AP-14 CV3

#### Phân loại huyệt (Point Category Membership)

Extra point (经外奇穴): EX-CA1 in WHO nomenclature. Not on any
meridian.

Five Shu: not applicable. Yuan-Source: not applicable.
Luo-Connecting: not applicable. Xi-Cleft: not applicable.
Back-Shu: not applicable. Front-Mu: not applicable.
Eight Confluent: not applicable. Hui-Meeting: not applicable.

Special designations:
- **Only extra point in the Chest/Abdomen region:** EX-CA1 is the
  sole representative of the EX-CA category in the WHO-WPRO 48-point
  system. All other lower-abdominal acupoints are on meridians
  (CV, ST, SP, KI, LR).
- **Functional "Front-Mu of the Uterus":** while no formal Front-Mu
  exists for the Uterus in TCM's standard Zang-Fu system (the Uterus
  is a 奇恒之腑 "extraordinary organ," not one of the 5 Zang or 6
  Fu), EX-CA1 functions analogously — it is the anterior diagnostic
  and treatment point for uterine conditions, paralleling how CV3 is
  the Front-Mu for the Bladder at the same level.
→ Cross-ref: B01 S1.3.3

#### Cross-tradition mapping

**1. TCM acupoint:** EX-CA1 Zigong (子宫, "Palace of the Child").
Functions: warms the uterus and regulates menstruation (暖宫调经),
benefits fertility, lifts prolapsed uterus (举宫). Primary
indication: infertility (the original 1601 indication), irregular
menses, dysmenorrhea, uterine prolapse. The name is unique among
acupoints in directly naming the target organ — most points use
poetic metaphor or anatomical landmarks. ✓

**2. Trigger point:** no specific Travell & Simons trigger point has
been mapped to this precise location (3 cun lateral to midline at the
suprapubic level). Abdominal wall trigger points in the rectus
abdominis and internal/external oblique are documented in chronic
pelvic pain syndromes, but the correspondence is regional/generic
rather than point-specific. ✗

**3. Ashi correspondence:** not a standard palpation-based tender
point in the general population. In patients with chronic pelvic
pain, lower abdominal tenderness at this level is common but is
associated with myofascial or visceral pathology rather than specific
acupoint sensitization. ✗

**4. Marma point:** no exact anatomical match. **Vitapa Marma** is
located at the inguinal region (junction of pelvis and thigh) and
governs urogenital function in Ayurvedic tradition — classically
indicated for inguinal hernia, hydrocele, and reproductive disorders.
Approximately 2-3 cm from EX-CA1 with overlapping functional
territory. 🟡

**5. Head zone:** L1 dermatome covers the inguinal/suprapubic region.
The uterine visceral referral zone encompasses T10-L1 (sympathetic
afferents via the hypogastric plexus), with ovarian/adnexal referral
more specifically at T12-L1. EX-CA1's L1 position sits at the
**caudal boundary** of the uterine viscerotome — a partial but
meaningful match for uterine body indications, and a stronger match
for ovarian/adnexal conditions. ✓
→ Cross-ref: A07 S2.1

**6. Anatomy Trains:** not applicable for a deep abdominal wall
point. ✗

**7. Neurogenic inflammatory spot:** no direct Kim 2017-type data.
However, infrared radiation spectroscopy (PMC6699375, n=32 healthy
women) demonstrated significantly higher radiation intensity at
left EX-CA1 vs control points throughout the menstrual cycle, with
spectral changes at ~15 μm suggesting altered local glucose
metabolism. Consistent with the prediction that reproductive organ
activity produces measurable changes at segmentally-linked skin
sites. 🟡
→ Cross-ref: C04 S4

**8. Cupping point:** not a standard cupping site. ✗

**Convergence score: 4/8** (TCM ✓, Marma 🟡, Head zone ✓, Neurogenic
🟡). MODERATE-HIGH — reflects genuine segmental basis for
gynecological function (L1 at uterine viscerotome boundary) and
cross-tradition recognition of the inguinal zone as a urogenital
governance point (Vitapa Marma). The infrared radiation evidence adds
an objective physiological signal at this location.

#### Chỉ định truyền thống (DATA — ghi nhận, không endorse as mechanism)

Nguồn cổ:
- First documentation (Zhenjiu Dacheng, 1601 CE):
  "Treats women who have long been without children" — infertility
  as primary indication
- Moxa: 14 cones (灸十四壮) — classical emphasis on moxibustion
  for warming the uterus

Modern textbook (Deadman, WHO):
- Infertility (不孕, bù yùn)
- Uterine prolapse (子宫脱垂, zǐgōng tuōchuí)
- Irregular menstruation (月经不调, yuè jīng bù tiáo)
- Dysmenorrhea (痛经, tòng jīng)
- Amenorrhea
- Abnormal uterine bleeding (崩漏, bēng lòu)

Classical combinations:
- **EX-CA1 + CV4 (Guanyuan) + SP6 (Sanyinjiao):** classic fertility
  protocol — lower abdominal + lower limb Three Yin Intersection.
  Warm-needle or moxibustion at CV4 and EX-CA1 for "cold uterus"
  (宫寒, gōng hán) pattern.
- **EX-CA1 + CV3 (Zhongji) + ST29 (Guilai) + SP6:** comprehensive
  lower-abdominal gynecological protocol — Front-Mu + extra point
  + Stomach channel + Spleen channel covering the full T12-L1
  segmental zone.
- **EX-CA1 + GV20 (Baihui):** uterine prolapse protocol —
  combining the local point with the vertex "lifting" point.

#### Bằng chứng hiện đại

**IVF/infertility — protocol-level evidence:**
EX-CA1 appears consistently among the top acupoints used in IVF-ET
(in vitro fertilization — embryo transfer) acupuncture trials. A
bibliometric analysis of acupuncture for endometrial receptivity
(Frontiers in Medicine, 2025) identified EX-CA1 alongside CV4, SP6,
CV3, ST36, and CV6 as core points across >10 studies. A systematic
review of acupuncture for IVF in poor-ovarian-response women included
CV3 and EX-CA1 in two of the reviewed studies.
🟡 Plausible (consistent protocol-level inclusion, but no
point-specific isolation — always in multi-point combination)

**Perimenopausal syndrome — near-point-specific RCT:**
EA at EX-CA1 (Zigong) + ST25 (Tianshu), 10Hz/50Hz alternating,
30 minutes, 3 times/week for 8 weeks vs hormone therapy (estradiol
valerate + medroxyprogesterone). N=58 (29 per group). EA group:
93.1% effectiveness on Menopause Rating Scale, with comparable
improvement to hormone therapy during treatment AND superior
long-term follow-up (PubMed 29071914).
🟡 Plausible (RCT data positive but small sample, 2-point protocol
not point-specific, no sham comparison)

**Infrared radiation specificity:**
Infrared radiation spectra (1.5-18 μm) measured at EX-CA1 in 32
healthy women across menstrual cycle phases. Left EX-CA1 showed
significantly higher radiation intensity vs control points throughout
the cycle. Right EX-CA1 differed pre/post-menses but not during
menses. Spectral changes at ~15 μm band suggested altered local
glucose metabolism (PMC6699375).
🟡 Plausible (demonstrates acupoint-specific physiological properties
but measures local characteristics, not therapeutic efficacy)

**Overall assessment:** EX-CA1 is a frequently INCLUDED point in
gynecological/fertility acupuncture protocols. No isolated systematic
review or RCT testing EX-CA1 alone against sham/control exists. All
efficacy claims are protocol-level, not point-specific. The infrared
study provides objective evidence of acupoint-specific physiological
properties but does not demonstrate therapeutic efficacy.

#### Cơ chế giải thích

**Proposed segmental pathway:**
Needling at EX-CA1 → ilioinguinal/iliohypogastric nerve (L1)
afferents → L1 dorsal horn → viscerosomatic convergence with
uterine sympathetic afferents (T10-L1 via hypogastric plexus) →
somatovisceral reflex modulating uterine sympathetic tone.

**L1 segmental position — nuance:** L1 sits at the CAUDAL BOUNDARY
of the uterine sympathetic viscerotome (T10-L1), not at its core
(T11-T12). This means EX-CA1's segmental access to the uterus is
real but peripheral. The point is better positioned segmentally for
ovarian/adnexal conditions (primary viscerotome T12-L1) than for
uterine body conditions per se. However, the lumbar splanchnic nerve
from L1 contributes to the superior hypogastric plexus, which is
continuous with the uterine sympathetic chain — providing indirect
but anatomically verified access.

**Round ligament local mechanism:** the round ligament of the uterus
passes through the inguinal canal near this needling site. The round
ligament contains smooth muscle continuous with the uterine
myometrium. Needling near this structure could provide a
local/mechanical reflex pathway to the uterus independent of the
segmental dermatomal argument — a direct physical connection between
the needle site and the target organ.

**Comparison with CV3 (Front-Mu of Bladder):** both points are at
the same craniocaudal level (4 cun below umbilicus) and share the
T12-L1 segmental field. CV3 at the midline accesses both sides
simultaneously through the linea alba. EX-CA1 at 3 cun lateral
accesses the same segment through the lateral abdominal wall, with
more direct contact with the ilioinguinal nerve trunk and the round
ligament. The lateral position may provide stronger unilateral
stimulation of the L1 segment.

**Confidence:** 🟡 Synthesis. The segmental pathway is anatomically
plausible. The round ligament proximity adds a unique local mechanism.
No direct mechanistic study (fMRI, electrophysiology) has been
conducted for EX-CA1.

#### Kỹ thuật kích thích

**Needling:** perpendicular, 0.8-1.2 cun (~2-3 cm). The classical
Zhenjiu Dacheng specified 2 cun depth — deeper than modern standard
recommendations. For uterine prolapse, some practitioners use oblique
needling directed toward the pubic symphysis (1-2 cun oblique).

**Moxibustion:** classical emphasis — Zhenjiu Dacheng prescribed 14
moxa cones (灸十四壮). Moxibustion at EX-CA1 is particularly
associated with the "cold uterus" (宫寒) pattern in TCM gynecology.
Warm-needle moxibustion (温针灸) combining needling with moxa is
commonly applied.

**Electroacupuncture:** used in modern fertility and perimenopausal
protocols. The perimenopausal RCT (PubMed 29071914) used 10Hz/50Hz
alternating frequency, 30 minutes duration.

**Acupoint herbal patching:** EX-CA1 is one of the target points for
herbal plaster therapy in dysmenorrhea protocols.

#### Dynamic acupoint properties

**Menstrual-cycle-dependent infrared radiation (PMC6699375):**
Infrared spectroscopy at EX-CA1 reveals physiological-state-dependent
changes: radiation intensity at the left EX-CA1 is consistently
higher than at control points across all menstrual cycle phases, but
the right EX-CA1 shows differential behavior — elevated pre- and
post-menses but not during menses.

This bilateral asymmetry with menstrual cycle phase suggests
EX-CA1 exhibits physiological-state-dependent properties — the
acupoint's local tissue characteristics change with reproductive
hormonal cycling. This is a form of the dynamic acupoint phenomenon
(C04 §4) expressed through physiological variation rather than
pathological sensitization: the point becomes measurably different
from surrounding tissue in a CYCLIC pattern linked to the organ it
treats.

The ~15 μm spectral band changes implicate local glucose metabolism
variations, potentially reflecting altered sympathetic tone or
blood flow at this site across the menstrual cycle — consistent
with the L1 viscerosomatic link to the uterine sympathetic pathway.
→ Cross-ref: C04 S4

#### An toàn

**Pregnancy: CONTRAINDICATED.** EX-CA1 is classified as a forbidden
point during pregnancy (禁针穴). Strong stimulation at this lower
abdominal point, with direct segmental access to the uterine
sympathetic pathway and physical proximity to the round ligament,
carries theoretical risk of inducing uterine contractions.

**Bladder perforation:** the bladder lies posterior to the anterior
abdominal wall at this level, especially when distended. Patient MUST
empty bladder before needling. A full bladder extends superiorly from
the pelvis and can be within range of needles at standard depth.

**Peritoneal entry:** deep needling (>1.5 cun) risks penetrating the
transversalis fascia and peritoneum. Small intestine loops may be
present at this level. Classified as a point requiring extra caution
for depth control.

**Inguinal canal structures:** the deep inguinal ring and the round
ligament (with accompanying ilioinguinal nerve) are at or near this
location. Overly deep or laterally-angled needling could irritate the
round ligament or nerve trunk.

**Heavy menstrual bleeding:** use with caution during menstruation
given the point's Qi-moving action in the lower burner.

---

## EX-UE — Upper Extremity (上肢部)

### EX-UE1 — Trửu Tiêm (肘尖, Zhǒujiān)

**Evidence tier: C (Minimum Viable Entry)**

"Elbow Tip" (肘 = elbow + 尖 = tip/apex). On the posterior aspect of
the elbow, at the tip of the olecranon process of the ulna when the
elbow is flexed. A primarily moxibustion point — needling is
difficult and generally not practiced at this bony prominence.
Historical primary indication is scrofula (瘰疬, luǒlì, cervical
lymphadenitis/tuberculosis), an unusual indication for an elbow point
explained by TCM empiricism rather than segmental logic.

#### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location: at the tip of the olecranon, with the elbow
flexed. Bilateral.

The olecranon is the proximal bony prominence of the ulna forming the
point of the elbow. In full flexion, the olecranon tip is the most
posterior projection of the elbow joint.

Spatial relationships:
- **HT3 (Shaohai):** medial epicondyle, medial to EX-UE1.
- **LI11 (Quchi):** lateral epicondyle area, lateral to EX-UE1.
- **TE10 (Tianjing):** 1 cun superior to the olecranon, in the
  depression when elbow is flexed.

Needle depth: moxibustion primary. If needled: 0.3-0.5 cun oblique
into periosteum — generally not recommended due to bony location.

#### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- **Posterior cutaneous nerve of the forearm** (branch of radial
  nerve, C5-C8): sensory to the posterior elbow skin.
- **Ulnar nerve:** passes in the cubital tunnel (ulnar groove) just
  medial to the olecranon. PROXIMITY RISK.
- **Medial cutaneous nerve of the forearm (C8-T1):** contributes
  sensory branches to the medial elbow region.

Spinal segments: C5-T1 (brachial plexus).

The olecranon tip is covered only by skin, subcutaneous tissue, and
the triceps tendon insertion — minimal soft tissue between the
surface and bone.
→ Cross-ref: A01 S2

#### Phân loại huyệt

Extra point (经外奇穴): EX-UE1. Not on any meridian.
No Five Shu, Yuan-Source, Luo, Xi-Cleft, or special designations.

#### Cross-tradition mapping

TCM acupoint: EX-UE1 Zhoujian. Functions: dissipates lumps and
nodules, treats scrofula. ✓
Trigger point: the olecranon tip is a bony landmark, not a muscular
trigger point site. ✗
Ashi: the olecranon can be tender in olecranon bursitis, but
EX-UE1's primary indication is systemic (scrofula), not local
pain. ✗

**4-8:** No correspondence for Marma, Head zone, Anatomy Trains,
Neurogenic, or Cupping. ✗

**Convergence score: 1/8** (TCM ✓). LOW — primarily a TCM
empirical-tradition point.

#### Chỉ định truyền thống

- Primary: scrofula (瘰疬, cervical lymph node tuberculosis) —
  moxibustion at the elbow tip for neck masses is a classical TCM
  empirical treatment
- Secondary: carbuncle, abscess, intestinal carbuncle (肠痈)
- Moxa: 3-7 cones direct. Some sources specify number of cones equal
  to the number of scrofula nodules

#### Bằng chứng hiện đại

No systematic review or RCT specific to EX-UE1. The classical
indication (scrofula/TB lymphadenitis) is managed with
anti-tuberculosis chemotherapy in modern medicine.

#### An toàn

**Ulnar nerve proximity (CRITICAL):** the ulnar nerve lies in the
cubital tunnel immediately medial to the olecranon. If needling is
attempted, angle AWAY from the medial side. Avoid deep or medial
insertion.

**Bony surface:** minimal soft tissue at olecranon tip. Moxibustion
is the traditional and safer technique at this location.

**Pregnancy:** NOT specifically contraindicated.

---

### EX-UE2 — Nhị Bạch (二白, Èrbái)

**Evidence tier: C (Minimum Viable Entry)**

"Two White" (二 = two + 白 = white). Two points on the volar aspect
of the forearm, 4 cun proximal to the wrist crease, on either side
of the tendon of flexor carpi radialis. Primary indication is
hemorrhoids and rectal prolapse — a DISTAL treatment paradigm
accessing the anorectum through upper-limb points.

#### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location: 4 cun above the transverse wrist crease, on
both sides of the tendon of flexor carpi radialis. Bilateral forearm.

Two points per forearm:
- Radial point: radial side of flexor carpi radialis tendon
- Ulnar point: ulnar side of flexor carpi radialis tendon

Spatial relationships:
- **PC5 (Jianshi):** on the Pericardium meridian, 3 cun above wrist
  crease, between palmaris longus and flexor carpi radialis — 1 cun
  distal and slightly ulnar.
- **PC4 (Ximen):** 5 cun above wrist crease — 1 cun proximal.

Needle depth: 0.5-1.0 cun (~1.2-2.5 cm) perpendicular.

#### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- **Median nerve (C6-T1):** lies deep between flexor digitorum
  superficialis and flexor digitorum profundus at this level.
- **Lateral cutaneous nerve of the forearm** (musculocutaneous
  branch, C5-C6): superficial sensory to the radial volar forearm.

Spinal segments: C6-T1 (median nerve territory).
→ Cross-ref: A01 S2

#### Phân loại huyệt

Extra point (经外奇穴): EX-UE2. Not on any meridian.
No special designations.

#### Cross-tradition mapping

TCM acupoint: EX-UE2 Erbai. Functions: treats hemorrhoids, promotes
rectal lifting (提肛). ✓
Trigger point: no specific TrP at this forearm location for
anorectal referral. ✗
Ashi: not a palpation-based tender point. ✗

**4-8:** No correspondence. ✗

**Convergence score: 1/8** (TCM ✓). LOW.

#### Chỉ định truyền thống

- Primary: hemorrhoids (痔疮, zhìchuāng) — both internal and external
- Secondary: rectal prolapse (脱肛, tuōgāng)
- Forearm pain (local indication)
- Combined with BL57 (Chengshan), GV20 (Baihui) for hemorrhoids

#### Bằng chứng hiện đại

No systematic review or major RCT specific to EX-UE2. The distal
treatment paradigm (forearm → rectum/anus) has no established modern
mechanism.

#### An toàn

**Median nerve:** lies deep at this level. Standard depth (0.5-1.0
cun) should not reach the median nerve, but avoid excessive depth.

**Pregnancy:** NOT specifically contraindicated.

---

### EX-UE3 — Trung Tuyền (中泉, Zhōngquán)

**Evidence tier: C (Minimum Viable Entry)**

"Central Spring" (中 = center/middle + 泉 = spring/fountain). On the
dorsal wrist, in the depression between TE4 (Yangchi) and LI5
(Yangxi), between the extensor digitorum communis and extensor
pollicis longus tendons. A wrist dorsum point for chest and gastric
pain.

#### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location: on the dorsum of the wrist, in the depression
radial to the tendon of extensor digitorum communis, between TE4
(Yangchi) and LI5 (Yangxi). At the level of the dorsal wrist crease.

Spatial relationships:
- **TE4 (Yangchi):** ulnar to EX-UE3, in the depression ulnar to
  the extensor digitorum tendon. Yuan-Source of Triple Energizer.
- **LI5 (Yangxi):** radial to EX-UE3, in the anatomical snuffbox.

Needle depth: 0.3-0.5 cun (~0.7-1.2 cm) perpendicular.

#### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- **Posterior interosseous nerve** (deep branch of radial nerve,
  C7-C8): motor to wrist extensors, sensory to dorsal wrist capsule.
- **Superficial branch of radial nerve (C6-C7):** sensory to dorsal
  wrist skin.

Spinal segments: C6-C8.
→ Cross-ref: A01 S2

#### Phân loại huyệt

Extra point (经外奇穴): EX-UE3. Not on any meridian.
No special designations.

#### Cross-tradition mapping

TCM acupoint: EX-UE3 Zhongquan. Functions: regulates Qi, unbinds
the chest, harmonizes the stomach. ✓
Trigger point: no specific TrP at the dorsal wrist. ✗
Ashi: not a characteristic tender point. ✗

**4-8:** No correspondence. ✗

**Convergence score: 1/8** (TCM ✓). LOW.

#### Chỉ định truyền thống

- Primary: chest pain, gastric pain, diaphragmatic spasm (hiccup)
- Secondary: dorsal wrist pain (local)
- Combined with PC6 (Neiguan) for chest and gastric complaints

#### Bằng chứng hiện đại

No systematic review or RCT specific to EX-UE3.

#### An toàn

**Low risk.** Shallow needling on the dorsal wrist. Avoid extensor
tendons — needle between tendons.

**Pregnancy:** NOT specifically contraindicated.

---

### EX-UE4 — Trung Khôi (中魁, Zhōngkuí)

**Evidence tier: C (Minimum Viable Entry)**

"Middle Chief" (中 = middle + 魁 = chief/head/leader). On the dorsum
of the middle finger, at the midpoint of the proximal interphalangeal
(PIP) joint transverse crease. Primarily a moxibustion point for
nausea and hiccup — one of the "finger joint" extra points accessing
digital nerve terminals for systemic indications through distal
stimulation.

#### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location: on the dorsum of the middle finger, at the
midpoint of the PIP joint crease. Bilateral.

Spatial relationships:
- **EX-UE5 (Dagukong):** on the thumb IP joint — analogous position
  on a different digit.
- **EX-UE6 (Xiaogukong):** on the little finger PIP joint —
  analogous position on the ulnar side.

Needle depth: moxibustion primary. If needled: 0.1-0.2 cun shallow
oblique or prick to bleed.

#### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- **Dorsal digital nerves** from the superficial branch of the
  radial nerve (radial side) and dorsal branch of the ulnar nerve
  (ulnar side — variable for middle finger).
- **Proper palmar digital nerves** (from median nerve): extend
  dorsally over the PIP joint.

Spinal segments: C7-C8 (middle finger).
→ Cross-ref: A01 S2

#### Phân loại huyệt

Extra point (经外奇穴): EX-UE4. Not on any meridian.
No special designations.

#### Cross-tradition mapping

TCM acupoint: EX-UE4 Zhongkui. Functions: harmonizes the stomach,
descends rebellious Qi, stops hiccup. ✓
Trigger point: no TrP at the dorsal PIP joint. ✗
Ashi: not a tender point. ✗

**4-8:** No correspondence. ✗

**Convergence score: 1/8** (TCM ✓). LOW.

#### Chỉ định truyền thống

- Primary: nausea, vomiting, hiccup (呃逆, è nì)
- Secondary: epistaxis, toothache (distal)
- Primarily treated with moxibustion (3-5 cones direct) or pressure

#### Bằng chứng hiện đại

No systematic review or RCT specific to EX-UE4.

#### An toàn

**Low risk.** Bony prominence — minimal soft tissue. Moxibustion
is the standard technique. Avoid needling directly into the joint
space.

**Pregnancy:** NOT specifically contraindicated.

---

### EX-UE5 — Đại Cốt Không (大骨空, Dàgǔkōng)

**Evidence tier: C (Minimum Viable Entry)**

"Large Bone Hollow" (大 = large + 骨 = bone + 空 = empty/hollow). On
the dorsum of the thumb, at the midpoint of the interphalangeal (IP)
joint transverse crease. Paired conceptually with EX-UE6 (Xiaogukong,
"Small Bone Hollow" on the little finger). Both are traditionally
indicated for eye disorders — a distal treatment paradigm using
digital points for ophthalmic conditions.

#### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location: on the dorsum of the thumb, at the midpoint
of the IP joint crease. Bilateral.

The thumb has only one interphalangeal joint (unlike other fingers
with PIP and DIP). The point is on the dorsal (extensor) surface.

Spatial relationships:
- **LU11 (Shaoshang):** at the radial nail corner of the thumb.
  Jing-Well of the Lung meridian.
- **EX-UE4 (Zhongkui):** analogous point on the middle finger PIP.
- **EX-UE6 (Xiaogukong):** paired "small" counterpart on the little
  finger.

Needle depth: moxibustion primary. If needled: 0.1-0.2 cun shallow
oblique.

#### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- **Dorsal digital nerve of the thumb** from the superficial branch
  of the radial nerve (C6-C7).
- **Proper palmar digital nerve of the thumb** from the median nerve
  (C6-C7): extends dorsally over the IP joint.

Spinal segments: C6-C7 (thumb).
→ Cross-ref: A01 S2

#### Phân loại huyệt

Extra point (经外奇穴): EX-UE5. Not on any meridian.
Paired with EX-UE6 (Xiaogukong) as the "large-small bone hollow"
set — thumb (large) and little finger (small), both for eye
disorders.

#### Cross-tradition mapping

TCM acupoint: EX-UE5 Dagukong. Functions: brightens the eyes
(明目), stops tearing. ✓
Trigger point: no TrP at the dorsal thumb IP joint. ✗
Ashi: not a tender point. ✗

**4-8:** No correspondence. ✗

**Convergence score: 1/8** (TCM ✓). LOW.

#### Chỉ định truyền thống

- Primary: eye disorders — pterygium (翳, yì), cataracts (目翳),
  excessive tearing (迎风流泪)
- Secondary: eye pain, redness
- Primarily treated with moxibustion (3-5 cones)

#### Bằng chứng hiện đại

No systematic review or RCT specific to EX-UE5.

#### An toàn

**Low risk.** Bony prominence with minimal soft tissue.

**Pregnancy:** NOT specifically contraindicated.

---

### EX-UE6 — Tiểu Cốt Không (小骨空, Xiǎogǔkōng)

**Evidence tier: C (Minimum Viable Entry)**

"Small Bone Hollow" (小 = small + 骨 = bone + 空 = empty/hollow). On
the dorsum of the little finger, at the midpoint of the proximal
interphalangeal (PIP) joint transverse crease. Paired with EX-UE5
(Dagukong) — the thumb (large) and little finger (small) "bone
hollow" pair, both indicated for eye disorders. EX-UE6 additionally
treats sore throat, reflecting the little finger's connection to the
Heart (HT) and Small Intestine (SI) meridians that traverse the
throat in TCM channel theory.

#### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location: on the dorsum of the little finger, at the
midpoint of the PIP joint crease. Bilateral.

Spatial relationships:
- **HT9 (Shaochong):** at the radial nail corner of the little
  finger. Jing-Well of Heart.
- **SI1 (Shaoze):** at the ulnar nail corner of the little finger.
  Jing-Well of Small Intestine.
- **EX-UE4 (Zhongkui):** analogous point on the middle finger PIP.

Needle depth: moxibustion primary. If needled: 0.1-0.2 cun shallow
oblique.

#### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- **Dorsal digital nerve of the little finger** from the dorsal
  branch of the ulnar nerve (C8-T1).
- **Proper palmar digital nerve** from the ulnar nerve (C8-T1).

Spinal segments: C8-T1.
→ Cross-ref: A01 S2

#### Phân loại huyệt

Extra point (经外奇穴): EX-UE6. Not on any meridian.
Paired with EX-UE5 (Dagukong).

#### Cross-tradition mapping

TCM acupoint: EX-UE6 Xiaogukong. Functions: brightens the eyes,
clears the throat. ✓
Trigger point: no TrP at the dorsal little finger PIP joint. ✗
Ashi: not a tender point. ✗

**4-8:** No correspondence. ✗

**Convergence score: 1/8** (TCM ✓). LOW.

#### Chỉ định truyền thống

- Primary: eye disorders (same indications as EX-UE5)
- Secondary: sore throat, toothache
- Combined with EX-UE5 (Dagukong) for eye conditions

#### Bằng chứng hiện đại

No systematic review or RCT specific to EX-UE6.

#### An toàn

**Low risk.** Bony prominence with minimal soft tissue.

**Pregnancy:** NOT specifically contraindicated.

---

### EX-UE7 — Yêu Thống Điểm (腰痛点, Yāotòng Diǎn)

**Evidence tier: B (Standard Entry)**

"Lumbar Pain Point" (腰 = lumbar + 痛 = pain + 点 = point). TWO
points on the dorsum of the hand: one between the 2nd and 3rd
metacarpals, one between the 4th and 5th metacarpals — at the motor
points of the dorsal interosseous muscles. A DISTAL treatment point
for acute lumbar sprain, characteristically used with 动气针法
(dòngqì zhēnfǎ, "movement acupuncture"): the patient actively moves
the lumbar spine during needle retention. One of the few extra points
with a dedicated systematic review protocol and point-specific (not
combination) clinical trials. The modern name "Yaotongdian" narrows
the original broader indications to lumbar pain alone — the point's
classical names were 威灵 (Weiling) and 精灵 (Jingling).

#### Lịch sử và danh pháp (History and Nomenclature)

First documented by **Gong Tingxian** (龚廷贤, c.1538-1635), a Ming
dynasty physician with the courtesy name "Yunlin" (云林) and the
honorific "Yilin Zhuangyuan" (医林状元, "top scholar of the medical
world"). Appears in his text **Xiao'er Tuina Fangmai Huoying Miqi
Quanshu** (《小儿推拿方脉活婴秘旨全书》, published 1604 CE) — notably
a **pediatric tuina (infant massage) manual**, not an adult
acupuncture text.

In the original source, the points had separate names:
- **威灵 (Wēilíng, "Majestic Spirit"):** between 2nd/3rd
  metacarpals, radial side of the 2nd extensor tendon
- **精灵 (Jīnglíng, "Essential Spirit"):** between 4th/5th
  metacarpals, ulnar side of the 4th extensor tendon

Original indications were BROAD: lumbar pain, toothache, headache,
infant convulsions, phlegm-cough, wrist/upper-limb pain, dyspnea,
dizziness, tinnitus, and dorsal hand swelling. Lumbar pain was only
ONE of many. The narrowing to "Yaotongdian" (腰痛点/腰痛穴, "Lumbar
Pain Point") is a **modern clinical development** that elevated one
indication among many to the point's defining identity.

Hán-Việt: Yêu Thống Điểm (腰痛点). Yêu = lumbar, Thống = pain,
Điểm = point.

#### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location: two points on the dorsum of the hand:
- **Point 1 (Weiling):** between the 2nd and 3rd metacarpal bones,
  approximately at the midpoint of the 2nd metacarpal bone.
- **Point 2 (Jingling):** between the 4th and 5th metacarpal bones,
  approximately at the midpoint of the 4th metacarpal bone.
Both bilateral (total: 4 needling sites across both hands).

Spatial relationships:
- **LI4 (Hegu):** in the 1st dorsal interosseous, between 1st and
  2nd metacarpals — adjacent to Point 1 but in a different
  interosseous space.
- **TE3 (Zhongzhu):** between 4th and 5th metacarpals, proximal to
  the MCP joint — distal to Point 2.
- **EX-UE8 (Wailaogong):** between 2nd and 3rd metacarpals, at the
  MCP joint level — distal to Point 1.

Needle depth: 0.5-0.8 cun (~1.5-2.0 cm) perpendicular or oblique at
45° toward the palm center.

#### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- **Deep branch of the ulnar nerve (C8-T1):** motor innervation to
  ALL FOUR dorsal interosseous muscles. Primary nerve accessed at
  motor-point depth.
- **Superficial branch of the radial nerve (C6-C7):** sensory to
  the dorsal skin over the radial 3.5 digits — covers Point 1.
- **Dorsal branch of the ulnar nerve (C8-T1):** sensory to the
  dorsal skin over the ulnar 1.5 digits — covers Point 2.

The two points straddle the hand's radial-ulnar sensory watershed:
Point 1 is in radial nerve territory, Point 2 in ulnar nerve
territory. Both share the same deep motor supply (ulnar nerve,
C8-T1).

Spinal segments: C8-T1 (motor), C6-C8 (sensory).

Vascular: dorsal metacarpal arteries (from dorsal carpal arch).
Dorsal venous network of the hand overlies these points.

Muscle layers (superficial → deep):
- Skin → subcutaneous tissue
- Extensor tendons (digitorum communis crosses superficially)
- Dorsal interosseous muscles — **Point 1 accesses the 1st dorsal
  interosseous** (the largest, between 2nd-3rd metacarpals),
  **Point 2 accesses the 3rd or 4th dorsal interosseous** (between
  4th-5th metacarpals). Needling to motor-point depth (0.5-0.8 cun)
  reaches the interosseous muscle belly.
→ Cross-ref: A01 S2

#### Phân loại huyệt (Point Category Membership)

Extra point (经外奇穴): EX-UE7 in WHO nomenclature. Not on any
meridian.

Five Shu: not applicable. Yuan-Source: not applicable.
Luo-Connecting: not applicable. Xi-Cleft: not applicable.
Back-Shu: not applicable. Front-Mu: not applicable.
Eight Confluent: not applicable. Hui-Meeting: not applicable.

Special designations:
- **Premier distal point for acute lumbar sprain:** the most
  frequently cited single-point intervention for acute low back pain
  in TCM emergency acupuncture protocols.
- **Movement acupuncture (动气针法) exemplar:** the point's clinical
  technique defines the movement-acupuncture paradigm — needling at
  a distal site while the patient actively mobilizes the affected
  area.
- **Two-point laterality system:** the Weiling (radial) and Jingling
  (ulnar) points are traditionally matched to contralateral sides of
  lumbar pain, allowing targeted unilateral treatment.
→ Cross-ref: B01 S1.3.3

#### Cross-tradition mapping

**1. TCM acupoint:** EX-UE7 Yaotongdian (腰痛点, "Lumbar Pain
Point"). Originally named Weiling (威灵) and Jingling (精灵) with
broad indications; modern usage narrowed to acute lumbar sprain.
Functions: activates blood and Qi circulation in the lumbar region,
relieves acute lumbar pain. The point is NOT on any TCM meridian —
purely empirical, one of the clearest examples of clinical
observation driving point identification independent of channel
theory. ✓

**2. Trigger point:** dorsal interosseous muscles have documented
trigger points (Fernández-de-las-Peñas et al., PMID 26000673:
"Referred pain patterns of the third and fourth dorsal interosseous
muscles"). However, the referred pain territories are LOCAL to the
hand, fingers, and wrist — mimicking carpal tunnel syndrome or
C7/C8 radiculopathy patterns. **No source documents referral to the
lumbar region.** The anatomical TrP exists at the needling location
but the functional correlation (lumbar pain) is absent from TrP
theory. 🟡

**3. Ashi correspondence:** not a palpation-based tender point. The
point is located by anatomical measurement, not by tenderness. ✗

**4. Marma point:** no corresponding Marma at the dorsal
intermetacarpal spaces. ✗

**5. Head zone:** C8-T1 dermatome (dorsal hand). The lumbar spine
corresponds to L1-S1 dermatomes — NO segmental overlap. This
represents the single largest explanatory gap in Yaotongdian's
mechanism: the therapeutic target is OUTSIDE its segmental
territory entirely. ✗
→ Cross-ref: A07

**6. Anatomy Trains:** the dorsal interosseous muscles are intrinsic
hand muscles without myofascial continuity to the lumbar region via
any named line. ✗

**7. Neurogenic inflammatory spot:** no data. ✗

**8. Cupping point:** not a cupping site. ✗

**Convergence score: 2/8** (TCM ✓, Trigger point 🟡). LOW — the
extremely low convergence reflects the fundamental challenge of
Yaotongdian: it is a DISTAL treatment point whose mechanism cannot
be explained by segmental convergence, trigger point referral, or
any cross-tradition mapping system. Its effectiveness rests on TCM
clinical empiricism and possibly supraspinal pain-modulation
pathways that bypass the segmental logic underlying most
cross-tradition correspondences.

#### Chỉ định truyền thống (DATA — ghi nhận, không endorse as mechanism)

Nguồn cổ (Gong Tingxian, 1604 CE — original broad indications):
- Lumbar pain (腰痛)
- Toothache, headache
- Infant acute/chronic convulsions
- Phlegm-cough, wrist/upper-limb pain
- Dyspnea, dizziness, tinnitus
- Dorsal hand swelling

Modern usage (narrowed):
- **Acute lumbar sprain (急性腰扭伤)** — the overwhelming modern
  clinical focus. Used for sudden-onset lower back pain from
  twisting, lifting, or awkward movement.
- Chronic lumbar pain (secondary)

Clinical combinations:
- **EX-UE7 alone** (point-specific intervention): unusually for
  acupuncture, Yaotongdian is frequently used as a SOLE treatment
  point without additional points — rare in a field dominated by
  multi-point protocols.
- **EX-UE7 + active lumbar movement (动气针法):** the standard
  protocol involves active lumbar flexion/extension/rotation during
  needle retention.

#### Bằng chứng hiện đại

**Systematic review protocol (PMC7850650, Medicine 2021):**
Ma et al. published a registered protocol for SR/MA of acupuncture at
Yaotongdian for acute lumbar sprain. Searched 8 databases (PubMed,
EMBASE, Cochrane, CNKI, Wanfang, VIP). Primary outcome: clinical
cure rate (complete pain relief + normal ROM). Secondary: VAS pain,
lumbar ROM, Oswestry Disability Index. **No completed results
published** as of the search date — the protocol remains unfulfilled.
🟡 Plausible (protocol registered, confirming sufficient literature
to warrant SR/MA, but results not yet available)

**Controlled study — acupuncture + tuina vs tuina (2015):**
N=180 (Journal of Acupuncture and Tuina Science, Springer,
13:171-175). Yaotongdian acupuncture + tuina manipulation vs tuina
alone, 3 sessions. Observation group: 98.0% cure rate, 100% total
effective rate. Control group: 92.7% cure rate, 97.6% effective rate.
Difference NOT statistically significant (P>0.05), but the
acupuncture + tuina group achieved pain relief MORE QUICKLY.
🟡 Plausible (largest available controlled study, no sham comparison,
added value was speed of onset rather than final cure rate)

**Acupuncture-movement therapy — paradigm evidence:**
RCT (PMID 26946614) using acupuncture + active movement for acute
lumbar sprain showed persistent pain relief and improved lumbar ROM
vs static acupuncture. This study used Yintang + lumbar exercise
rather than Yaotongdian, but validates the movement-during-needling
technique class.
🟡 Plausible (supports the 动气针法 paradigm but not Yaotongdian
specifically)

**Overall assessment:** Yaotongdian has a dedicated SR/MA protocol
confirming research interest and sufficient literature, but the
completed evidence base is modest. The largest controlled study
(n=180) showed no significant advantage over tuina alone except in
speed of onset. Clinical consensus substantially exceeds published
evidence quality.

#### Cơ chế giải thích

**The explanatory gap:** EX-UE7's mechanism poses a fundamental
challenge for segmental acupuncture theory. The point (dorsal hand,
C8-T1) is COMPLETELY OUTSIDE the segmental territory of the lumbar
spine (L1-S1). Gate control theory (Melzack & Wall 1965) requires
shared segmental input, which does not exist between C8 and L1-S1.

**Supraspinal descending modulation — most plausible pathway:**
Needling at the dorsal interosseous motor points (rich
proprioceptive and nociceptive afferent density) → spinal cord
C8-T1 → ascending pathways to midbrain periaqueductal gray (PAG)
and hypothalamus → activation of descending pain-modulation
pathways (PAG → rostroventromedial medulla → dorsolateral
funiculus) → inhibition of nociceptive transmission at lumbar
spinal levels (L1-S1). This pathway is NON-SEGMENTAL — it operates
through brainstem relay, not dermatomal convergence.

**Movement-facilitated disinhibition:** the 动气针法 technique
(active lumbar movement during needling) may work through a
mechanism independent of the acupuncture itself: proprioceptive
input from active movement interrupts reflexive muscle guarding and
spasm (the primary cause of pain in acute lumbar sprain). The
combination of peripheral nociceptive stimulation (needle) +
cortical motor engagement (active movement) + proprioceptive
feedback (spinal movement) creates a multi-level input pattern
that may override the protective spasm cycle.

**Master Tung tradition interpretation:** in Tung's Acupuncture
(董氏奇穴), the distal-treatment paradigm is central — hand points
treat contralateral body regions through empirically observed
correspondences that do not map to dermatomal theory. The system
asserts empirical observation validated through clinical repetition,
not a specific neural pathway.

**Confidence:** 🔴 Hypothesis. No mechanistic study (fMRI,
electrophysiology, fiber tracing) has been conducted for EX-UE7.
The supraspinal pathway is plausible from general pain-modulation
literature but not demonstrated for this specific point.

#### Kỹ thuật kích thích

**Needling (standard):** perpendicular or 45° oblique toward the
palm, 0.5-0.8 cun (~1.5-2.0 cm). Strong manipulation (lifting-
thrusting, rotating) to elicit De Qi — typically described as
distending sensation radiating along the dorsal hand.

**Movement acupuncture (动气针法, dòngqì zhēnfǎ):**
The CHARACTERISTIC technique for Yaotongdian — the point's primary
clinical identity:
1. Insert needles at EX-UE7 bilateral (or contralateral to the
   affected side)
2. While retaining needles, instruct patient to actively perform
   lumbar movements: flexion/extension, lateral bending, rotation
3. Patient performs movements progressively — within pain tolerance,
   increasing ROM as spasm releases
4. Treatment duration: typically 10-20 minutes with intermittent
   manual needle stimulation between movement sets

The technique was formalized within the Tung's Acupuncture (董氏奇穴)
lineage, documented by first-generation students of Dong Jingchang
(董景昌) in Taiwan — particularly Yang Weijie (杨维杰) in the 1975
edition of Zhenjiu Jingwei (《针灸经纬》).

**Laterality matching (traditional):**
- Weiling point (2nd-3rd metacarpal) — for one side of lumbar pain
- Jingling point (4th-5th metacarpal) — for the opposite side
- Some practitioners needle contralateral only; others bilateral

**No moxibustion:** unlike many other extra points, moxibustion is
NOT the standard technique for Yaotongdian. Efficacy is specifically
associated with needling + movement.

#### Dynamic acupoint properties

**Dynamic activation through movement (unique paradigm):**
Unlike points that exhibit dynamic properties through pathological
sensitization (e.g., Dingchuan skin impedance in asthma, Dannangxue
tenderness in gallbladder disease), Yaotongdian exhibits a different
form of dynamism: its therapeutic effect is ACTIVATED BY PATIENT
MOVEMENT during needling.

In standard acupuncture, the point is the active agent and the
patient is passive. In Yaotongdian's 动气针法 technique, both the
point (needle stimulus) and the patient's motor engagement (active
lumbar movement) are required for optimal effect. The therapeutic
outcome emerges from the INTERACTION between peripheral stimulation
and central motor activation.

This "dynamically activated" paradigm contrasts with the "dynamically
sensitized" paradigm seen in diagnostic points (Dannangxue/Lanweixue)
or disease-responsive points (Dingchuan impedance). Both express the
dynamic acupoint phenomenon (C04 §4) through different mechanisms:
sensitization = bottom-up (pathology creates the acupoint signal)
vs activation = top-down (patient engagement unlocks the effect).
→ Cross-ref: C04 S4

#### An toàn

**Low risk overall.** The dorsum of the hand is a safe needling area
with no major deep structures at standard depth.

**Dorsal venous network:** bruising/hematoma is the most common
minor adverse event. The dorsal veins are visible and avoidable.

**Extensor tendons:** avoid direct tendon penetration. Needle between
tendons into the interosseous muscle belly.

**Vasovagal response:** patients who are fatigued, fasted, or anxious
may be prone to vasovagal syncope with strong needle stimulation.
Treat in seated or supine position.

**Pregnancy:** NOT specifically contraindicated. The point has no
gynecological function and is distant from the abdomen/pelvis.

---

### EX-UE8 — Ngoại Lao Cung (外劳宫, Wàiláogōng)

**Evidence tier: C (Minimum Viable Entry)**

"Outer Palace of Toil" (外 = outer/external + 劳宫 = Laogong,
"Palace of Toil" = PC8). On the dorsum of the hand, directly
opposite PC8 (Laogong) on the palmar surface, between the 2nd and
3rd metacarpal bones (some sources: 3rd and 4th). Also known as
**Luozhen (落枕, "Stiff Neck/Torticollis")** — the point's primary
clinical name after its signature indication: acute stiff neck upon
waking.

#### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location: on the dorsum of the hand, opposite PC8
(Laogong), between the 2nd and 3rd metacarpal bones, at the level
of the MCP joints. Some sources place it between 3rd and 4th
metacarpals. Bilateral.

Spatial relationships:
- **PC8 (Laogong):** palmar counterpart, directly deep/volar to
  EX-UE8.
- **EX-UE7 (Yaotongdian) Point 1:** between 2nd-3rd metacarpals
  but more proximal (midshaft). EX-UE8 is more distal (near MCP).
- **LI4 (Hegu):** between 1st-2nd metacarpals, more radial.

Needle depth: 0.5-0.8 cun (~1.2-2.0 cm) perpendicular.

#### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- **Deep branch of ulnar nerve (C8-T1):** motor to the 2nd dorsal
  interosseous at this level.
- **Superficial branch of radial nerve (C6-C7):** dorsal cutaneous.

Spinal segments: C7-T1.
→ Cross-ref: A01 S2

#### Phân loại huyệt

Extra point (经外奇穴): EX-UE8. Not on any meridian.
Also designated by its clinical name: **Luozhen** (落枕穴, "Stiff
Neck Point").

#### Cross-tradition mapping

TCM acupoint: EX-UE8 Wailaogong / Luozhen. Functions: relaxes
sinews and tendons, treats stiff neck. ✓
Trigger point: dorsal interosseous TrPs present at this general
area; referred pain is to the hand/fingers, not the neck. 🟡
Ashi: not a standard tender point. ✗

**4-8:** No correspondence for Marma, Head zone (C7-T1 hand ≠ C2-C4
cervical neck), Anatomy Trains, Neurogenic, or Cupping. ✗

**Convergence score: 2/8** (TCM ✓, Trigger point 🟡). LOW.

#### Chỉ định truyền thống

- Primary: acute stiff neck / torticollis (落枕, luòzhěn) —
  waking with stiff/painful neck from sleeping awkwardly
- Secondary: hand/finger pain, headache
- Needle while patient rotates neck (similar 动气针法 technique as
  EX-UE7 — distal hand point + active movement of the affected area)

#### Bằng chứng hiện đại

No systematic review or major RCT specific to EX-UE8. Widely used in
Chinese clinical practice for acute torticollis but lacks
high-quality controlled evidence.

#### An toàn

**Low risk.** Standard dorsal hand needling precautions. Avoid dorsal
venous network. Needle between extensor tendons.

**Pregnancy:** NOT specifically contraindicated.
