## EX-LE — Lower Extremity (下肢部)

### EX-LE1 — Khoan Cốt (髋骨, Kuāngǔ)

**Evidence tier: C (Minimum Viable Entry)**

"Hip Bone" (髋 = hip + 骨 = bone) — a misleadingly named extra point
located NOT at the hip but at the DISTAL THIGH flanking ST34 (Liangqiu).
The name references the Stomach channel's trajectory from hip (ASIS) to
knee, not the point's actual anatomical location. Kuangu is a PAIRED
point: two sub-points per leg (medial and lateral to ST34), 4 total
bilaterally. First documented in Zhenjiu Dacheng (Yang Jizhou, 1601 CE)
as treating leg pain with moxibustion.

#### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO): EX-LE1. Two paired points per leg: 1.5
cun medial and 1.5 cun lateral to ST34 (Liangqiu), approximately 2
cun above the superior border of the patella.

Zhenjiu Dacheng (1601): "四穴，在梁丘两旁，各开一寸五分，两足共四穴。
治腿痛，灸七壮" — "Four points, 1.5 cun to either side of Liangqiu,
four points total on both legs. Treats leg pain. Apply 7 cones of
moxibustion."

Spatial relationships:
- **ST34 (Liangqiu):** Xi-Cleft of Stomach, 2 cun above superolateral
  patellar border, between rectus femoris and vastus lateralis.
  Kuangu flanks ST34 medially and laterally.
- **EX-LE2 (Heding):** midpoint of superior patellar border — ~2 cun
  distal to Kuangu.
- **SP10 (Xuehai):** 2 cun above medial patella border on vastus
  medialis — near Kuangu's medial sub-point.

Needle depth: 0.5–1.5 cun perpendicular or angled toward the knee.
Moxibustion primary (7 cones per Zhenjiu Dacheng).

**Naming note:** 髋骨 literally means "hip bone." Some sources
incorrectly place Kuangu near the ASIS. The WHO standardization
follows Zhenjiu Dacheng's description at the distal thigh flanking
ST34. The name indicates the point's therapeutic reach across the
lower limb from hip to knee rather than its own anatomical position.

#### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- **Lateral sub-point:** lateral femoral cutaneous nerve (L2-L3) and
  motor branches of femoral nerve to vastus lateralis.
- **Medial sub-point:** anterior/intermediate femoral cutaneous nerve
  (L2-L3) and motor branches to vastus medialis.
- Spinal segments: L2-L4 (femoral nerve territory).
→ Cross-ref: A01 S2

Vascular: descending branches of lateral circumflex femoral artery
(lateral sub-point), muscular branches of femoral artery (medial).

Muscle: lateral sub-point on vastus lateralis, medial sub-point on
vastus medialis — flanking the same femoral shaft at the quadriceps
tendon formation zone.

#### Phân loại huyệt

Extra point (经外奇穴): EX-LE1. Not on any meridian.
Multi-point group: 2 per leg (medial + lateral), 4 total bilaterally.
No special designations.

#### Cross-tradition mapping

TCM acupoint: EX-LE1 Kuangu. Functions: opens channels, relieves
pain (通经止痛). ✓
Trigger point: vastus lateralis and vastus medialis distal TrPs in
the corresponding territories. 🟡
Ashi: tender in anterior thigh/quadriceps strain conditions. 🟡

**4-8:** No correspondence (Marma ✗, Head zone ✗ purely somatic,
Anatomy Trains ✗, Neurogenic ✗, Cupping ✗).

**Convergence score: 3/8** (TCM ✓, Trigger point 🟡, Ashi 🟡). LOW.

#### Chỉ định truyền thống

- Primary: knee pain, knee swelling and stiffness, leg pain
- Secondary: lower limb weakness, atrophy, difficulty walking,
  Wind-Damp Bi syndrome (风湿痹证)
- Primarily treated with MOXIBUSTION (7 cones per Zhenjiu Dacheng)

#### Bằng chứng hiện đại

No systematic review or RCT specific to EX-LE1.

#### An toàn

**Low risk.** Adequate muscle cushion over femur at standard depth.
Avoid deep perpendicular insertion on the medial sub-point — proximity
to femoral neurovascular bundle at adductor canal level.

**Pregnancy:** NOT specifically contraindicated.

---

### EX-LE2 — Hạc Đỉnh (鹤顶, Hèdǐng)

**Evidence tier: B (Standard Entry)**

"Crane's Summit" (鹤 = crane + 顶 = summit/vertex) — named with dual
logic: the patella atop the knee resembles a crane's head projecting
forward on its long leg, and the point's primary indication is "Crane's
Knee Wind" (鹤膝风, hèxī fēng), where knee swelling with emaciated
thigh and calf makes the leg resemble a crane's limb. A well-established
supplementary local point for knee conditions, consistently SECONDARY
to the Xiyan pair (ST35 + EX-LE4) in knee OA protocols but with
sensitization data confirming its clinical relevance.

#### Lịch sử và danh pháp (History and Nomenclature)

**Waike Dacheng** (外科大成, "Great Compendium of External Medicine,"
Qi Kun 祁坤, 1665 CE, Qing dynasty): the most prominent description
of Heding. Describes treating Crane's Knee Wind and beriberi at this
point, calling it **"a secret method not recorded in other books"**
(秘法他书不载). Prescribes 7 cones direct moxibustion. Also uses the
alternative name **Xiding** (膝顶, "Knee Top").

The Waike Dacheng describes Crane's Knee Wind: "swelling on both sides
of the knees, alternating chills and fever, pain like a tiger bite,
with thighs becoming thinner while knees enlarge." The modern
equivalent is likely chronic inflammatory/infectious arthritis
(tuberculosis arthritis historically, rheumatoid arthritis).

**Dongyi Bogam** (东医宝鉴, "Precious Mirror of Eastern Medicine," Heo
Jun 허준, Korea, 1613 CE): references Heding for "paralysis and
weakness of both feet" using moxibustion.

**Zhenjiu Jicheng** (针灸集成, "Compendium of Acupuncture and
Moxibustion," Qing dynasty compilation): formally records the name
"Heding" (鹤顶) for this location.

**WHO-WPRO:** standardized as EX-LE2.

#### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): on the anterior aspect of the knee,
in the depression at the midpoint of the superior border of the patella.
Best located with knee slightly flexed to relax the quadriceps tendon
and open the suprapatellar depression.

Spatial relationships:
- **ST34 (Liangqiu):** at the superolateral patellar border, ~1-1.5
  cun lateral to Heding. Same vertical level but different mediolateral
  position: Heding = midline, ST34 = lateral border.
- **ST35 (Dubi) + EX-LE4 (Neixiyan):** at the infrapatellar border
  (lateral and medial respectively) — Heding is at the OPPOSITE pole
  of the patella, completing the vertical axis of knee access points.
- **SP10 (Xuehai):** 2 cun above medial patella border — approximately
  2 cun proximal-medial to Heding.
- **EX-LE1 (Kuangu):** flanking ST34, slightly more proximal.

Between the suprapatellar (Heding) and infrapatellar (Xiyan pair)
points, the patella is bracketed from above and below — creating a
vertical axis of knee access spanning the entire patellar extent.

Needle depth: 0.5–1.0 cun (12–25 mm) perpendicular. Alternative:
oblique insertion at 30°, 15-30 mm (Yu 2021 RCT protocol).

**Suprapatellar pouch relationship:** the suprapatellar pouch (bursa)
extends ~5-6 cm above the superior patellar border between the
quadriceps tendon and the femur, communicating directly with the knee
joint cavity in ~85% of adults (only ~15% retain a complete
suprapatellar plica separating them). At standard acupuncture depth,
perpendicular needling at Heding approaches or enters this space.

#### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- **Intermediate femoral cutaneous nerve (L2-L3):** primary cutaneous
  supply to the anterior distal thigh and suprapatellar region. An
  anterior cutaneous branch of the femoral nerve.
- **Anterior femoral cutaneous nerve branches (L2-L4):** contributing
  to the suprapatellar area.
- Deep: femoral nerve motor branches to quadriceps (vastus intermedius,
  rectus femoris) pass through this level but deeper than standard
  needle depth.
- Spinal segments: L2-L4 (femoral nerve territory). Dermatome: L3.
→ Cross-ref: A01 S2

Vascular: descending genicular artery branches (from femoral artery),
lateral and medial superior genicular arteries forming the suprapatellar
anastomosis. Great saphenous vein tributaries superficially.

Tissue layers (superficial → deep):
- Skin → subcutaneous tissue
- **Quadriceps tendon** — convergence of rectus femoris, vastus
  medialis, vastus lateralis, and vastus intermedius tendons merging
  into the common tendon inserting on the superior patellar pole.
- Suprapatellar fat pad (pre-femoral fat pad)
- **Suprapatellar pouch** — communicating with knee joint cavity

#### Phân loại huyệt (Point Category Membership)

Extra point (经外奇穴): EX-LE2. Not on any meridian.
No formal category membership in the 8 standard systems.

Functionally positioned as a SUPPLEMENTARY local knee point —
secondary to the Xiyan pair (ST35 + EX-LE4) in knee OA protocols.
The suprapatellar position provides access to the joint from ABOVE
the patella, complementing the infrapatellar access of the Xiyan pair.

#### Cross-tradition mapping

**1. TCM acupoint:** EX-LE2 Heding. Functions: activates Qi and Blood
(活气活血), benefits the knee joint (利膝关节), dispels Wind-Damp
(祛风湿), strengthens sinews (强筋). Primary: Crane's Knee Wind, knee
pain, swelling, weakness, leg paralysis, beriberi. ✓

**2. Trigger point:** quadriceps tendon/distal quadriceps TrPs.
Travell & Simons: vastus intermedius TrPs in the distal thigh refer
deep anterior knee pain and restrict knee flexion. The Heding location
overlaps with this distal quadriceps TrP zone. 🟡

**3. Ashi correspondence:** the suprapatellar midpoint is a frequent
site of spontaneous tenderness in quadriceps tendinopathy,
patellofemoral pain, suprapatellar bursitis, and knee OA with
effusion. Sensitization confirmed: Chen 2022 (PMID 35676697, n=666)
demonstrated reduced pressure pain threshold at Heding in knee OA
patients vs healthy controls, with PPT increasing after acupuncture
treatment. 🟡

**4. Marma point:** Janu Marma (जानु, "knee") — Sandhi Marma. The
anterior Janu Marma extends from the central patellar region to the
suprapatellar area (Lad & Durve 2008). Heding is at the superior
boundary of this zone — adjacent but less central than the Xiyan pair
(infrapatellar). 🟡

**5. Head zone:** L3 dermatome. No visceral correspondence — purely
musculoskeletal. ✗

**6. Anatomy Trains:** Superficial Front Line (SFL). The quadriceps
tendon at its patellar insertion is a key SFL node — the patella acts
as a sesamoid holding the SFL away from the knee fulcrum. Heding is
precisely at the SFL's entry into the patella. 🟡

**7. Neurogenic inflammatory spot:** No data for suprapatellar region
in Kim 2017 model. ✗

**8. Cupping:** not specifically indicated at the suprapatellar
midpoint. ✗

**Convergence score: 5/8** (TCM ✓, Trigger point 🟡, Ashi 🟡,
Marma 🟡, Anatomy Trains 🟡). Same convergence as ST35 (Dubi),
reflecting the shared Janu Marma zone and SFL pathway.

#### Chỉ định truyền thống (DATA — ghi nhận, không endorse as mechanism)

Nguồn cổ:
- **Waike Dacheng** (1665): Crane's Knee Wind (鹤膝风), beriberi
  (脚气). "Secret method" — 7 cones direct moxibustion.
- **Dongyi Bogam** (1613): paralysis and weakness of both feet.

Nguồn hiện đại:
- Knee osteoarthritis (supplementary local point)
- Patellofemoral pain syndrome
- Quadriceps tendinopathy
- Post-surgical knee rehabilitation (quadriceps activation)
- Almost always used in COMBINATION: typically with Xiyan pair
  (EX-LE4 + ST35) + ST36 + GB34 + SP9/SP10. Rarely used as sole
  point.
→ Cross-ref: AP-03 ST34, ST35, ST36

#### Bằng chứng hiện đại

🟡 Plausible. Heding has no point-specific evidence but consistent
protocol inclusion and emerging sensitization data:

**Systematic reviews and data mining:**
- Cai 2022 (PMC9429477): across 46 EA trials for knee OA, Heding
  ranked 10th among acupoints (5/157 mentions, 3.18%). Classified
  as "adjunctive acupoint" — grouped with LR8, GB33, and Ashi points.
  The CORE cluster = ST35, ST34, EX-LE4, SP10, GB34, SP9.
- Chen 2022 (PMC9050311): SR/MA of acupuncture for knee OA. Three
  included RCTs used Heding (Witt 2005, Lin, Foster).

**RCTs including Heding:**
- Yu 2021 (PMID 34457022, PMC8387169): n=92, 3-group comparison.
  Heding in the PROXIMAL group (EX-LE2 + GB34 + SP9). Both proximal
  and distal groups showed significant pain reduction vs sham, but
  the distal group had greater effect size (Cohen's d 1.05 vs 0.80
  for proximal).
- Chen 2022 (PMID 35676697, PMC9175460): multicenter RCT, n=666.
  13 acupoints around the knee including Heding. **Pain threshold
  assessment confirmed Heding among the top 5 high-frequency
  acupoints** in the higher pain threshold group, "highly consistent"
  with commonly recommended local knee points.
- Witt 2005 (PMID 16005336): large German RCT, n=294, used EX-LE2
  among protocol points; acupuncture superior to sham.

**Status:** secondary/adjunctive, not core — present in ~15-25% of
knee OA trials (vs 50-80% for the Xiyan pair and ST36).

#### Cơ chế giải thích

**Track A systems at Heding:**

A01 (Neural): intermediate femoral cutaneous nerve (L2-L3) + anterior
cutaneous branches → L2-L4 dorsal horn → segmental modulation. Gate
control via Aβ/Aδ activation in the dense quadriceps tendon. Complete
segmental match for knee indications — no supraspinal pathway needed.
🟢/🟡.

A03 (Fascia): quadriceps tendon = one of the densest tendinous
convergence zones in the body (4 muscle tendons merging). Needle
manipulation may release fascial tension and decompress sensitized
mechanoreceptors. 🟡.

**Suprapatellar pouch access:** in knees with effusion, needling at
Heding depth may access the suprapatellar pouch — allowing DIRECT
modulation of synovial tissue and inflammatory mediators within the
joint space. This converts Heding from a musculotendinous point to
an INTRA-ARTICULAR point, similar to the Xiyan pair's fat pad access
from below. 🟡.

**MINIMAL mechanism gap:** like ST35/EX-LE4 (Xiyan pair), Heding's
indications are entirely local. Segmental match is complete (L2-L4).
No distant organ effects to explain.

#### Kỹ thuật kích thích

Châm (B01): perpendicular insertion 0.5-1.0 cun (12-25 mm). Oblique
at 30° for 15-30 mm (Yu 2021 protocol). Knee slightly flexed to
relax the quadriceps tendon. De Qi: local soreness and distension,
may radiate into the knee joint.
→ Cross-ref: B01 S4.3

**Moxibustion (B08):** historically emphasized — the Waike Dacheng
prescribes 7 cones direct moxa as the "secret method" for Crane's
Knee Wind. Modern practice: 5-15 minutes indirect moxa. Considered
particularly appropriate for cold-type (寒) knee conditions with
stiffness.

EA: when used, typically connected with another knee point (EX-LE4
or ST35), continuous wave, 2 Hz, 30 minutes.

Acupressure (B02): firm pressure at the suprapatellar depression
with knee slightly flexed. Self-applicable for mild knee discomfort.

#### Dynamic acupoint properties

Heding demonstrates measurable dynamic behavior in knee OA:
- **Pressure pain threshold reduction:** Chen 2022 (PMID 35676697,
  n=666 multicenter RCT) confirmed that Heding shows REDUCED pressure
  pain threshold in KOA patients compared to healthy controls. After
  acupuncture treatment, PPT at Heding INCREASED — suggesting
  reversal of the disease-related sensitization. This parallels the
  fat pad sensitization documented at ST35/EX-LE4 (Dragoo 2012) but
  at the suprapatellar rather than infrapatellar level.
→ Cross-ref: C04 S4, AP-03 ST35

- Whether this meets formal C04 dynamic acupoint criteria is
  debatable (same question as ST35): knee OA creates LOCAL pathology
  at the needle site, blurring the distinction between "dynamic
  acupoint" and "tender pathological tissue."

#### An toàn

**Low-moderate risk.** The primary concern is suprapatellar pouch
penetration:

**Suprapatellar pouch access:** in non-effused knees, the pouch is
essentially collapsed — minimal risk at standard depth. In EFFUSED
knees, the pouch is distended and accessible at moderate depth
(15-25 mm). If the needle enters the joint space, the risks include
septic arthritis (surgical emergency) and hemarthrosis (especially
with anticoagulants).

**Mitigation:** in knees with significant effusion, use shallower
depth (0.3-0.5 cun, 8-12 mm) to stay in subcutaneous/tendinous
layers. Strict aseptic technique essential.

Standard infection risk with clean needle technique. Bruising from
saphenous vein tributaries minor. No major nerve injury risk at
standard depth.

**Contraindicated in acute joint infection** or knee sepsis.

**Pregnancy:** NOT specifically contraindicated.

---

### EX-LE3 — Bách Trùng Oa (百虫窝, Bǎichóngwō)

**Evidence tier: C (Minimum Viable Entry)**

"Hundred Bug Nest" (百 = hundred/many + 虫 = bug/insect/worm + 窝 =
nest/den) — a vividly named extra point 1 cun above SP10 (Xuehai,
"Sea of Blood"), on the vastus medialis. The name is a metaphor for
skin that "itches as though a hundred insects were burrowing beneath
it" — describing the formication sensation accompanying the
dermatological conditions this point treats. First documented in
Zhenjiu Dacheng (Yang Jizhou, 1601 CE). Functionally similar to SP10
but with stronger anti-pruritic specialization.

#### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO): EX-LE3. On the medial aspect of the
thigh, 3 cun above the medial superior border of the patella (= 1 cun
above SP10 Xuehai), on the vastus medialis muscle.

Located by measuring 1 cun proximal from SP10 (2 cun above medial
patellar border + 1 cun = 3 cun total). Patient supine or seated with
knee flexed. Found by palpation for tenderness as well as measurement.

Spatial relationships:
- **SP10 (Xuehai):** 1 cun distal — the primary blood-related point
  on the Spleen channel. Same muscle, same nerve territory.
- **ST34 (Liangqiu):** approximately same vertical level but LATERAL
  (superolateral patellar border vs medial thigh).
- **EX-LE1 (Kuangu, medial sub-point):** similar region.

Needle depth: 1.0–1.5 cun perpendicular into the vastus medialis
muscle belly.

#### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- **Saphenous nerve (L3-L4):** cutaneous, continuing from SP10
  territory. Emerging from the adductor canal at this level.
- **Medial femoral cutaneous nerve (L2-L3):** branch of femoral nerve,
  creating a DUAL CUTANEOUS ZONE with the saphenous — same pattern
  documented at SP10 (AP-04 SP10 entry).
- Deep: nerve to vastus medialis (femoral nerve L2-L4), motor nerve
  with intramuscular branches accessible at needling depth.
- Spinal segments: L2-L4 (femoral nerve territory).
→ Cross-ref: A01 S2, AP-04 SP10

Vascular: great saphenous vein superficially (visible, avoidable).
Descending genicular artery branches.

Muscle: vastus medialis (VMO) muscle belly — identical territory to
SP10, 1 cun proximal.

#### Phân loại huyệt

Extra point (经外奇穴): EX-LE3. Not on any meridian.
**Alternative name:** 血郄 (Xuexi, "Blood Xi-Cleft") — recorded in
Zhenjiu Jicheng. An unofficial Xi-Cleft designation for Blood,
paralleling SP10's "Sea of Blood" (血海) name. Neither is a formal
category. The dual "Blood" naming (Sea + Xi-Cleft) in adjacent points
reflects the Spleen channel's dominance over blood-related functions.
→ Cross-ref: AP-04 SP10

#### Cross-tradition mapping

TCM acupoint: EX-LE3 Baichongwo. Functions: clears Blood Heat
(清血热), expels Wind (祛风), resolves Dampness (化湿). Anti-pruritic
specialization. ✓
Trigger point: vastus medialis TrP — same territory as SP10's
documented VMO TrP (Dorsher 2009). 🟡
Ashi: diagnostic palpation site — "most patients with eczema have
obvious tenderness at this point." 🟡

**4-8:** Marma ✗ (Urvi approximate but not specific). Head zone ✗
(purely somatic L2-L4). Anatomy Trains 🟡 (SFL-DFL interface, same
as SP10). Neurogenic ✗. Cupping ✗.

**Convergence score: 4/8** (TCM ✓, Trigger point 🟡, Ashi 🟡,
Anatomy Trains 🟡). Same territory and convergence as SP10 (4/8).

#### Chỉ định truyền thống

- Primary: skin disorders — eczema (湿疹), urticaria (荨麻疹/风疹),
  pruritus (瘙痒), chronic dermatitis
- Historical: intestinal parasites (蛔虫, ascariasis), parasitic skin
  conditions — reflecting the "hundred bugs" name and classical view
  that skin itching was caused by parasites beneath the skin
- TCM patterns: Blood Heat (血热), Wind-Damp (风湿) affecting the skin
- Often combined with SP10 (Xuehai) + SP6 (Sanyinjiao) + LI11
  (Quchi) for dermatological protocols

#### Bằng chứng hiện đại

No systematic review or RCT specific to EX-LE3. Baichongwo appears
in multi-point protocols within systematic reviews of acupuncture for
chronic spontaneous urticaria (PMC12164643, PMC12867926) but is not
isolated as a study variable.

#### An toàn

**Low risk.** Adequate muscle belly cushion (vastus medialis). Standard
clean needle technique. Great saphenous vein superficially — visible
and avoidable. No proximity to major vessels or nerves at standard
depth.

**Pregnancy:** NOT specifically contraindicated.

---

### EX-LE4 — Nội Tất Nhãn (内膝眼, Nèixīyǎn)

**Evidence tier: B (Standard Entry)**

"Medial Knee Eye" (内 = medial/inner + 膝 = knee + 眼 = eye) — the
medial infrapatellar depression, forming the MEDIAL component of the
Xiyan pair (膝眼, "Knee Eyes"). The lateral component is ST35 Dubi
(犊鼻) — a regular Stomach meridian point. Together they create the
most commonly used local knee combination in acupuncture. EX-LE4 is
an extra point (not on any meridian), positioned in the medial
infrapatellar fossa medial to the patellar ligament — the mirror image
of ST35's lateral position. The WHO designates EX-LE4 as the individual
medial point and EX-LE5 as the pair concept.

#### Lịch sử và danh pháp (History and Nomenclature)

The medial and lateral infrapatellar depressions were recognized early
in Chinese medical literature. The term "膝眼" (Knee Eyes) appears in
classical texts describing the two depressions flanking the patellar
ligament as resembling a pair of eyes when the knee is flexed.

**Zhenjiu Dacheng** (Yang Jizhou, 1601 CE): describes the knee eye
points including the medial depression. The lateral knee eye was
assigned to the Stomach channel as ST35 (Dubi, 犊鼻, "Calf's Nose"),
while the medial was classified as an extra point — reflecting the
channel trajectory: the Stomach channel descends along the lateral
side of the patellar ligament, not the medial.

**WHO-WPRO coding:** the naming convention varies across editions:
- EX-LE4 (Neixiyan 内膝眼) = the individual MEDIAL knee eye point
- EX-LE5 (Xiyan 膝眼) = the PAIR concept (medial + lateral together)
- Some older editions use EX-LE2 for the medial knee eye
- The pair concept is stable regardless of code numbering.

**Naming:** ST35 Dubi (犊鼻, "Calf's Nose") names the pair of
depressions after a calf's nostrils; EX-LE4 Neixiyan (内膝眼,
"Medial Knee Eye") uses the "eye" metaphor. Both describe the same
anatomical observation — two depressions flanking a central ligament.

#### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): on the knee, in the depression
medial to the patellar ligament, when the knee is flexed.

Anatomical landmarks: in the medial infrapatellar fossa. The patellar
ligament runs from the inferior pole of the patella to the tibial
tuberosity — EX-LE4 is in the soft tissue depression immediately
MEDIAL to this ligament. ST35 is in the corresponding LATERAL
depression.

Spatial relationships at the knee (documented in AP-03 ST35):
- **ST35 (Dubi):** lateral infrapatellar fossa — mirror counterpart
- **SP9 (Yinlingquan):** medial knee, pes anserinus (below joint line)
- **GB34 (Yanglingquan):** lateral knee, fibular head (below joint)
- **EX-LE2 (Heding):** midpoint of superior patellar border —
  suprapatellar counterpart to the infrapatellar Xiyan pair
- **LR8 (Ququan):** medial popliteal crease (posterior to EX-LE4)
→ Cross-ref: AP-03 ST35, AP-04 SP9, AP-11 GB34, AP-12 LR7/LR8

Needle depth: 15–25 mm oblique, directed toward the center of the
joint space or toward the opposite knee eye (ST35). Knee must be
FLEXED (~90°) to expose the infrapatellar fossa.

#### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- **Infrapatellar branch of saphenous nerve (L3-L4):** the primary
  cutaneous nerve of the anterior knee. This branch crosses the
  patellar ligament area, providing sensation to the infrapatellar
  skin. The MEDIAL side receives more direct saphenous innervation
  than the lateral (where common peroneal articular branches
  predominate).
- **Medial articular branches from tibial nerve and obturator nerve:**
  joint afferents to the medial knee compartment. The obturator nerve
  (L2-L4) provides articular branches to the medial knee — an
  additional segmental input not present at the lateral knee eye.
- Spinal segment: L3-L4 (cutaneous); L2-L4 (articular, via femoral
  + obturator nerves).
→ Cross-ref: A01 S1, AP-03 ST35

Vascular: inferior medial genicular artery (branch of popliteal
artery), patellar arterial rete.

Tissue layers:
- Skin → subcutaneous tissue → medial retinaculum
- **Infrapatellar fat pad (Hoffa's fat pad):** from the medial side,
  needle access enters the medial portion of the fat pad. Contains
  abundant type IVa nociceptors and sympathetic fibers (Dragoo 2012).
- Joint capsule / synovial membrane
- **Medial meniscus:** the anterior horn of the medial meniscus is
  deep to the medial infrapatellar fossa but protected by the fat
  pad at standard acupuncture depth.

**Neural distinction from ST35:** the medial knee eye receives more
saphenous nerve input (femoral terminal branch, L3-L4) and additional
obturator articular branches (L2-L4), while the lateral knee eye
receives common peroneal articular branches (L4-S1). The two knee
eyes access DIFFERENT neural territories of the same joint — providing
complementary segmental coverage when used as a pair.

#### Phân loại huyệt (Point Category Membership)

Extra point (经外奇穴): EX-LE4. Not on any meridian.
No formal category membership.

**Paired with ST35 Dubi** (Foot Yangming Stomach meridian) to form
the Xiyan pair (EX-LE5). The pairing is unique: one point is a regular
meridian point (ST35), the other is an extra point (EX-LE4). This
reflects the Stomach channel's lateral trajectory past the knee — the
medial depression falls outside any regular meridian's path.

#### Cross-tradition mapping

**1. TCM acupoint:** EX-LE4 Neixiyan. Functions: activates channels
and alleviates pain, dispels Wind-Damp, benefits the knee joint.
Primary: knee pain, knee OA, difficulty in flexion/extension. ✓

**2. Trigger point:** medial infrapatellar fat pad TrPs. Fat pad
impingement (Hoffa's disease) creates localized pain at the medial
infrapatellar fossa. Medial retinacular TrPs also in this area.
ST35 has the same correspondence laterally. 🟡
→ Cross-ref: B11

**3. Ashi correspondence:** tender in virtually ALL medial knee
pathologies — OA, medial meniscal tear, medial collateral ligament
strain, pes anserinus tendinopathy. Among the highest-frequency
Ashi points in the lower limb. 🟡

**4. Marma point:** Janu Marma (जानु, "knee") — Sandhi (joint) Marma.
The medial infrapatellar depression is within the Janu Marma zone, as
documented for ST35. Size 3 angula covers both infrapatellar fossae.
🟡
→ Cross-ref: AP-03 ST35

**5. Head zone:** L3-L4 cutaneous; L2-L4 articular. No visceral
correspondence — purely musculoskeletal. ✗

**6. Anatomy Trains:** SFL — the patellar ligament is a core SFL
structure. The medial infrapatellar fossa also borders the Deep Front
Line (DFL) via the medial capsular and ligamentous structures. 🟡

**7. Neurogenic inflammatory spot:** the infrapatellar fat pad
nociceptors sensitize in knee OA (Dragoo 2012), same mechanism as
ST35. LOCAL tissue sensitization rather than segmental neurogenic. 🟡

**8. Cupping:** not applicable at the infrapatellar fossa. ✗

**Convergence score: 5/8** (TCM ✓, Trigger point 🟡, Ashi 🟡,
Marma 🟡, Anatomy Trains 🟡). Neurogenic fat pad sensitization noted
but not counted (local pathology, same scoring as ST35). Consistent
with ST35's 5/8 — the two infrapatellar knee eyes share the same
cross-tradition profile.

#### Chỉ định truyền thống (DATA — ghi nhận, không endorse as mechanism)

Nguồn cổ: knee pain (膝痛), swelling (膝肿), difficulty in bending
and stretching (屈伸不利), knee Bi syndrome (膝痹). Same indications
as ST35 — both access the anterior knee joint.

Nguồn hiện đại: knee osteoarthritis (core local point), patellar
tendinopathy, fat pad syndrome, post-surgical rehabilitation.

Almost exclusively used as part of the **Xiyan pair** with ST35
(bilateral medial + lateral):
- **ST35 + EX-LE4 = Xiyan pair** — minimal knee protocol
- **Xiyan + ST36 + SP9 + GB34** — standard comprehensive knee protocol
→ Cross-ref: AP-03 ST35

**Medial-specific indications:** some practitioners preferentially
needle the medial knee eye (EX-LE4) for MEDIAL compartment pathology
(medial meniscal tear, medial OA, pes anserinus) and the lateral eye
(ST35) for LATERAL compartment pathology. This anatomically-guided
selection is not well studied but follows logical segmental reasoning
(saphenous/medial vs peroneal/lateral).

#### Bằng chứng hiện đại

🟡 Plausible. EX-LE4 is a CORE local point in knee OA protocols —
included in the majority of knee acupuncture trials. However, like
ST35, it is NEVER studied in isolation.

Data mining analyses (Cai 2022, PMC9429477) place EX-LE4 in the
**core acupoint cluster** for knee OA (alongside ST35, ST34, SP10,
GB34, SP9) — not in the secondary cluster where Heding falls.

The evidence for EX-LE4 is inseparable from the Xiyan pair evidence.
See EX-LE5 below for the full evidence synthesis.
→ Cross-ref: AP-03 ST35 (Bằng chứng hiện đại section)

#### Cơ chế giải thích

Identical to ST35 mechanism (AP-03 ST35, Cơ chế giải thích section):

A01/A02 (Neural): infrapatellar branch of saphenous nerve (L3-L4) →
segmental pain modulation. ADDITIONAL input from obturator nerve
articular branches (L2-L4) at the medial side — expanding the
segmental convergence zone beyond ST35's lateral access. Gate control
via Aβ/Aδ in fat pad and peritendinous tissue. 🟢/🟡.

A03 (Fascia): patellar retinaculum and fat pad — needle manipulation
may release fascial adhesions and decompress sensitized nerve endings.
🟡.

A07 (Segmental): L2-L4 segmental match. FULL CONVERGENCE — purely
local. No supraspinal pathway needed.

**MINIMAL mechanism gap.** Same as ST35 — the most mechanistically
transparent access zone in the Atlas.
→ Cross-ref: AP-03 ST35

#### Kỹ thuật kích thích

Châm (B01): 15-25 mm oblique, directed toward the center of the joint
space or toward ST35 (opposite knee eye). Knee must be FLEXED (~90°).
De Qi: distension and heaviness within the knee joint, warmth
spreading around the patella.

**Xiyan pair technique:** one needle in ST35 (lateral) and one in
EX-LE4 (medial), both directed toward the center. EA connecting the
two needles creates a current path through the infrapatellar fat pad
and anterior joint space — one of the most studied electrode
configurations in knee OA trials (ST35-to-EX-LE4 trans-articular).
→ Cross-ref: AP-03 ST35 (Kỹ thuật kích thích section)

EA: 2-15 Hz, low-medium intensity. Trans-articular (EX-LE4 to ST35)
or EX-LE4 to ST36 (longitudinal).

**Medial arthrocentesis parallel:** the medial infrapatellar approach
for EX-LE4 parallels the standard medial approach for diagnostic
arthrocentesis/joint aspiration in orthopedics — both access the knee
joint through the medial infrapatellar soft spot. The orthopedic
procedure uses a larger gauge and deeper insertion, but the access
path is anatomically identical.

#### Dynamic acupoint properties

Same dynamic behavior as ST35 (AP-03 ST35, Dynamic section):
- Fat pad nociceptor upregulation of substance P and CGRP in OA
  (Dragoo 2012) — local tissue sensitization
- Pressure pain sensitivity correlates with OA severity
- The medial side may sensitize differently from the lateral in
  medial-predominant OA (compartment-specific fat pad changes)
→ Cross-ref: C04 S4

#### An toàn

**Low-moderate risk.** Same considerations as ST35:

**Joint capsule penetration:** oblique needling directed into the
joint enters the synovial space through the fat pad — INTENTIONAL in
some protocols (intra-articular acupuncture) but requires strict
asepsis. Septic arthritis = surgical emergency.

**Medial meniscus:** the anterior horn is deep to the medial
infrapatellar fossa but protected by the fat pad at standard depth.
Not a practical risk at 15-25 mm oblique insertion.

**Medial inferior genicular artery:** small, not a significant
vascular risk.

**Infrapatellar branch of saphenous nerve:** may be contacted during
needling, causing sharp pain. Redirect if this occurs.

**Pregnancy:** NOT specifically contraindicated.

---

### EX-LE5 — Tất Nhãn (膝眼, Xīyǎn)

**Evidence tier: A (Deep Analysis)**

"Knee Eyes" (膝 = knee + 眼 = eyes) — the PAIR DESIGNATION for the
medial (EX-LE4 Neixiyan) and lateral (ST35 Dubi) infrapatellar
depressions used together. WHO recognizes EX-LE5 as this pair concept,
distinct from EX-LE4 (individual medial point). The Xiyan pair is the
most commonly used local point combination for knee osteoarthritis and
appears in virtually every documented knee acupuncture protocol across
the Atlas.

**Knee OA = acupuncture's strongest overall evidence base.** This
indication has more RCTs, more systematic reviews, more meta-analyses,
and more individual patient data than any other acupuncture application.
The Xiyan pair sits at the center of this evidence — as the core local
point combination in the majority of these trials. However, this
evidence strength comes with a defining paradox: the sham comparison
results for knee acupuncture are among the most debated in the field.

#### Lịch sử và danh pháp (History and Nomenclature)

The two infrapatellar depressions flanking the patellar ligament have
been recognized as clinically significant sites throughout Chinese
medical history. The LATERAL depression was assigned to the Stomach
channel as ST35 (Dubi 犊鼻, "Calf's Nose") — named for the paired
depressions resembling a calf's nostrils (Ling Shu Ch. 2 describes
Dubi as where "the Qi of the Stomach channel enters the knee"). The
MEDIAL depression, falling outside any regular meridian trajectory,
was classified as an extra point.

**Textual history of the pair concept:**
- **Huangdi Neijing — Ling Shu** (黄帝内经灵枢, ~200 BCE-200 CE):
  references the lateral knee depression (Dubi 犊鼻) as a Stomach
  channel point. Warning: "Needling Dubi causes the knee to be unable
  to extend" — a caution against improper technique.
- **Beiji Qianjin Yaofang** (备急千金要方, Sun Simiao, 652 CE): **the
  EARLIEST source using the term 膝眼 (Knee Eyes).** Describes the
  location as "below the kneecap, in the depressions on both sides,
  in the hollow" — confirming the pair concept from its first textual
  appearance. Original indication: beriberi (脚气) with knee symptoms.
- **Waitai Miyao** (外台秘要, Wang Tao, 752 CE): records Xiyan
  moxibustion for beriberi: "7 cones at the depression lateral to the
  sinew, below the kneecap bone."
- **Yu Long Ge** (玉龙歌, "Jade Dragon Song," 1329): prescribes "LR7 +
  Xiyan" for knee redness, swelling, and pain — early documentation of
  the pair as a named clinical entity.
- **Zhenjiu Dacheng** (Yang Jizhou, 1601): expands clinical description,
  notes moxibustion protocol. Distinguishes treatment: "if ulcerated
  cannot be treated; if not ulcerated can be treated."
- **Leijing Tuyi** (Zhang Jiebin, Ming dynasty): specifies 5 fen needle
  depth, prohibits moxibustion — contradicts other sources that
  recommend moxa (unresolved classical disagreement).
- **WHO-WPRO** (1991, 2008): formalizes the distinction between EX-LE4
  (individual medial point) and EX-LE5 (pair concept).

**Architectural note:** the Xiyan pair is the only combination in the
WHO extra point system where the pair itself receives a separate code
(EX-LE5) from its components. This reflects the pair's clinical
primacy — it is almost never used as individual points but always as
a bilateral unit.

#### Vị trí giải phẫu

The Xiyan pair accesses the knee joint from two complementary
directions:

| Component | Location | Nerve access | Fat pad portion |
|-----------|----------|-------------|-----------------|
| **ST35 (lateral)** | Lateral infrapatellar fossa | Common peroneal articular (L4-S1) + saphenous (L3-L4) | Lateral fat pad |
| **EX-LE4 (medial)** | Medial infrapatellar fossa | Saphenous (L3-L4) + obturator articular (L2-L4) | Medial fat pad |
| **Combined** | Bilateral anterior joint access | L2-S1 spanning 5 segments | Complete fat pad |

The pair provides COMPREHENSIVE anterior knee access: medial + lateral
fat pad, medial + lateral retinaculum, bilateral articular nerves
spanning L2 to S1. No other 2-point combination covers as many
segmental levels at a single joint.

Needle depth: 15-25 mm oblique for each component, directed toward
the center of the joint space. The trans-articular path between the
two needles traverses the infrapatellar fat pad and anterior synovial
space.

For detailed anatomy of each component, see:
→ Cross-ref: AP-03 ST35 (Vị trí giải phẫu), EX-LE4 above

#### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

**Infrapatellar fat pad (Hoffa's fat pad):** the key structure accessed
by both components of the Xiyan pair. The fat pad is a highly
innervated, vascularized adipose tissue within the knee joint space,
occupying the space between the patellar ligament anteriorly and the
femoral condyles posteriorly.

Neural significance of the fat pad:
- **Type IVa nerve fibers (nociceptors):** abundant — the fat pad is
  one of the most richly innervated structures in the knee.
- **Sympathetic fibers:** present, contributing to vasomotor control.
- **Substance P and CGRP fibers:** upregulated in OA (Dragoo 2012) —
  the fat pad becomes a pain generator in diseased joints.
- **Mechanoreceptors:** present, contributing to proprioception.
→ Cross-ref: A01 S1, A02 S3

The combined neural input from both knee eyes:
- Saphenous nerve terminal branches (L3-L4) — bilateral cutaneous
- Common peroneal articular branches (L4-S1) — lateral joint
- Obturator articular branches (L2-L4) — medial joint
- Tibial articular branches — bilateral deep joint
- Total segmental coverage: L2 through S1 (5 spinal segments) from
  just 2 needle insertions.

#### Phân loại huyệt (Point Category Membership)

**PAIR DESIGNATION** — unique in the WHO extra point system. EX-LE5
is not a single anatomical point but a clinical combination concept
given its own code due to its paramount importance in knee treatment.

No formal TCM category membership (no Five Shu, Yuan-Source, etc.).
Neither component has any classical category designation. Like SP10
(AP-04) and LI15 (AP-02), the Xiyan pair derives its clinical
importance from anatomical positioning and treatment frequency rather
than from theoretical classification.

**The most cross-referenced extra point combination in the Atlas:**
documented in AP-03 ST35 (L6714, 6843, 6937, 7037-7060), AP-04 SP
(L2179), AP-08 KI10 (L3161), AP-11 GB (L7168), AP-12 LR7 (L858,
3364) — spanning 6+ separate Atlas files.

#### Cross-tradition mapping

**1. TCM acupoint:** EX-LE5 Xiyan (膝眼). The pair concept — bilateral
infrapatellar access for all knee conditions. Functions: dispels
Wind-Damp (祛风湿), activates channels (通络), alleviates knee pain
(止膝痛). The MOST FREQUENTLY SELECTED local knee combination across
all documented TCM knee protocols in the Atlas. ✓

**2. Trigger point:** infrapatellar fat pad TrPs and patellar tendon
enthesopathic points bilaterally. Fat pad impingement (Hoffa's disease)
creates pain at exactly the Xiyan locations. Quadriceps insertion TrPs
refer to both fossae. ✓/🟡
→ Cross-ref: B11

**3. Ashi correspondence:** tender in virtually ALL anterior knee
pathologies — OA, patellar tendinopathy, fat pad impingement, meniscal
tear, chondromalacia patellae. The highest-frequency Ashi pair in the
lower limb. 🟡

**4. Marma point:** Janu Marma (जानु, "knee") — Sandhi (joint) Marma.
Size 3 angula covers both infrapatellar fossae. The Xiyan pair matches
the bilateral extent of Janu Marma more closely than either individual
component alone. Injury causes lameness (pangulya). 🟡
→ Cross-ref: AP-03 ST35, AP-04 SP9

**5. Head zone:** L2-S1 articular (combined). No visceral
correspondence. ✗

**6. Anatomy Trains:** SFL — the patellar ligament between the two
knee eyes is a core SFL structure, connecting the quadriceps through
the patella to the tibial tuberosity. The Xiyan pair flanks the SFL
at its most prominent relay point. 🟡

**7. Neurogenic inflammatory spot:** fat pad nociceptor sensitization
in OA (Dragoo 2012) — documented at both medial and lateral fat pad.
LOCAL tissue sensitization correlated with disease severity. 🟡

**8. Cupping:** not applicable at infrapatellar fossae. ✗

**Convergence score: 5/8** (TCM ✓, Trigger point 🟡, Ashi 🟡,
Marma 🟡, Anatomy Trains 🟡). Neurogenic fat pad sensitization noted
(🟡) but not counted toward score (local pathology, consistent with
ST35 scoring convention). Same convergence as each component
individually — the pair concept strengthens existing correspondences
without adding new traditions.

#### Chỉ định truyền thống (DATA — ghi nhận, không endorse as mechanism)

Nguồn cổ:
- **Ling Shu** Ch. 2: Dubi (ST35) as gateway for Yangming Qi to the
  knee joint.
- **Yu Long Ge** (1329): LR7 + Xiyan for knee redness, swelling, pain.
- **Zhenjiu Dacheng** (1601): paired knee eye needling for all knee
  conditions.

Nguồn hiện đại — the Xiyan pair is the CORE of knee protocols across
every Atlas channel:
- **ST35 + EX-LE4** = minimal local protocol for knee OA
- **Xiyan + ST36 + SP9 + GB34** = standard comprehensive protocol
- **Xiyan + Heding + SP10 + ST34** = extended local coverage
- **Xiyan + distal points (GB34, SP6, ST36)** = local + distal protocol
→ Cross-ref: AP-03 ST34-36, AP-04 SP9/SP10, AP-11 GB34, AP-12 LR7/LR8

#### Bằng chứng hiện đại

**Knee OA = acupuncture's STRONGEST evidence base.** The Xiyan pair
is at the center of this evidence. The data presents a clear pattern
with a defining paradox:

**Cochrane Review — Manheimer 2010 (CD001977, PMID 20091527):**
16 RCTs, 3,498 participants (12 knee OA trials).

| Comparison | Outcome | SMD (95% CI) | Clinical significance |
|-----------|---------|-------------|----------------------|
| vs Sham, short-term | Pain | −0.28 (−0.45 to −0.11) | Below threshold (≥0.39) |
| vs Sham, short-term | Function | −0.28 (−0.46 to −0.09) | Below threshold (≥0.37) |
| vs Sham, long-term | Pain | −0.10 (−0.21 to 0.01) | NOT significant |
| vs Sham, long-term | Function | −0.11 (−0.22 to 0.00) | Borderline |
| vs Waiting list | Pain | −0.96 (−1.19 to −0.72) | LARGE, clinically meaningful |
| vs Waiting list | Function | −0.89 (−1.18 to −0.60) | LARGE, clinically meaningful |

GRADE quality: Low (short-term vs sham), High (long-term null findings).
Not formally updated with new trials since 2010.

**Liu 2024 — LARGEST and most recent SR/MA (BMJ Evidence-Based Medicine,
PMID 39486882):** 80 RCTs, 9,933 participants.

| Comparison | Pain SMD (95% CI) | Function SMD (95% CI) |
|-----------|-------------------|----------------------|
| vs Sham | −0.74 (−1.08 to −0.39) | −0.77 (−1.21 to −0.34) |
| vs NSAIDs | −0.86 (−1.26 to −0.46) | −1.06 (−1.52 to −0.60) |
| vs Usual care/Wait | −1.01 (−1.47 to −0.54) | −0.61 (−1.01 to −0.21) |

EA vs MA (NMA): EA significantly superior (SMD −0.75, CI −1.34 to
−0.17). EA vs sham significant (−1.22), MA vs sham NOT significant
(−0.46). Dose-response: high dose SMD −2.30, low dose NS (p<0.001
interaction). GRADE: very low certainty, 85% high risk of bias.
Benefits NOT sustained at 26-52 weeks.

**KEY DISCREPANCY:** Cochrane 2010 (SMD −0.28) vs Liu 2024 (SMD −0.74)
vs sham. Likely explanations: Liu 2024 includes more Chinese-language
RCTs with higher effect sizes, broader sham definitions, and different
quality assessment. Neither invalidates the other — they represent
different slices of the evidence landscape. The 2024 SR notes 85%
high risk of bias, suggesting the higher SMD may be inflated.

**Vickers Individual Patient Data Meta-Analysis:**
- Vickers 2012 (Arch Intern Med, PMID 22965186): 29 RCTs, 17,922
  patients. OA vs sham: SMD 0.16 (CI 0.07-0.25); OA vs no-acu:
  SMD 0.57 (CI 0.50-0.64).
- Vickers 2018 (J Pain, PMID 29198932): 39 RCTs, 20,827 patients.
  Effect persisted at 12-month follow-up with only ~15% decrease.
  ~60% of total effect = non-specific (sham captures it), ~40% =
  specific acupuncture advantage over sham.
  Conclusion: "Acupuncture is effective for the treatment of chronic
  pain, with treatment effects persisting over time."

**Key individual RCTs:**
- **Berman 2004** (Ann Intern Med, PMID 15611489): n=570. WOMAC
  function MD −2.9 (CI −5.0 to −0.8, p=0.01) at 8 weeks, MD −2.5
  at 26 weeks. WOMAC pain MD −0.87 at 26 weeks (p=0.003). Protocol:
  ST35/Xiyan pair as core local points + ST36, SP9, GB34. The trial
  that established acupuncture as a serious knee OA intervention.
- **Scharf 2006** (Ann Intern Med, GERAC/ART, PMID 16818924): n=1,007.
  Success rates: verum 53.1%, sham 51.0%, conservative 29.1%.
  Verum ≈ sham, both >> conservative. Led to German insurance
  reimbursement — the total treatment package was judged clinically
  meaningful regardless of point specificity.
- **Hinman 2014** (JAMA, PMID 25268438): n=282. Needle vs sham:
  pain −0.4 (CI −1.2 to 0.4, NS). A NEGATIVE trial. Criticized
  for not requiring radiographic OA confirmation.

**The sham paradox at the knee:**

This evidence pattern is the clearest demonstration of the 3-layer
sham resolution established in C02 and A09:

```
Layer 1: Acupuncture > no treatment       → YES (SMD −0.96)
  Acupuncture WORKS for knee OA pain.

Layer 2: Verum ≈ sham at same location    → YES (SMD −0.28, below
  clinical threshold). Location precision is MODEST.

Layer 3: Interpretation
  Effects are REAL but arise from MULTIPLE simultaneous mechanisms:
  - Location-DEPENDENT: fat pad nociceptor modulation, segmental
    L3-L4 gate control, fascial release (explain the ~0.28 SMD
    advantage of verum over sham)
  - Location-INDEPENDENT: non-specific needle-induced analgesia,
    expectation/placebo effects, therapeutic ritual, attention
    (explain why sham also works)
```
→ Cross-ref: C02 S3.4, A09 S3.1

**WHY sham works well at the knee:** the infrapatellar fat pad is a
large, richly innervated structure. Sham needles placed at "non-
acupuncture" points near the knee STILL access fat pad tissue, STILL
activate L3-L4 segmental mechanisms, STILL produce gate control
effects. The knee is anatomically DIFFICULT to sham adequately
because the therapeutic target (fat pad, periarticular tissue) is
broadly distributed, not confined to a precise point.

**Overall assessment:** 🟡 Plausible. The evidence confirms that
acupuncture at the knee produces REAL pain relief and functional
improvement beyond no treatment. The location-specific contribution
of the Xiyan pair is modest but significant. The evidence
simultaneously demonstrates acupuncture's THERAPEUTIC VALIDITY and
LOCATION SPECIFICITY LIMITATIONS.

#### Cơ chế giải thích

**The most mechanistically TRANSPARENT acupoint combination in the
Atlas.**

All Xiyan indications are LOCAL — knee pain, knee stiffness, knee
swelling. There are NO distant organ effects to explain. The
segmental match is COMPLETE: L2-S1 (combined from both components)
fully covers the knee joint's entire innervation territory.

**Track A systems:**

A01/A02 (Neural): bilateral fat pad nociceptor activation → L3-L4
dorsal horn → segmental gate control. Aβ/Aδ fiber input from the
dense periarticular tissue closes the gate to C-fiber nociceptive
transmission from the inflamed joint. Additional obturator (medial)
and common peroneal (lateral) articular branches provide 5-segment
coverage (L2-S1). 🟢.

A03 (Fascia): bilateral patellar retinaculum and fat pad — fascial
adhesion release, decompression of sensitized mechanoreceptors. 🟡.

A05 (Immune/Inflammatory): local anti-inflammatory effects via
MULTIPLE pathways:
- Axon reflex → CGRP/substance P modulation → blood flow increase →
  inflammatory mediator clearance from joint space.
- Sympathetic-noradrenergic pathway (Chen 2023, PMID 36959872): EA
  increases local norepinephrine in knee synovium → β2-AR activation
  on synovial macrophages → M1-to-M2 polarization shift → suppresses
  IL-1β, IL-6, TNF-α. 10 Hz optimal. 🟡.
- NF-κB pathway suppression → TLR4/MyD88 inhibition → reduced
  NLRP3 inflammasome activation → decreased IL-1β/IL-18 maturation.
- Adenosine release (Goldman 2010, Nature Neuroscience, PMID 20512135):
  needle manipulation at knee-level acupoints → 24-FOLD increase in
  local adenosine concentration → adenosine A1 receptor activation →
  anti-nociception. A1 receptor knockout mice showed NO analgesic
  effect from acupuncture. Confirmed in humans (Takano 2012,
  PMID 23182227). 🟢/🟡.

EA-specific: trans-articular EA (EX-LE4 to ST35) creates a current
path THROUGH the fat pad → direct electrical stimulation of fat pad
nociceptors → enhanced gate control + anti-inflammatory effects.
2 Hz EA → encephalin release (supraspinal). 🟡.

**ZERO mechanism gap.** The Xiyan pair represents the OPPOSITE EXTREME
from BL67 (strongest evidence, no mechanism) or Sifeng (robust
evidence, unknown mechanism). At the knee, BOTH evidence and mechanism
are well-characterized. The remaining question is not "does it work?"
or "how does it work?" but "how much of the effect is point-specific
vs non-specific?" — and this is an epistemological question, not a
mechanistic one.

**Mechanism extremes at ST35-ST36 junction:** two adjacent point
combinations bracket the full range of acupuncture mechanism:
- **Xiyan pair (ST35 + EX-LE4):** local-segmental, zero gap, purely
  musculoskeletal, gate control dominates. 🟢
- **ST36 (Zusanli, 3 cun below):** distant-supraspinal, requires
  PROKR2 → vagal-adrenal → NTS/DMVN brainstem relay for gastric
  effects. Complex pathway. 🟡

Three centimeters of distance separates acupuncture's simplest and
most complex mechanisms.
→ Cross-ref: AP-03 ST35/ST36, A09 S2.2

#### Kỹ thuật kích thích

**Xiyan pair technique (primary method):**
Both needles inserted simultaneously with knee flexed ~90°:
- ST35: lateral infrapatellar fossa, 15-25 mm oblique toward center
- EX-LE4: medial infrapatellar fossa, 15-25 mm oblique toward center
- Both directed toward each other — converging on the central fat pad
→ Cross-ref: AP-03 ST35 (Kỹ thuật kích thích)

**EA configuration:** trans-articular connection (EX-LE4 to ST35)
creating current path through the fat pad. 2-15 Hz, low-medium
intensity, 20-30 minutes. This electrode configuration is one of the
most studied in knee OA trials. Alternative: EX-LE4-to-ST36
(longitudinal).
→ Cross-ref: B01 S4.3

**Moxibustion:** indirect moxa over both knee eyes for cold-pattern
knee Bi (寒痹). Classical sources specifically recommend moxa at both
fossae for cold-predominant knee conditions.

**Acupressure:** firm pressure in both infrapatellar fossae with the
knee flexed. Self-applicable technique for daily knee pain management.
→ Cross-ref: B02

#### Dynamic acupoint properties

The Xiyan pair demonstrates clear disease-state-dependent dynamic
behavior:

**Fat pad sensitization in OA:** type IVa nociceptors in the
infrapatellar fat pad upregulate substance P and CGRP in OA joints
(Dragoo 2012). The fat pad becomes a PAIN GENERATOR — increasing
the point's tenderness and potentially its therapeutic responsiveness.
This represents LOCAL tissue sensitization: the needle target itself
changes with disease state.

**Pressure pain threshold data:** Chen 2022 (PMID 35676697, n=666)
confirmed reduced PPT at knee acupoints (including nearby Heding) in
KOA patients, with PPT increasing after treatment — measurable
reversal of disease-related sensitization.

Whether the Xiyan pair meets formal C04 dynamic acupoint criteria is
debatable: OA CREATES local pathology at the needle site, blurring
the line between "dynamic acupoint sensitization" (organ disease →
distant point becomes tender) and "local pathological tissue"
(disease AT the needle site). The Xiyan pair occupies a boundary zone
in the dynamic acupoint classification — the disease IS at the
point, not projected to it from a distant organ.
→ Cross-ref: C04 S4

#### An toàn

**Low-moderate risk.** The primary concern is BILATERAL joint space
entry:

**Joint capsule penetration:** oblique needling through both
infrapatellar fossae accesses the synovial space through the fat pad.
This is INTENTIONAL in many protocols (intra-articular acupuncture)
but doubles the infection risk surface compared to a single needle.
Septic arthritis = surgical emergency.

**Infection prevention:** the PRIMARY safety concern. Two needles
entering the joint space from opposite sides means two potential
portals for bacterial introduction. Strict aseptic technique at BOTH
insertion sites is essential.

**Adverse events from SR data (Cochrane 2010):**
- Only 8 of 16 RCTs reported adverse events
- Frequency in acupuncture groups: 0% to 7%
- No serious adverse events reported across all included trials
- Minor: bruising/bleeding at needle sites (0% to 45%)
- The Xiyan pair's safety profile is one of the best-documented in
  acupuncture research due to knee OA's large trial volume.

**Infrapatellar branch of saphenous nerve:** crosses the anterior
knee and may be contacted during medial-side needling. Redirect if
sharp electric pain occurs.

**Genicular arteries:** small, not a significant vascular risk.

**Pregnancy:** NOT specifically contraindicated.

---

*Phase 2 Batch 9 COMPLETE. 5 entries added: EX-LE1 (Kuangu, Tier C,
3/8 convergence — "Hip Bone" actually at distal thigh flanking ST34,
paired point 4 total, Zhenjiu Dacheng 1601, name misleadingly
references ST channel hip-to-knee trajectory), EX-LE2 (Heding, Tier B,
5/8 convergence — "Crane's Summit" at superior patellar border, Waike
Dacheng 1665 "secret method" for Crane's Knee Wind, suprapatellar pouch
access, secondary/adjunctive in knee OA protocols, PPT sensitization
data Chen 2022 PMID 35676697 n=666), EX-LE3 (Baichongwo, Tier C, 4/8
convergence — "Hundred Bug Nest" 1 cun above SP10, anti-pruritic
specialization, formication metaphor, alternative name Xuexi "Blood
Xi-Cleft," same VMO territory as SP10), EX-LE4 (Neixiyan, Tier B, 5/8
convergence — "Medial Knee Eye" forming the medial half of the Xiyan
pair with ST35, saphenous + obturator neural access, medial fat pad,
standard medial arthrocentesis parallel), EX-LE5 (Xiyan, Tier A, 5/8
convergence — "Knee Eyes" PAIR CONCEPT for medial + lateral knee eyes
together, unique WHO pair designation EX-LE5, acupuncture's STRONGEST
evidence base, Cochrane 2010 16 RCTs n=3,498, Vickers 2018 39 RCTs
NNT≈4, sham paradox most clearly demonstrated at the knee: vs sham
SMD −0.28 below clinical threshold vs waitlist SMD −0.96 large effect,
ZERO mechanism gap with purely local segmental L2-S1 match, mechanism
extreme contrast with ST36 3 cm distal). **EX-LE 5/12 entries.** Total
entries in AP-15: 40/48 (including Phase 1 Yintang). No new ⚡
framework notes (running total: 41). Key findings: (1) the Kuangu
location correction — "Hip Bone" is NOT at the hip but at the distal
thigh, the name references the ST channel trajectory; (2) the Heding
sensitization data (Chen 2022) confirms dynamic acupoint behavior at
the suprapatellar level; (3) the Xiyan pair's evidence base presents
the sham paradox at its clearest — real therapeutic effect (vs no
treatment SMD −0.96) with modest location specificity (vs sham
SMD −0.28), explained by the fat pad's broad distribution making
adequate sham anatomically difficult; (4) the ST35-ST36 mechanism
extreme — 3 cm separates acupuncture's simplest mechanism (local
segmental gate control, zero gap) from its most complex (PROKR2 →
vagal-adrenal supraspinal pathway); (5) the Xiyan pair represents
a BOUNDARY CASE for dynamic acupoint classification — the disease
(OA) IS at the needle site, not projected from a distant organ.*
